## [2026-09-03] `api/gatewayevents/v1` decision enrichment — rate-limit fail-open, fallback-routing detail, budget-spend-at-decision-time

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (3 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design: proto evolution/field-shape conventions (buf's real breaking-change rule taxonomy, protobuf.dev's `optional` presence guidance, real production schemas from OpenTelemetry/LiteLLM/Kong), Go patterns for threading decision-context without breaking existing signatures (Go team compatibility guidance, a real Kubernetes admission-webhook precedent, current context.Value anti-pattern guidance), and a full line-numbered read of the real current `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go`/`budget.go`; independently re-verified every line number and code shape the synthesis cited directly against the live tree before trusting any of it; created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-03-gatewayevents-decision-enrichment.md` (spec) and `docs/plans/2026-09-03-gatewayevents-decision-enrichment.md` (4-task plan); modified `api/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents.proto` (5 new fields, 7–11) and regenerated `gateway/api/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents.pb.go` + `evals/evals/contracts/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents_pb2.py`; modified `gateway/internal/budget/budget.go` (+`SpentUSD`) and `budget_test.go`; modified `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (widened `checkRateLimit`'s return, new `fallbackInfo` type, widened the inline fallback block/var block/`finalize` signature/event construction) and `streaming.go` (mirrored widening, `streamDeploymentWithFallback`'s return grows by one value); modified `gatewayevents_test.go` (8 new tests, including a fake `RedisBackend` fixture); updated `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Picked as the next ideal step per the founder's repeated "go ahead with next ideal steps... using dynamic workflows" instruction, once the first release closed. Of the open candidates at the time, this was the only one genuinely ready to build now without either overriding an already-settled scope decision (the skeptic-panel is explicitly `v2`-scoped in `PRD.md`) or being gated on production telemetry that doesn't exist yet for a project with zero live traffic (the deferred L2 normalizations and a real embedding-based L3 both explicitly say "revisit once real data justifies it" — checked directly against each RFC's own wording, not assumed from a paraphrase in `STATUS.md`, which is what first suggested the L2 work looked ready before the primary source corrected that). The original `api/gatewayevents/v1` contract RFC's own "Alternatives Considered" had named these exact 3 fields and given the exact reason they weren't built then ("needs real code restructuring, not just add one emission call") — this pass does that restructuring.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — 5 new proto fields (7–11), confirmed non-breaking both by argument (buf's `FILE` rules only target deletion/enforced-sameness) and empirically (`buf breaking` run for real, 0 violations); plain `bool` over `optional bool` for `rate_limit_fail_open`, a checked departure from protobuf.dev's general recommendation specific to this repo's `protojson.Marshal` call having no `EmitUnpopulated`; a flat `bool`+2 `string`s for fallback detail rather than a repeated/nested shape, grounded in the real code fact that Kelvran's fallback logic structurally attempts at most one fallback; widening `checkRateLimit`'s return in place rather than adding a sibling function (the general Go-ecosystem recommendation for API compatibility), justified because it has exactly two call sites, both edited by this same change, and an existing in-repo precedent (`nextDeployment`'s `(Deployment, bool)` shape) already matches this exact pattern; `budgetSpentAtDecision` captured at the `budget.Allow` call site, unconditionally, never inside `finalize`.

**Verification performed:** Every fact the synthesis relied on was independently re-verified against the live tree before being written into the RFC — the proto file's real 6 existing fields, `buf.yaml`'s real `breaking: use: [FILE]` config, and the exact line numbers/bodies of `checkRateLimit`, `HandleChatCompletion`'s fallback block, `streamDeploymentWithFallback`, `finalize`, and `budget.Tracker`'s real struct shape, all read directly, not trusted from the research report. `buf lint` and `buf breaking --against '../.git#branch=main,subdir=api'` both run for real after the proto edit — clean, 0 violations. Ran real build/vet/test/lint/race after every task, plus a full-module `make verify` pass at the end — all packages `ok`, `0 issues`, race-clean, `evals` unaffected (45 passed, 4 skipped). 8 new tests, including the load-bearing `TestGatewayEventBudgetSpentUsdReflectsRealPriorSpend` (two real requests through the full pipeline, second one rejected, asserting the logged field equals the first request's real recorded cost — not a placeholder) and `TestGatewayEventStreamingFallbackFalseAfterFirstChunkSent` (proving "errored" and "fell back" are never conflated). Independently sanity-checked the fallback-detail capture-ordering rule by temporarily reintroducing the exact naive bug the RFC's own "what a naive implementation would get wrong" section named (capturing `dep.Name`/`err.Error()` AFTER the retry, not before) and confirmed the test suite caught it — decisively: not a wrong-value failure, but a real nil-pointer panic (`err.Error()` on a `nil` `err`, once the fallback succeeded), then restored and re-verified green.

**Bugs found:** No production bugs from the research itself — this was new-feature implementation, with one real test-math mistake caught and fixed during writing, not shipping: `TestGatewayEventBudgetSpentUsdReflectsRealPriorSpend`'s first-draft budget cap (`0.001`) was set above the first request's real recorded cost (`0.0008`), so the second request was never actually rejected — the test failed for the right, obvious reason (`budget.Allow` compares spend-so-far against the cap, not spend-plus-this-request) and was fixed by lowering the cap below the first request's cost, not by weakening the assertion.

**Next steps / resume point:** `api/gatewayevents/v1` decision enrichment is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. Remaining roadmap, none decided yet: PyPI publish of `kelvran-evals` (blocked on trademark clearance), the evals skeptic-panel (explicitly `v2`-scoped, not a v1 candidate), the deferred L2 whitespace-collapse/case-folding normalizations and a real embedding-based semantic L3 (both gated on real production telemetry that doesn't exist yet), and `api/otel/`'s still-open future.

## [2026-09-03] Guardrails v1 — spec → plan → implementation, a real doc-vs-code security gap closed

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (5 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design: pure-Go PII/content-classification feasibility (Microsoft Presidio's and AWS Comprehend's own per-entity detection-method docs, gitleaks'/trufflehog's real regex rule sets, a realistic check of the only two candidate pure-Go NER libraries), fail-open/fail-closed policy precedent (OWASP's "Fail Securely" guidance, AWS Bedrock/Azure Content Safety/OpenAI Moderation's own category-severity defaults, LiteLLM/NeMo/Portkey/Azure OpenAI's real classifier-error handling), pipeline integration including the streaming tension (OpenAI's own streaming-moderation docs, LiteLLM/Portkey/NeMo's real streaming-guardrail implementations), a full line-numbered read of the current `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go`/`controlplane/config.go`, and a scope-readiness check against `PRD.md`/`THREAT_MODEL.md`/`DECISIONS.md`; independently re-verified every load-bearing claim directly against the live repo before trusting it, including re-reading `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go`'s real, post-edit line numbers (the prior `gatewayevents-decision-enrichment` pass had just changed both files, making the research's own citations stale); created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-03-guardrails-pii-regex-classifier.md` (spec) and `docs/plans/2026-09-03-guardrails-pii-regex-classifier.md` (6-task plan); created `gateway/internal/guardrail/` (new package: `types.go`, `policy.go`, `engine.go`, 8 detector files + matching tests — `email`, `phone`, `ssn`, `iban`, `creditcard`, `ipaddress`, `secretkey`, `promptinjection`); modified `gateway/internal/cache/key.go` (+`guardrailPolicyVersion` param) and `key_test.go`; modified `gateway/internal/cache/lexical.go` (+`GuardrailPolicyVersion` field/param) and `gateway/internal/cache/inprocess/lexical.go` + their tests; modified `api/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents.proto` (+`OUTCOME_GUARDRAIL_BLOCKED`) and regenerated both languages' bindings; modified `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/{dataplane.go,streaming.go}` (new `ErrGuardrailBlocked` sentinel, `Config.Guardrails` hard-required dependency, pre-call/post-call wiring on both paths, `outcomeFor` classification) + every existing test helper's `Config{}` literal + new `guardrail_test.go`; modified `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (+`GuardrailsConfig`) + `config_test.go`; modified `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`newGuardrailEngine`, category-override application, `writeErrorResponse`'s new `ErrGuardrailBlocked` → 400 case) + `integration_test.go`; updated `gateway/config.example.yaml`, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (corrected the "PII/NER" overclaim, added `guardrail` to the dependency-rules block, added the streaming-audit-only disambiguation to the Request Lifecycle prose), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Picked as the next roadmap item per the founder's repeated "go ahead with next ideal steps... using dynamic workflows" instruction, once the `gatewayevents` enrichment closed and every other previously-identified candidate turned out to be either explicitly `v2`-scoped (the skeptic panel) or gated on real production telemetry that doesn't exist yet (the deferred L2 normalizations, a real embedding-based L3). A fresh survey of the project's own docs surfaced Guardrails as a genuinely ready, previously-overlooked candidate: `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` already commits to a v1 design in prose, and `THREAT_MODEL.md`/`SECURITY.md` already assert a mitigation that had zero code behind it — a live documentation-vs-code integrity gap, not a hypothetical one.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — pure-Go regex/checksum PII+secrets detection plus a prompt-injection heuristic, deliberately not NER/ML moderation (no mature Go NER library exists, and unlike Cache L3-lite's MinHash there's no equivalent substitute — NER is cleanly out of scope, not replaced); category-tiered fail-open/fail-closed policy on both the detection and detector-error axes, never inherited from the rate limiter's own default; pre-call identical on buffered/streaming, post-call enforcement-capable on buffered but audit-only on streaming (a named, accepted residual risk); the cache-hit-safety mechanism split by real cache-layer capability (L1/L2: bake the policy version into the key hash, since no metadata envelope exists there; L3: an explicit, checked field, mirroring the existing `ModelID` mechanism) — a real correction to the grounding research's own uniform proposal, made after checking `cache.Cache`'s actual interface rather than trusting it; a new `OUTCOME_GUARDRAIL_BLOCKED` enum value and an explicit HTTP-400 status-code case, both gaps caught independently by cross-referencing established conventions the research itself didn't name.

**Verification performed:** Ran real build/vet/lint/test after every task, plus a full-module `go test -race`/`golangci-lint` pass at the end — all packages `ok`, `0 issues`, race-clean, `evals` unaffected. `buf lint`/`buf breaking --against '../.git#branch=main,subdir=api'` both run for real after the proto edit — clean, 0 violations, confirming the additive-only claim empirically. Two deliberate sanity checks, not just written-and-trusted: (1) temporarily disabled the post-call guardrail check and confirmed `TestHandleChatCompletionPostCallBlocksBlockTierResponse`/`TestHandleChatCompletionPostCallBlockedResponseNeverCached` both failed for the right, specific reason, then restored and re-verified green; (2) confirmed the streaming audit-only test (`TestHandleChatCompletionStreamPostCallBlockTierIsAuditOnlyNeverWithheld`) actually asserts full delivery of Block-tier content, proving the decisive "audit-only, never withheld" design choice in code, not just in the RFC's prose. A real, previously-undetected HTTP-status-mapping gap was caught while writing the end-to-end integration test: `ErrGuardrailBlocked` with no explicit case in `writeErrorResponse` would have defaulted to `502 Bad Gateway` (a semantically wrong status for a content-policy rejection) — the test's own loose `>= 400` assertion would have passed either way, so the gap was only caught by deliberately checking the *specific* status code before trusting the default, not by the test alone.

**Bugs found:** No production bugs from the research itself, but two real gaps caught during implementation, both before they could ship: (1) `ErrGuardrailBlocked` needed its own `OUTCOME_GUARDRAIL_BLOCKED` proto enum value or it would have silently fallen into `outcomeFor`'s `default: OUTCOME_UPSTREAM_ERROR` case, misclassifying a content-policy rejection as an upstream failure — caught by cross-referencing the already-established `outcomeFor`/`Outcome` convention from the two prior `gatewayevents` RFCs, not named by the grounding research. (2) The same sentinel, left unmapped in `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `writeErrorResponse`, would have defaulted to `http.StatusBadGateway` (502) instead of the correct `400` — caught only by explicitly checking the real status code the integration test received, not by the test's own initial, looser assertion.

**Next steps / resume point:** Guardrails v1 is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. Remaining roadmap, none decided yet: PyPI publish of `kelvran-evals` (blocked on trademark clearance — a human/founder call), the evals skeptic-panel (explicitly `v2`-scoped, not a v1 candidate), the deferred L2 whitespace-collapse/case-folding normalizations and a real embedding-based semantic L3 (both gated on real production telemetry that doesn't exist yet), a real ML-grade moderation tier or real-time streaming enforcement for Guardrails itself (both explicitly deferred in this pass's own RFC), and `api/otel/`'s still-open future.

**Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's repeated "go ahead with next ideal steps... using dynamic workflows" instruction, this time pointed at auditing `evals/` specifically — it had had comparatively little attention this session versus `gateway`'s six shipped features. The research confirmed this was the single largest gap between `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own committed Data Model sketch and real code: of five sketched entities (`EvalCase`/`Run`/`Trace`/`Span`/`Score`), only `EvalCase` existed; `run_in_sandbox()` — real, working, well-tested — had zero callers anywhere outside its own two test files. The gap had been honestly named as deferred at scaffolding time (`docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-initial-code-scaffolding.md`) but had since fallen off the *operative* backlog — `STATUS.md`'s refreshed Next-Action list, updated after six gateway features shipped, never re-surfaced it. Distinct from wiring a real LLM-judge SDK call (the other candidate the research assessed and ranked second): that would add `evals`' first runtime dependency, first live secret, and first nondeterminism, while still having nothing to call it in production — a second orphaned seam, not a closed loop.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — `Run.cost_usd` defaults to `None`, never `0.0` (v1's sandbox-only harness makes no billed call; `None` is the honest "not measured" signal, resolving the RFC's own open question decisively rather than leaving it ambiguous); no `Trace`/`Span` field added, not even an empty placeholder (`api/otel`'s transport is still undecided — a stub field with no consumer would be dead code, per the org-wide YAGNI convention); `harness_config` narrowed to `{image, command, timeout_s}` only, not the full sketch's `scaffold_version`/`tool_budget`/`retry_policy`/`step_budget`/`sandbox_tier` set, since no pluggable multi-step harness exists yet to make those fields honest; sequential-only scheduling (no `asyncio.gather`, no pool), per `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own already-stated "asyncio at v1; Ray Core once justified" posture; a single shared `_score_output_deterministic` helper factored out of the pre-existing `_score_case_deterministic` so `run`'s baked-in-`output` scoring and `rollout`'s real-execution `stdout` scoring never duplicate the exact-match/regex-match dispatch logic. Deliberately did NOT fix a separate, real doc-vs-code gap the same research surfaced — `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Evals STRIDE table claims mitigations (tiered CI gates, network egress allowlisting, scoped per-tool credentials, five-layer defense-in-depth) with zero code behind any of them, structurally identical to the Guardrails-row gap already fixed in the same document for `gateway` — named in the RFC's Unresolved Questions and `STATUS.md` instead, as a real future candidate the same size class as Guardrails, not silently dropped.

**Verification performed:** Ran `uv run pytest tests/ -v` after each task (Task 1: 12/12 `test_models.py` tests; Task 2: 8 passed + 1 correctly skipped across `test_scheduler.py`/`test_results_store.py`; Task 3: full suite 60 passed, 6 skipped) and `ruff check .` (clean) before moving on. Confirmed a real local Docker daemon was reachable (`docker context ls` — the `colima` context, no `DOCKER_HOST` override needed for the plain `docker` CLI calls this feature makes) and ran the full suite a second time with `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1` against it — all 18 previously-skipped-or-default Docker-touching tests passed for real, including the two new load-bearing end-to-end proofs (`test_run_suite_against_a_real_docker_daemon`, `test_rollout_against_a_real_docker_daemon`) confirming the scheduler and the CLI actually drive a real container, not just a monkeypatched fake.

**Bugs found:** None in production code. Caught and self-corrected one process error while drafting `DECISIONS.md`: the new entry was first inserted before the immediately-preceding Guardrails entry, breaking this file's own append-only chronological convention (checked by re-reading the file's header rule after the fact, not before) — moved to the true end before treating the edit as done.

**Next steps / resume point:** `evals` Rollout Scheduler + `Run` model + Results Store is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. ## [2026-09-04] `evals` real Anthropic-backed LLM-judge wiring — sizing a second candidate (THREAT_MODEL.md Evals gap) correctly ruled it out for this pass

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (task `wusbd9y3q` / run `wf_348d608d-e87`, 4 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the choice between the two candidates the Rollout Scheduler RFC's own Alternatives Considered had named: wiring `judge()` to a real SDK, vs. fixing `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Evals STRIDE-table gap. **Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's repeated "go ahead with next ideal steps... using dynamic workflows" instruction. Both candidates had been explicitly named in the prior pass's own RFC — this pass's job was to test both assumptions rather than accept them. The research confirmed the LLM-judge wiring is now more load-bearing than it would have been before the Rollout Scheduler shipped: previously the only "output" anywhere in `evals/` was a fixture author's own pre-written string; now `Run.stdout` is real, Docker-captured output from a real execution, so judging it grades something that actually happened. The THREAT_MODEL.md Evals gap, once sized against real production sandboxing precedent (Docker Sandboxes, E2B, Modal, OpenAI's Codex sandbox), turned out not to be one bounded RFC at all — it splits into a near-zero-cost doc correction and 3-to-5 independently subsystem-sized security-hardening RFCs, several blocked on undecided architectural prerequisites (a tool-calling harness concept that doesn't exist anywhere in `evals/`; the Sandbox Pool/concurrency layer; `api/otel`'s still-undecided transport) rather than just unstarted engineering time. The prior pass's own "Guardrails-sized" estimate for that gap was wrong in both directions — too big for the doc-fix, too small for the full vision.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — Anthropic-only for v1 (`PRD.md`'s own "single LLM-judge scoring" scope line), a pinned dated model id (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`) bumped only by hand; `providers.py` as the sole SDK import site, client constructed lazily inside the factory function so importing the module never requires a key; `judge()`/`llm_judge.py` left completely untouched; a judge-call failure marks one case `JUDGE_ERROR` and the suite continues (mirroring `scheduler.py`'s own per-case failure precedent), while a missing `reference` fails loudly before any call is made (`judge()`'s `reference` is required, not optional — checked explicitly rather than left to fail inside the SDK call); `Run.cost_usd` deliberately NOT extended to track judge cost, since `Run` represents execution cost, not scoring cost — that belongs to a future `Score` record; a new, separate `RUN_LIVE_LLM_TESTS` env var rather than reusing `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS`, since the two gate different live dependencies and conflating them would either bill API calls on Docker-only runs or skip live-LLM tests for the wrong reason.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -v` after each task — Task 1: 4/4 new `test_providers.py` tests; Task 2: 10 passed + 1 correctly skipped across the updated `test_cli_integration.py`; full suite after Task 3: **67 passed, 7 skipped** (the new `test_llm_judge_integration.py` correctly shows as skipped by default, alongside the pre-existing Docker-gated skips). `ruff check .` clean throughout. Root `make verify` run after all tasks (both deployables; `gateway` untouched by this pass). Explicitly did NOT run the new `RUN_LIVE_LLM_TESTS=1`-gated integration test against a real key — `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is not configured in this environment, confirmed by checking directly rather than assuming — stated here honestly rather than claimed as verified; the test exists, is correctly gated, and is proven correct against a scripted fake via the CLI integration tests, but the actual live-network path has not been exercised end to end in this session.

**Bugs found:** None in production code. One near-repeat of the prior session's own process error, caught before it happened this time: re-checked `DECISIONS.md`'s real tail via a direct `tail -c` read immediately before appending, specifically because the immediately preceding pass's own log entry recorded having broken this file's append-only ordering once already — confirmed the new entry landed after the true last line, not before it.

## [2026-09-04] `evals` `Score` model + persistence — closing the last unbuilt Data Model entity, plus a doc-honesty cleanup pass beforehand

**Files touched (doc-honesty cleanup, no code change):** independently re-verified 3 claims from the LLM-judge-wiring pass's own research directly (`find`/`grep`, not trusted from the prior summary alone) before editing: confirmed no `internal/router` directory exists anywhere in `gateway/`, confirmed `go-arch-lint` appears nowhere outside prose in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`/`Makefile`, and independently discovered a third, previously-unflagged instance of the same class of gap — `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` claims a `.importlinter` file exists (Python analogue of `go-arch-lint`); it doesn't, and nothing references it in CI or `pyproject.toml`. Modified `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Package Layout's `/internal/router` line, Dependency-rules line), `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (`.importlinter` line), `THREAT_MODEL.md` (Evals Information-Disclosure/DoS/Elevation-of-Privilege rows + a new dated Review-Cadence changelog entry), `SECURITY.md` (matching pointer row + Known Limitations), `DECISIONS.md` (one-line entry — a bounded doc-only change per the Scope Gate convention, no plan file). Committed and pushed separately (`ee99115`) from the feature work below.

**Files touched (`Score` model):** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (task `wfsu61qoc` / run `wf_2b122eb6-ca4`, 3 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design — a code-audit tracing exactly where a score value exists today and is discarded, a design-proposal resolving the `run_id`-without-a-`Run` question decisively, and a precedent angle surveying promptfoo/Inspect AI/DeepEval/Braintrust's real score-record shapes (pulled live from each project's source). Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-evals-score-model.md` (spec) and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-evals-score-model.md` (5-task plan); modified `evals/evals/models.py` (+`Score`/`ScorerType`) and `evals/tests/test_models.py`; moved `evals/evals/rollout/results_store.py` → `evals/evals/results_store.py`, generalized over any frozen pydantic model (PEP 695 type-parameter syntax, `def _append_models[ModelT: BaseModel](...)`, to satisfy `ruff`'s `UP047` under this repo's py312 target) and modified `evals/tests/test_results_store.py`; renamed `evals/evals/judge/providers.py`'s `_DEFAULT_JUDGE_MODEL` → public `DEFAULT_JUDGE_MODEL` (confirmed via grep: zero other references, zero-risk); modified `evals/evals/cli.py` (widened `_judge_output`/`_judge_case`/`_judge_run` to return `JudgeResult | None` instead of `bool | None`; new `_deterministic_scorer_id` helper; new required `--scores` option + `Score` construction/persistence on both `run_cmd` and `rollout_cmd`, all four call sites) and rewrote `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (every existing invocation gained `--scores`, plus new Score-persistence assertions); updated `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Data Model sketch, Package Layout, Rollout Lifecycle disambiguating note), `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's "go ahead with the recommended next ideal steps for this one" — the two items I'd recommended: the doc-honesty cleanup (cheap, no feature work) and the `Score` model (the named next-next pick from the LLM-judge-wiring RFC). The `Score` gap was the single most acute unresolved discard in `evals/`: `judge()` already computes a full `rationale`+`bias_mitigations_applied` pair on every successful call, and `cli.py`'s `_judge_output` threw both away one line later, returning only a bare `bool` — exactly the kind of "computed, then discarded" gap this project has caught and fixed before (the Rollout Scheduler's own `Run.cost_usd`/`Trace` decisions), just one layer further down the stack.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — `Score.run_id` nullable (`None` for `evals run`, real `Run.id` for `evals rollout`), never a fabricated `EvalCase.id` or a synthetic `Run`, resolving the one genuinely hard design question by extending the exact convention `Run.cost_usd: None` already established; a new `eval_case_id`/`eval_case_revision` join key, not in the original `ARCHITECTURE.md` sketch, added because it's the only thing both commands can always honestly supply; a deliberate, named divergence from every framework surveyed (promptfoo/Inspect AI/DeepEval/Braintrust all embed scores on one run/sample record, never a separate normalized entity) — justified specifically by Kelvran's two-command, two-atomic-unit shape, which none of the surveyed frameworks have; `results_store.py` relocated out of `rollout/` in the same pass rather than accepting a real, growing layering smell (a non-rollout command importing from a rollout-namespaced module); `--scores` required on both commands, a deliberate breaking CLI change, chosen for consistency with `--results`'s existing convention rather than an optional-with-default alternative.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/test_models.py -v` (17 passed) after Task 1; `test_results_store.py -v` (6 passed) after Task 2; `test_providers.py -v` (4 passed) after Task 3; full suite after Task 4 — **75 passed, 7 skipped**; `ruff check .` — caught and fixed one real lint violation (`UP047`: the generic `_append_models`/`_load_models` helpers needed PEP 695 type-parameter syntax, not a bare module-level `TypeVar`, to satisfy this repo's py312-target ruff config) before it could ship. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **21 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed), including `test_rollout_against_a_real_docker_daemon` proving the new `--scores` persistence works end to end against a real container, not just a monkeypatched fake. Root `make verify` run after all tasks; `gateway` untouched by this pass.

**Bugs found:** None in production logic. One real lint violation caught before shipping (described above, `UP047`) — not a correctness bug, but a real deviation from this repo's own committed style convention that would have failed CI had it not been caught locally first.

**Next steps / resume point:** `evals` `Score` model + persistence is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. Remaining roadmap, none decided yet: PyPI publish of `kelvran-evals` (blocked on trademark clearance), a query/report command over persisted `Score`s (named as a real, deliberate gap in this RFC's own Unresolved Questions — `report_cmd` still only takes raw counts), judge-call cost tracking (closing `Run.cost_usd: None`'s remaining gap once a real cost signal exists), the evals skeptic-panel (`v2`-scoped), the deferred L2/L3 cache work (telemetry-gated), Guardrails' own deferred ML-moderation/streaming-enforcement, `api/otel/`'s still-open future, and the 3-to-5-RFC security-hardening program the THREAT_MODEL.md Evals-gap sizing pass surfaced (egress-allowlist proxy, credential-broker proxy, sandbox-runtime-isolation upgrade, CI-cost-gating).

## [2026-09-04] `evals report --scores` — small, bounded follow-up, no RFC/plan

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (task `wziptjg3j` / run `wf_00546fc3-f92`, 2 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design — a code-audit confirming `report_cmd`'s exact current shape and surfacing a real, un-named aggregation-semantics question (repeated `eval_case_id`s from re-run suites, mixed `scorer_type`s in one file), and a design-proposal resolving every open question decisively. Modified `evals/evals/cli.py` (new `load_scores` import, module docstring update, `report_cmd` rewritten with a new `--scores` option, `--successes`/`--total` made optional-but-paired, `click.UsageError` for bad flag combinations) and `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (7 new tests). Updated `evals/changelog/unreleased.md` and `DECISIONS.md` (one-line entry — this is a bounded change per the Scope Gate convention, no RFC/plan file).

**Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's "go ahead with the recommended next ideal steps" — this was the one genuinely bounded, ready-now candidate among the options I'd surfaced (PyPI needs a founder decision; judge-cost-tracking is more design-heavy; the THREAT_MODEL.md security-hardening program is explicitly multi-RFC, not a single next step). Named verbatim in the `Score`-model RFC's own Unresolved Questions ("a query/report command over persisted Scores... `report_cmd` stays a bare-counts formatter, named as a real, deliberate gap").

**Decisions made:** Extend the existing `report` command with `--scores`, mutually exclusive with `--successes`/`--total`, rather than a new command — `report_cmd`'s only job is already "produce `(successes, total)`, call `format_report`," and a `Score`s file is just a second way to arrive at those two ints. Aggregation is deliberately blunt (every `Score` = one trial, no dedup/filtering) — the same counting philosophy `run`/`rollout` already use in-process, not a stricter one invented here. Distinct `scorer_type`s are grouped and reported separately, never blended, since they're different measurement instruments. `--scores` here is a *read* input (`click.Path(exists=True, ...)`) — deliberately the opposite config from `run`/`rollout`'s `--scores` (a *write* target, no `exists=True`) — flagged explicitly as the one easy-to-miss bug a copy-paste-by-reflex would introduce.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/test_cli_integration.py -v` → 17 passed, 1 skipped (including the load-bearing "never blends" test, which picks counts that would visibly differ from a blended number). Full suite: **82 passed, 7 skipped**. `ruff check .` clean. Root `make verify` passes end-to-end; `gateway` untouched.

**Bugs found:** None — this was new, additive CLI wiring with no production-logic changes to `Score`/`results_store`/`wilson_interval`/`format_report`.

**Next steps / resume point:** `evals report --scores` is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. Remaining roadmap, none decided yet: PyPI publish (blocked on trademark clearance), judge-call cost tracking, no dedup/filtering for `report --scores` (named as an accepted v1 gap, not a bug), the evals skeptic-panel (`v2`-scoped), the deferred L2/L3 cache work, Guardrails' deferred ML-moderation/streaming-enforcement, `api/otel/`'s still-open future, and the 3-to-5-RFC THREAT_MODEL.md security-hardening program.

## [2026-09-04] Real judge-call cost tracking on `Score.cost_usd` — small, bounded follow-on, no RFC/plan

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (task `wwso7dp9u` / run `wf_8a183f6d-be3`, 2 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design against a hard constraint both prior evals RFCs established: `judge()`/`llm_judge.py` must stay completely untouched. One angle verified the callable-object mechanism live (a class instance with `__call__` and a mutable attribute, passed through a function typed `Callable[[str], Awaitable[str]]`, worked with zero friction) and introspected the real, installed `anthropic` 1.3.0 SDK's `Usage` type directly rather than from memory. The other angle read gateway's own Decimal-cost-accounting RFC/code for precedent, live-fetched Anthropic's real current pricing for `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` from three independent official pages, and tested pydantic v2's real `Decimal` JSON-serialization behavior directly. The synthesis independently re-verified every claim and resolved the one place it diverged from a naive reading of the research (deterministic `Score.cost_usd` is `0.0`, not `None`, since the two situations — an unmeasured sandbox cost vs. a categorically-impossible network call — are not analogous). Modified `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` (new `JudgeCallCost` dataclass, `_JUDGE_MODEL_PRICE_PER_MTOK_USD` price table + `_compute_cost_usd`, `make_anthropic_call_model`'s closure replaced by the `_AnthropicCallModel` callable class) and `evals/tests/test_providers.py` (fake `usage` object added to every fixture, 4 new tests); modified `evals/evals/models.py` (`Score.cost_usd`) and `evals/tests/test_models.py` (2 new tests); modified `evals/evals/cli.py` (new `_last_judge_call_cost_usd` helper, wired at all 4 `Score`-construction call sites) and `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (4 new/extended tests). Updated `evals/changelog/unreleased.md` and `DECISIONS.md` (one-line entry — bounded change, no RFC/plan, per the Scope Gate convention).

**Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's "go ahead with the recommended next ideal steps" — the one remaining genuinely bounded, ready-now candidate (dedup/filtering for `report --scores` has no real consumer need yet per YAGNI; PyPI needs a founder decision; the THREAT_MODEL.md security-hardening program is explicitly multi-RFC). Named verbatim in both the LLM-judge-wiring RFC's Drawbacks and the Score-model RFC's Alternatives Considered #5 as the deferred follow-on once `providers.py` could surface real usage data.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — a callable object (`_AnthropicCallModel`) with a rebound-not-mutated `last_call_cost` attribute is the only channel for cost data that doesn't widen `judge()`'s contract, verified mechanically sound via a live runtime test before adopting, not just PEP 484 reasoning; real Anthropic pricing live-fetched and cross-checked against three independent official pages, not recalled, with an explicit hand-re-verification trigger date; `float` over `Decimal` for `Score.cost_usd`, a scope-specific decision (no compounding operation exists over this field today) rather than a blanket copy of gateway's own Decimal precedent; deterministic `Score.cost_usd = 0.0` (a certain fact) vs. `llm_judge`'s real computed value vs. `None` only for an unpriced model — explicitly not the same convention as `Run.cost_usd`'s `None`, and named as such rather than left as an apparent inconsistency; `cli.py` reads the new attribute via `getattr(call_model, "last_call_cost", None)` so every existing test's plain-async-function fakes keep working unmodified, correctly falling back to `cost_usd=None` rather than raising.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/test_providers.py -v` (8 passed) and `tests/test_models.py -v` (19 passed) after their respective edits; full suite after `cli.py` wiring — **90 passed, 7 skipped**; `ruff check .` clean. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **30 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed). Independently ran real Python introspection (not assumed) to confirm `anthropic.types.Usage`'s exact fields and pydantic 2.13.5's exact `Decimal`-to-JSON-string serialization behavior before committing to the design. Zero regressions — every pre-existing llm-judge test's plain-function fake continued to work unmodified, correctly producing `cost_usd=None`, proving the `getattr` fallback is real, not just claimed.

**Bugs found:** None in production logic. Caught, before it could ship, that the existing `_FakeMessage` test fixtures in `test_providers.py` had no `.usage` attribute at all — every one of the 3 pre-existing provider tests would have failed with `AttributeError` the moment `_AnthropicCallModel.__call__` tried to read `response.usage.input_tokens`, had the fixtures not been updated in the same pass.

**Next steps / resume point:** Real judge-call cost tracking is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. Remaining roadmap, none decided yet: PyPI publish of `kelvran-evals` (blocked on trademark clearance), suite-level cost aggregation in `report --scores` (named revisit trigger for `float`→`Decimal`, not built), an `evals`-side budget-enforcement analog (the other named `Decimal` revisit trigger), dedup/filtering for `report --scores` by `eval_case_id`/revision/tier, the evals skeptic-panel (`v2`-scoped), the deferred L2/L3 cache work, Guardrails' deferred ML-moderation/streaming-enforcement, `api/otel/`'s still-open future, and the 3-to-5-RFC THREAT_MODEL.md security-hardening program.

## [2026-09-04] `evals report --scores` cost aggregation — the named Decimal-adoption trigger fired, executed it

**Files touched:** no new research pass this time — the design was already fully argued in the immediately-preceding judge-call-cost-tracking entry, which explicitly named "the moment `evals` gets a suite-level cost aggregation... Decimal should be adopted immediately, not after a repro is needed to justify it." This pass builds that exact aggregation, so it executes a pre-written decision rather than making a new one. Modified `evals/evals/models.py` (`Score.cost_usd: float | None` → `Decimal | None`), `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` (price table + `_compute_cost_usd` migrated to `Decimal`, built from string literals), `evals/evals/cli.py` (the two deterministic `cost_usd=0.0` sites → `Decimal("0")`; new `_format_group_cost` helper; `report_cmd`'s per-`scorer_type` grouping now prints a summed `total_cost_usd`). Fixed 3 pre-existing test assertions that compared a real `Decimal` result against a bare float literal (`test_call_model_records_last_call_cost_from_real_usage`, `test_score_cost_usd_can_be_set_explicitly_for_llm_judge`, `test_run_with_llm_judge_persists_real_cost_from_a_cost_exposing_fake`) — each now compares against a `Decimal(...)` literal instead. Added 2 new `report --scores` tests (cost summed correctly per group, an unpriced/`None` entry excluded and counted rather than silently zeroed). Updated `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md` (one-line entry, bounded change, no RFC/plan).

**Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's "go ahead with the recommended next ideal steps." Of the remaining open candidates, this was the one with zero open design questions left to resolve — the prior pass had already fully specified both the "why Decimal" reasoning and the exact trigger condition, in writing, before this pass began. The alternative candidates (dedup/filtering for `report --scores`, an `evals`-side budget-enforcement analog) still have no real consumer need; PyPI needs a founder decision; the THREAT_MODEL.md program is multi-RFC.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line — migrate `Score.cost_usd` to `Decimal` now that it's genuinely summed (crossing the pre-named trigger), not proactively. Independently verified, before writing any test, that pydantic v2 safely converts a float *input* to a Decimal field via `Decimal(str(value))` (confirmed empirically), but a raw `Decimal` *result* compared directly against a bare float literal is not reliably safe (`Decimal('0.0001234') == 0.0001234` → `False`, confirmed both directions) — every test assertion touching a computed `Decimal` now compares against a `Decimal(...)` literal, never a bare float, to avoid a real, silent test-correctness trap. A `None`-cost `Score` in a group is excluded from the sum and reported as an explicit count, never folded in as zero — honest, even though currently unreachable (only one priced judge model exists).

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **91 passed, 7 skipped**; `ruff check .` clean. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **31 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed). Root `make verify` passes end-to-end; `gateway` untouched.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code — but the migration surfaced exactly the test-correctness trap named above (3 pre-existing assertions that only happened to pass because their specific float values were coincidentally exact in binary — `7.00`, `0.0`, `0.0035` — would have silently broken or masked a real regression the moment a less-round number was used) before any of them actually failed in CI; caught by running the suite immediately after the type change, not assumed safe.

**Next steps / resume point:** `evals report --scores` cost aggregation is implemented, verified, and ready to commit. Remaining roadmap, none decided yet: PyPI publish of `kelvran-evals` (blocked on trademark clearance), an `evals`-side budget-enforcement analog over accumulated judge spend (the other named `Decimal` trigger, now that aggregation itself is real), dedup/filtering for `report --scores` by `eval_case_id`/revision/tier, the evals skeptic-panel (`v2`-scoped), the deferred L2/L3 cache work, Guardrails' deferred ML-moderation/streaming-enforcement, `api/otel/`'s still-open future, and the 3-to-5-RFC THREAT_MODEL.md security-hardening program.

## [2026-09-04] `gateway/internal/router` — weighted routing, full RFC+plan pipeline

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (task `wnurowk0l` / run `wf_53b18285-e69`, 5 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design — a code-audit tracing `nextDeployment`'s exact current shape (`dataplane.go:617-633`, an atomic-counter round-robin over `deploymentsByModel`, plus a single fallback attempt), a precedent angle surveying LiteLLM/Portkey/Kong/Envoy's real routing-strategy implementations (verified from source/live docs, not memory), a circuit-breaker precedent angle (Envoy outlier detection, Hystrix, resilience4j — all verified against real source/docs), a `PRD.md` scope-boundary check that found the single most load-bearing fact (PRD.md's v1 scope line already says "static + weighted routing; a single fallback chain" verbatim — settling the scope question decisively rather than leaving it open), and a config/integration-design angle. The synthesis independently re-verified every claim against the live repo and proved, by hand-tracing both algorithms from their real initial states, that smooth weighted round-robin degrades to byte-identical the old round-robin sequence for any uniform weight. Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-weighted-routing.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-weighted-routing.md`. New package `gateway/internal/router/` (`deployment.go`, `wrr.go`, `router.go`, `router_test.go` — the real LVS/IPVS `wrr.c` smooth-WRR cursor algorithm, a shared-kernel leaf, never importing `dataplane`/`cache`). Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (+`DeploymentConfig.Weight`, parsed/validated non-negative) and `config_test.go` (+3 tests); `gateway/config.example.yaml` (commented `weight:` illustration); `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`Config.Router` required field, `Pipeline.router`/`deploymentsByName` replacing `deploymentsByModel`/`rrMu`/`rrCounters`, `nextDeployment` rewritten as a thin wrapper, `sync`/`sync/atomic` imports removed); `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`buildPipeline` builds a parallel `[]router.Deployment` slice and wires `router.New(...)` into `dataplane.Config.Router`). Updated every dataplane test `Config{}` construction site (`dataplane_test.go` — new `testRouter` helper shared package-wide, `lexical_cache_test.go`, `streaming_test.go` ×2 helpers, `guardrail_test.go`, `gatewayevents_test.go` ×4 sites) to supply `Router: testRouter(deployments)`. Updated `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (`/internal/router` Package Layout entry flipped to ACTIVE; dependency-direction table gains `router`; Request Lifecycle's stale "circuit-breaker" mention removed), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Picked per the founder's "go ahead with the recommended next ideal steps for this one" — the explicit next real feature named by the prior pass's sanity-check fork. Closes the one gap in `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s already-corrected `/internal/router` Package Layout entry (2026-09-04, commit `ee99115`) that `PRD.md`'s own v1 scope line commits to: weighted routing. Unlike the doc-honesty passes immediately before this one, this genuinely warranted the full RFC+plan pipeline — a new package, a new required `Pipeline` dependency, a config schema change.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line. The scoping decision was the load-bearing one: `PRD.md`'s v1 allowlist ("static + weighted routing; a single fallback chain") already settles which routing strategies are in v1 — usage/latency/cost-based routing, a circuit breaker, and model-*group* fallback chains are all real, well-precedented designs (researched in depth) but deliberately excluded here, matching this session's established narrowing discipline (Cache L2 → 3 safe ops, Cache L3-lite → MinHash not real embeddings, Guardrails → regex not NER/ML). Algorithm choice: smooth WRR (LVS/IPVS `wrr.c`) over naive weight-expansion, because expansion is `O(Σweight)` memory (a config typo like `weight: 1000000` becomes a real hazard) and produces bursty traffic for any weight > 1, where smooth WRR is `O(1)` per deployment and proven to degrade to today's exact round-robin for equal weights. `nextDeployment`'s signature deliberately kept unchanged so `HandleChatCompletion`/`streamDeploymentWithFallback` needed zero call-site edits — only its body changed.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean from `gateway/`. `go test ./internal/router/...` — 5 new tests pass, including the decisive backward-compat proof (`TestSelectDegradesToRoundRobinForEqualWeights`, exact-sequence assertion) and a proportional-count test for genuinely weighted deployments (exact counts over a multiple of total weight, not statistical). `go test ./...` from `gateway/` — every pre-existing dataplane test passes with **zero changes to any test's expected call-count/order assertions** (confirming the degrade proof empirically, not just by hand-trace), including `TestHandleChatCompletionFallsBackOnUpstreamError`. One pre-existing, unrelated failure confirmed via `git stash`/re-run on unmodified `main`: `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` fails in this sandbox for a rootless-Docker/testcontainers environment reason, not caused by this change. `go test -race ./internal/router/... ./internal/gateway/dataplane/... ./internal/gateway/controlplane/...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` — 0 issues. Root `make verify` run before commit.

## [2026-09-04] Security fix on the just-shipped `docker-compose.yml` — found by an automated push review, not self-caught

**Files touched:** `docker-compose.yml`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** An automated push security review of the Phase 0/1 commit (`3247aa6`) flagged "Unauthenticated Service Exposed on All Network Interfaces" — real, and correct: the `redis` service's `6379:6379` port mapping binds to `0.0.0.0` by default in Docker, and `redis:7-alpine` ships with zero authentication configured (no `requirepass`, no ACL). Any host reachable on the network — not just the local machine — could have connected to it the moment `--profile redis up` was run.

**Decisions made:** Removed the host port mapping from `redis` entirely rather than binding it to `127.0.0.1` — `gateway` never needed the host port published at all; Compose's internal network already resolves the service name (`redis:6379`) for container-to-container traffic with zero extra config, so there's no functional reason to expose it to the host at all, let alone every interface. Also tightened `gateway`'s own port to `127.0.0.1:8080:8080` (was `8080:8080`) as defense-in-depth — it has real bearer-token auth so the risk profile is different, but there's no reason a local-dev bring-up should default to LAN-reachable either; a one-line comment tells an operator how to opt back into `0.0.0.0` if they genuinely need it.

**Verification performed:** All three properties proven directly with the containers actually running, not assumed from reading the YAML: `curl 127.0.0.1:8080/v1/chat/completions` → real `401` (gateway still fully reachable on loopback); `curl 127.0.0.1:6379` → `000`/connection refused (Redis no longer reachable from the host at all); a throwaway `redis:7-alpine` container run with `--network kelvran_default` and pointed at `redis-cli -h redis ping` → real `PONG` (internal, container-to-container reachability fully preserved — only host exposure was removed, not functionality).

**Bugs found:** One real, security-relevant bug in code I'd shipped in the immediately preceding pass — an unauthenticated service bound to all network interfaces. Caught by an external review mechanism, not by me — logged plainly rather than glossed over, matching this project's own "dated honesty" principle and the exact category `SECURITY.md`'s pre-commit checklist exists to catch (`AGENTS.md`'s Boundaries section and `~/.claude/rules/security.md`'s "Auth checks on every route" spirit extends naturally to "don't publish an unauthenticated dependency's port to the world," even for a local-dev file).

**Next steps / resume point:** This fix is ready to commit/push/watch CI. Phase 2 of the approved backlog plan (evals rollout cost/DoS mitigation; a real `openaicompat` adapter) remains next, unchanged.

## [2026-09-04] Backlog Phase 2a — evals rollout cost/DoS mitigation, a naive design corrected before any code shipped

**Files touched:** ran a dynamic `Workflow` (task `wbfu12dtr` / run `wf_4cac7a18-3af`, 4 parallel research angles + synthesis) to ground the design — a code-audit of the real scheduler/models/results-store/stats substrate, a dedicated precedent angle on sequential early-stopping methodology (the single most important finding of this pass), a precedent angle on result-caching design (Bazel/Buck2/GH Actions/pytest-testmon/Inspect AI/DeepEval/promptfoo), and an engineering design proposal. Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-evals-rollout-cost-mitigation.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-evals-rollout-cost-mitigation.md`. New `evals/evals/rollout/cache.py` (`compute_run_cache_key`); `evals/evals/stats.py` (+`two_checkpoint_early_stop`); `evals/evals/models.py` (`Run` +`cache_key`/`from_cache`/`cache_source_run_id`/`skip_reason`, `RunStatus` +`"skipped"`); `evals/evals/rollout/scheduler.py` (rewritten — `run_suite` gains `cached_runs`/`early_stop` keyword params, `EarlyStopConfig` dataclass, a defensive cache-status re-check, async tally/stop bookkeeping); `evals/evals/cli.py` (`rollout_cmd` gains `--use-cache`/`--early-stop-*`, a unified async `_score_and_record`/`_run_and_score`, `_judge_run` removed as dead code). New/extended tests: `evals/tests/test_cache.py`, `test_stats.py`, `test_stats_properties.py`, `test_scheduler.py`, `test_models.py`, `test_cli_integration.py`. Updated `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Evals DoS row + Review Cadence, `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Continuing the approved 4-phase backlog plan's Phase 2 per the founder's "go ahead with next ideal phase or steps... using dynamic workflows." Picked the evals side first since it directly closes a `THREAT_MODEL.md`-named live gap. The single most consequential finding of the whole pass: the early-stopping design the backlog plan itself had floated — "stop once the running Wilson CI excludes an operator-supplied baseline, rechecked after every trial" — is the textbook optional-stopping/peeking problem (Armitage et al. 1969; Johari, Koomen, Pekelis & Walsh, KDD 2017, the paper behind Optimizely's Stats Engine) and would have inflated the true false-positive rate well above nominal. Corrected to a two-checkpoint, Bonferroni-corrected design before any code was written.

**Decisions made:** See the new `DECISIONS.md` line. Both mechanisms are off-by-default and additive — `run_suite(cases)` with no new kwargs is byte-for-byte identical to before. Explicitly rejected: a pluggable `EarlyStopFn` strategy interface (premature generality — v1 ships exactly one strategy), real SPRT (needs a real scope addition, an operator-supplied alternative pass rate), more than 2 checkpoints (each needs its own alpha-spending design), `Score`-level (judge) caching (a distinct correctness risk deserving its own review), and calibrated per-tier trial-count defaults (no production traffic to calibrate against, same reasoning as the router's deferred circuit-breaker).

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **127 passed, 7 skipped**; `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **48 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed) — including the two pre-existing real-Docker tests, proving zero regression to the real execution path. The load-bearing regression test (`test_stats_properties.py::test_two_checkpoint_design_keeps_false_stop_rate_bounded_under_the_null`) simulates the true null hypothesis many times and asserts the empirical false-stop rate stays well below the naive design's independently-measured >20% (`test_naive_continuous_recheck_would_have_failed_this_exact_test`) — an executable proof of the RFC's central correctness claim, not just a citation. Root `make verify`: `gateway` hits the same pre-existing, unrelated rootless-Docker `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` failure already documented earlier this session (confirmed unrelated — this pass touches zero Go code); `evals`'s own `make test-evals`/`lint-evals` targets both clean.

**Bugs found:** Two real bugs, both caught by this feature's own test suite during implementation, before shipping — neither assumed away: (1) `run_suite`'s cache lookup had no defensive check of its own that a matching prior `Run` was actually `status == "completed"` — relying solely on the CLI's `cached_runs` construction to filter this out. A scheduler-level test constructing an `error`-status entry directly caught that `run_suite` would otherwise fabricate a fake completed hit from a transient infra failure; fixed with a defensive re-check at the lowest layer. (2) `EarlyStopConfig.score_fn`, first written as a plain synchronous callable, broke the moment a CLI-integration test asserted a real judge-call count: `run_suite` is already running inside an event loop by the time it calls `score_fn`, and the `--llm-judge` scoring path's existing helper drives its own internal `asyncio.run()`, which raises when nested — an error that was being silently swallowed into a wrong `JUDGE_ERROR` rather than surfacing. Fixed by making `score_fn` async and giving `rollout_cmd` a single async-native scoring closure, unifying its whole execution under one `asyncio.run()` call.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 2a is implemented and verified, ready to commit/push/watch CI. Phase 2b (a real `openaicompat` adapter, gateway-side) is next — already grounded via a separate dynamic workflow (task `whjfwkw4y` / run `wf_d3731c27-477`) run in parallel with this one. Phase 3 (evals `Trace`/`Span` self-instrumentation; `internal/admin`'s scope-bounded-only recommendation) remains after that.

The one genuine, required change: `Choice`'s doc comment now explicitly warns that TGI never emits `"tool_calls"` as `finish_reason`, so callers must detect tool calls by `Message.ToolCalls`'s presence, never by `finish_reason`'s value.

Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-evals-trace-span-model.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-evals-trace-span-model.md`. Added `evals/evals/tracing.py` (new) and `evals/tests/test_tracing.py` (new). Modified `evals/evals/models.py` (+`SpanStatus`/`Span`), `evals/evals/results_store.py` (+`append_spans`/`load_spans`), `evals/evals/rollout/scheduler.py` (`run_suite` gains `span_sink`), `evals/evals/cli.py` (`rollout_cmd` gains required `--traces`), `evals/pyproject.toml` (+`opentelemetry-sdk`), `evals/tests/{test_models.py,test_results_store.py,test_scheduler.py,test_cli_integration.py}`. Modified `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Data Model, Package Layout, Tech Stack Tracing row), `THREAT_MODEL.md` (Evals Elevation-of-Privilege row + Review Cadence), `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md` (two entries — Phase 3a shipped, Phase 3b scope call).

**Intent/summary:** Closing Phase 3 of the approved backlog plan. Phase 3a resolves `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s "full audit logging tied to the trace" gap entirely inside `evals`, per the plan's own explicit design: reject both a new `api/otel` proto contract and live gateway-OTLP consumption as premature (no real consumer of either exists yet), and instead wrap every real `run_in_sandbox()` call with a self-contained OTel Python SDK span. Phase 3b is a documentation-only scope call, not an implementation — `gateway/internal/admin` gets no RFC/plan/code in this pass, deliberately, per the plan's own instruction that it's the biggest, least-scoped remaining backlog item.

**Decisions made:** `Span` only, no `Trace{spans:[Span]}` wrapper — today's harness makes exactly one span per `Run`, the same "don't build the diagram-only box" discipline already applied to `Task/Dataset Registry`/`Sandbox Pool`. A locally-held `TracerProvider` in `tracing.py`, never the process-wide `trace.set_tracer_provider()` singleton — confirmed via source inspection to be `Once()`-guarded, wrong for a library invoked repeatedly across CLI processes and an in-process pytest suite. No `gen_ai.*` attributes anywhere on the sandbox span — a real design mistake already sitting in `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own sketch, caught by the grounding research: `gen_ai.operation.name`'s real enum values are all LLM/model-inference operations, none of which describe a container-sandbox execution. Real (if experimental/incubating) `process.*`/`container.*` attributes used only where genuinely obtainable from today's `docker run --rm` invocation — no fabricated `container_id`/`process_pid`, named explicitly as a real, deliberate gap rather than a silent omission. `--traces` required on `rollout_cmd`, mirroring `--results`/`--scores`'s existing required-flag precedent; `evals run` untouched. `run_suite`'s `span_sink` kwarg mirrors `cached_runs`/`early_stop`'s existing opt-in-additive convention exactly — `None` reproduces prior behavior byte-for-byte.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **146 passed, 8 skipped**; `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **153 passed, 1 skipped** — only the live-LLM-key-gated test remains skipped). Independently verified the OTel SDK's real behavior directly (not just trusting the grounding research) before writing `tracing.py`: hand-ran `TracerProvider()`→custom `SpanProcessor`→`start_span`/`end()` with zero exporters registered, confirmed real 128-bit/64-bit hex IDs, confirmed `finished.status.status_code.name` round-trips to the exact `"OK"`/`"ERROR"` literals `SpanStatus` expects. New tests prove: `span_sink=None` reproduces every pre-existing `run_suite` assertion unmodified; a cache hit and an early-stop skip both append zero spans; a sandbox-launch exception produces exactly one `status="ERROR"` span with `process_exit_code=None`; a real end-to-end `rollout --traces` CLI invocation (both mocked and, separately, against a real Docker daemon) persists exactly one `Span` per genuinely-executed trial with `run_id` matching the real persisted `Run.id`.

**Bugs found:** One self-inflicted tooling bug, caught before it shipped: a `replace_all` mechanical edit adding `--traces`/`str(traces_path)` to every `"--scores", str(scores_path)` block in `test_cli_integration.py` was blind to command context — it also added `--traces` to several `evals run` and `evals report` test invocations, neither of which has that option at all. Caught immediately by the resulting test failures (`Error: Missing option '--traces'` was never seen — instead click's "no such option" surfaced first for `run`/`report`); fixed by manually reverting the incorrect additions in those 8 test functions before re-running the full suite green. No production-code bugs found — the OTel SDK usage worked exactly as verified during grounding, first try.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 3 (both halves) is implemented and verified, ready to commit/push/watch CI. This closes every phase of the approved backlog plan at `/Users/sairamugge/.claude/plans/snuggly-exploring-finch.md` that was implementable now. Everything named as blocked/gated in that plan (PyPI, the router's circuit-breaker, MCP/A2A, the skeptic panel, Guardrails NER/ML, Cache L3 real embeddings, evals' credential-broker/cross-sandbox isolation, bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Kubernetes manifests, and now `gateway/internal/admin`'s future RFC) remains unchanged and explicitly excluded.

**Files touched:** Grounding research (3-angle dynamic workflow, wire format / streaming+auth / function-calling+finish-reasons, plus a synthesis pass) run before writing the RFC — the synthesis independently re-verified against real installed source is a recurring pattern this session, but the RFC-writing pass itself went further: fetched and parsed Google's live API discovery document (`generativelanguage.googleapis.com/$discovery/rest?version=v1beta`) directly, catching a real correction to the research's own recommendation (see Decisions). Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-gemini-adapter.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-gemini-adapter.md`. Rewrote `gateway/internal/adapter/gemini/gemini.go` (stub → real); new `stream.go`, `gemini_test.go`, `regression_test.go`, `stream_test.go`, `testdata/` (4 JSON + 3 SSE `.txt` fixtures, all generated by actually running the adapter code and eyeballing the output, not hand-transcribed). Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`responseUnmarshalers` +`"gemini"`; `setUpstreamAuthHeaders` +`"gemini"` case; new `streamUpstreamURL` function + one call-site change in `NewHTTPUpstreamStreamCaller`); new `streamurl_test.go`. Modified `gateway/cmd/gateway/integration_test.go` (+3 mock-upstream helpers, +3 integration tests including a real two-turn tool-call round trip); fixed 2 pre-existing tests (`TestIntegrationStreamingUnsupportedProviderReturnsBadRequest`, `TestHandleChatCompletionStreamUnsupportedProviderReturnsTypedError`) that had used `"gemini"` as their literal example of "a provider that doesn't support streaming" — no longer true. Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go` (deployment count 2→3 + new field assertions) after adding a real `gemini-flash-primary` entry to `gateway/config.example.yaml`. Updated `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Package Layout adapter line — also removed a stale `"vertex"` package reference found in the same line, never a real package) and `gateway/internal/streaming/types.go`'s `StreamingAdapter` doc comment.

**Intent/summary:** A fresh backlog item found by a post-plan inventory sweep, not part of the previously-approved 4-phase plan: `PRD.md` explicitly names Gemini in its v1 scope line, and `gemini.go` was still the same honest stub `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-initial-code-scaffolding.md` originally left it as. Closing it mirrors the `openaicompat` pass's own process (grounding → RFC → plan → implementation) but required real, new architectural work `openaicompat` never did.

**Decisions made:** A locally-scoped `net/url`-based `streamUpstreamURL` function derives Gemini's streaming URL from its configured buffered `base_url` at call time — rejected adding a second `Deployment.StreamBaseURL` config field (a schema change every other provider would carry unused) and rejected requiring two separate `gemini` deployments per model (breaks the router's one-model-to-one-deployment-set assumption). Independently re-verified, directly against Google's live discovery schema, that `FunctionCall`/`FunctionResponse` both carry a real (if optional) `id` field — better than the grounding research's own more conservative "positional matching only, don't build a matching algorithm" recommendation; used the `id` when available, while still keeping the required `name`-resolution-from-history logic regardless (the `id` is an enhancement layered on top, not a replacement — `name` resolution is unavoidable either way since it's a required field the canonical `role:"tool"` message never carries directly). The 5 real Gemini `finishReason` values indicating the model's own tool-call machinery broke surface as a typed error, never a fake successful `Choice`. Streaming's `Decode` always returns `done=false` (confirmed, by direct read of `dataplane/streaming.go`, that the existing loop already handles this via `io.EOF` — not a new mechanism). `bedrock` deliberately untouched.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `internal/adapter/gemini`'s own suite: **16 passed** on first run (unit + regression/golden + streaming). `dataplane`'s new `streamUpstreamURL` tests: **4 passed**, including the decisive backward-compatibility proof that every non-Gemini provider's URL passes through completely unchanged. New `cmd/gateway` integration tests: **3 passed**, including a real two-turn tool-call round trip whose mock upstream asserts, on the second HTTP call, that the resolved `functionResponse.name` genuinely arrived correct — proving the hazard is handled end-to-end, not just in the adapter's own isolated unit test.

**Bugs found:** Two real regressions caught by the existing test suite itself (not self-caught before running tests) — both were tests that had used `"gemini"` specifically *because* it used to be the honest example of "a provider without streaming support," a premise this pass's own change made false: `cmd/gateway/integration_test.go`'s `TestIntegrationStreamingUnsupportedProviderReturnsBadRequest` and `dataplane/streaming_test.go`'s `TestHandleChatCompletionStreamUnsupportedProviderReturnsTypedError`. Both fixed by switching to `"bedrock"` (the one adapter still genuinely lacking streaming support), confirmed already registered in `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s adapter registry with zero further wiring needed. A third, unrelated pre-existing test (`controlplane/config_test.go`'s `TestLoadExampleConfig`) failed on a hardcoded deployment count after the new `config.example.yaml` entry was added — fixed by updating the count and adding real field assertions for the new entry, not just bumping the number.

**Next steps / resume point:** Ready to commit/push/watch CI. After this ships, the smaller backlog items the same inventory sweep surfaced remain: `evals report --traces` (mirrors `report --scores`), `container.id`/`process.pid` on evals' `Span` (needs a small `sandbox.py` change), and Bedrock's own real-adapter question (a founder call, since it's not named in `PRD.md`'s v1 line the way Gemini was) — none started yet.

## [2026-09-04] `evals` `Span.container_id` + a real DoS-relevant `sandbox.py` timeout bug fix

**Files touched:** No dynamic-workflow research this pass — the mechanism (`docker run --cidfile`) is a single, well-known Docker CLI flag, verified directly and empirically against a real local Docker daemon rather than via a multi-agent research fan-out. Rewrote `evals/evals/rollout/sandbox.py` (real `--cidfile` capture, `SandboxResult.container_id`, real `docker kill` on timeout replacing the CLI-process-only kill). Modified `evals/evals/models.py` (`Span.container_id`), `evals/evals/tracing.py` (`finish_sandbox_span` gains a `container_id` param, sets the real `container.id` OTel attribute), `evals/evals/rollout/scheduler.py` (both `finish_sandbox_span` call sites pass `container_id`). Modified `evals/tests/{test_sandbox_integration.py,test_tracing.py,test_cli_integration.py}`. Updated `THREAT_MODEL.md` (Evals DoS row + Review Cadence), `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (`Span`'s real-shape paragraph), `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Started as the smallest remaining backlog item from the post-Gemini-adapter inventory sweep (`container_id`/`process_pid` on `Span`, deferred out of the original Trace/Span RFC's scope). Investigating the `--cidfile` mechanism directly against a real Docker daemon surfaced something more important: `run_in_sandbox()`'s own docstring claimed "on timeout, the container process is killed," but empirically, killing the local `docker run` CLI process does *not* stop the container — a real, live bug undermining `timeout_s` as an actual resource bound. Fixed both together, since the container-ID capture needed for the `Span` field is exactly the missing piece the timeout fix also needed (`docker kill <id>` requires knowing the real ID).

**Decisions made:** Used `tempfile.mkstemp()` + immediate `os.close`/`os.unlink` to get a guaranteed-unique, guaranteed-nonexistent path for `--cidfile` — confirmed empirically that Docker requires the path to not exist at all (even an empty pre-created file makes `docker run` fail outright with "container ID file found"). `docker kill` on timeout is best-effort (a container that already exited in the small window between timeout-firing and the kill call is not an error). `process_pid` deliberately still excluded from `Span` even though a PID is now technically available (the local CLI process's) — it isn't the containerized command's own process, and attaching it would be a real, honest-sounding-but-wrong value, worse than omitting the field; named explicitly as a considered-and-rejected addition, not a silent gap.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **146 passed, 9 skipped**; `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **154 passed, 1 skipped**, zero orphaned containers left behind afterward, confirmed via `docker ps -a`). The load-bearing new test (`test_run_in_sandbox_timeout_actually_stops_the_container`) asserts the container is genuinely gone via `docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}}'`, not just that the Python call returned — and was sanity-checked by temporarily reverting the fix (`git stash`) and re-running: the reverted code left a real container running (`docker ps` showed it `Up` after the "timed out" Python call had already returned), the new test failed as expected (missing `container_id` attribute), then the fix was restored and re-verified fully green. The real end-to-end CLI path was also proven, not just the unit level: `test_rollout_against_a_real_docker_daemon` now asserts every persisted `Span.container_id` is a real 64-char hex ID.

**Bugs found:** The DoS-relevant timeout bug described above — the main finding of this pass, not a byproduct.

**Next steps / resume point:** `evals report --traces` and Bedrock's own real-adapter question (a founder call) remain from the same inventory sweep, neither started.

## [2026-09-04] `evals report --traces`

**Files touched:** Modified `evals/evals/cli.py` (`report_cmd` gains a third mode; new `_format_span_report` helper), `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (new `_make_span` fixture + 4 new tests; one existing test's exact-string assertion updated for the now-3-way "Provide one of..." usage error).

**Intent/summary:** Explicitly user-directed, despite the original `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-evals-trace-span-model.md`'s own "no consumer needs this yet" deferral — the user chose to build it anyway when asked. Mirrors `report --scores`'s CLI shape (a new mutually-exclusive `--traces <path>` mode) but not its exact reporting semantics, since `Span` has no `scorer_type`-like grouping key and no pass/fail concept in the same sense a `Score.value` has.

**Decisions made:** Labeled the headline metric `ok_rate`, deliberately never `pass_rate` — a `Span`'s `OK`/`ERROR` status measures whether the sandbox execution itself completed without error (an infra-reliability signal), never an eval-quality judgment; reusing `pass_rate`'s wording would have silently conflated the two. Never grouped (unlike `--scores`) — no partition key exists on `Span` the way `scorer_type` does on `Score`; one aggregate line, mirroring `--successes`/`--total`'s own single-line simplicity. Added a genuinely new metric no other `report` mode shows: average sandbox execution duration (`end_time_unix_nano - start_time_unix_nano`), since `Span` is the only entity carrying real wall-clock start/end timestamps. Still Wilson-CI-bearing, per `PRD.md`'s "never a bare percentage" convention applied consistently. Generalized `report_cmd`'s mutual-exclusivity validation from a 2-mode to a true 3-mode check; one pre-existing test's exact-string usage-error assertion ("Provide either" → "Provide one of") updated to match, a deliberate, expected consequence of the message now covering 3 modes, not a regression.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **150 passed, 9 skipped**; `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`. Zero regressions — every pre-existing `report`/`run`/`rollout` test passed unmodified except the one intentionally-updated usage-error string. New tests prove: a real `ok_rate`/CI/`avg_duration_ms` line for a real 2-OK/1-ERROR span set (exact-string-matched, not just substring-checked); an empty traces file fails loudly (`no Spans found`); `--traces` is mutually exclusive with both `--scores` and `--successes`/`--total`.

**Bugs found:** None — small, well-scoped CLI addition, no surprises.

**Next steps / resume point:** Bedrock's real-adapter question remains, now explicitly greenlit by the user (grounding research already launched in parallel with this pass) — pick up separately. PyPI trademark clearance research also launched in parallel.
