## [2026-09-04] Real `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock` adapter — user-directed scope expansion

**Files touched:** Grounding research (4-angle dynamic workflow: API surface, auth/transport, streaming format, tool-use/stop-reasons, plus a synthesis stage) run before writing the RFC — then, before trusting the synthesis, independently re-fetched and read `aws-sdk-go-v2`'s real, current source directly (`service/bedrockruntime/endpoints.go`, `aws/signer/v4/v4.go`, `aws/credentials.go`), catching a real correction to the research's own recommendation (see Decisions). Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-bedrock-adapter.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-bedrock-adapter.md`. Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`DeploymentConfig` +4 fields, provider-conditional `Load()` validation) + `config_test.go` (+5 tests). Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`Deployment` +4 fields; `setUpstreamAuthHeaders` widened to take `ctx`/`body`, return `error`, +`case "bedrock"` real SigV4 signing; `responseUnmarshalers` +`"bedrock"`); new `bedrock_auth_test.go` (+3 tests). Modified `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`buildPipeline`'s credential-resolution branch). Rewrote `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/bedrock.go` (stub → real); new `bedrock_test.go`, `regression_test.go`, `testdata/` (4 fixtures, generated by running the adapter code, not hand-transcribed). Modified `gateway/cmd/gateway/integration_test.go` (+2 new integration tests). Modified `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `gateway/config.example.yaml` (+ real `bedrock` example), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Explicitly user-directed — the user greenlit building a real Bedrock adapter after being told the actionable backlog was exhausted, unlike Gemini's pass, which closed a gap `PRD.md`'s v1 line already named. Targets Amazon Bedrock Runtime's provider-agnostic Converse API, buffered/non-streaming only.

**Decisions made:** A real `Deployment`/config-schema change (`AccessKeyIDEnv`/`SecretAccessKeyEnv`/`SessionTokenEnv`/`Region`) — the first adapter that couldn't fit the existing single-bearer-secret model, since AWS SigV4 genuinely needs a different credential shape. `setUpstreamAuthHeaders` widened to accept the request body and return an error — SigV4 signs over a body hash and the signer call can genuinely fail, unlike every other provider's infallible branch. New minimal dependency: `aws-sdk-go-v2/aws/signer/v4` (+ core `aws` package), not the full service-client SDK. The single most important finding: independently reading `aws-sdk-go-v2`'s own current source (not a docs page) revealed the real SigV4 service-signing name for Bedrock Runtime is `"amazonbedrockfrontendservice"` — confirmed as the unconditional fallback in `service/bedrockruntime/endpoints.go`, since zero per-region `SigningName:` overrides exist anywhere in that file's endpoint table — correcting the grounding research's own recommended `"bedrock"`. Streaming (`ConverseStream`) deliberately scoped out — its real wire format is AWS's binary event-stream framing, not SSE, confirmed against real AWS SDK/docs sources. `toolResult` correlates purely by `toolUseId` with no required `name` field — genuinely simpler than Gemini's `functionResponse.name`-resolution-from-history hazard, named explicitly as a real, favorable difference, not assumed.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `internal/adapter/bedrock`'s own suite: **11 passed** on first run (unit + regression/golden). `dataplane`'s new SigV4 tests: **3 passed**, including the decisive backward-compatibility proof that every non-Bedrock provider's auth-header behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged. `controlplane`'s new provider-conditional-validation tests: **5 passed**. New `cmd/gateway` integration tests: **2 passed**, including asserting a real `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256`-prefixed `Authorization` header and non-empty `X-Amz-Date` genuinely reached the mock upstream — the real, load-bearing proof that signing worked end-to-end, not just in an isolated unit test with no live AWS account to fully verify against.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code — the one real "bug" this pass caught was in the grounding research itself (the wrong SigV4 service-signing name), corrected before any code was written, not after.

**Next steps / resume point:** `ConverseStream`/binary event-stream decoding remains a real, deliberately deferred follow-on. This closes the user's three explicitly-selected next steps (Bedrock adapter, PyPI trademark research, `evals report --traces`) from the prior turn — worth a fresh check-in on priorities.

## [2026-09-04] Real Bedrock `ConverseStream` streaming support — closing the deferred follow-on

**Files touched:** Grounding research (dynamic workflow, task `whniuav42`) — then, before trusting the synthesis, independently re-read `aws-sdk-go-v2`'s real `service/bedrockruntime/{serializers,deserializers}.go` and `aws/protocol/eventstream/{decode,encode}.go` directly, catching a real correction to the task's own initial framing (see Decisions). Created `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-bedrock-converse-stream.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-04-bedrock-converse-stream.md`. New `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/stream.go` (`StreamDecoder`, stateless) + `stream_test.go` (13 tests). Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (extracted `finishStreamedResponse`; added `streamDeploymentBedrock`; dispatch in `streamDeployment`; corrected `ErrStreamingNotSupported`'s doc comment) and `dataplane.go` (`streamUpstreamURL` +`case "bedrock"`; `NewHTTPUpstreamStreamCaller`'s `Accept` header made provider-conditional). New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/bedrock_stream_test.go` (2 tests, real `eventstream.Encoder`-built fixtures) and 2 new `TestStreamUpstreamURLBedrock*` tests in `streamurl_test.go`. Fixed `internal/streaming/types.go`'s doubly-stale `StreamingAdapter` doc comment. Modified `gateway/cmd/gateway/integration_test.go`: removed `TestIntegrationStreamingUnsupportedProviderReturnsBadRequest` + its now-unused `newIntegrationServerWithProvider` helper (both had used `"bedrock"` as their non-streaming example, now invalid); added `newMockBedrockStreamingUpstream` + `TestIntegrationStreamingRequestSucceedsBedrock`. Fixed the same stale-`"bedrock"`-example problem in `internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming_test.go` via a new `nonStreamingAdapter` stub. Modified `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Package Layout's Bedrock block; the tool-call-encoding/streaming-event-shape normalization list), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Closes the follow-on the buffered Bedrock adapter pass explicitly deferred. The user's own generic "keep going" directive was interpreted as authorizing this specific next step — a direct, lower-risk continuation of already-shipped work, chosen over multi-modal content-block support (bigger blast radius across every adapter) and the founder-blocked PyPI trademark question.

**Decisions made:** `bedrock.StreamDecoder` deliberately does not implement `streaming.StreamingAdapter` — ConverseStream's binary wire format isn't SSE, so forcing a shared interface for one binary-framed implementor was rejected; `streamDeployment` instead special-cases `dep.Provider == "bedrock"` and dispatches to a sibling `streamDeploymentBedrock`, mirroring the existing per-provider-string-switch convention. The single most important finding: Bedrock's streaming URL is a **path-segment** swap (`/converse` → `/converse-stream`), not a colon-suffix swap like Gemini's — this corrected the task's own initial framing, not just prior research, confirmed directly against `serializers.go`. Second finding: Bedrock's streaming tool-call arguments are an accumulating JSON-string fragment (matching OpenAI/openaicompat), never a whole object per chunk like Gemini's — despite Bedrock's buffered tool-call arguments being an already-parsed object, a real intra-provider buffered-vs-streaming divergence worth documenting explicitly. `StreamDecoder` is deliberately stateless — every Bedrock event self-describes its own content kind, unlike Anthropic's decoder. `Decode` never returns a `done` signal — `metadata` (usage) is confirmed to arrive after `messageStop`, so the binary loop relies purely on `io.EOF`. Two pre-existing tests that used `"bedrock"` as their "provider without streaming support" example (already updated once, when Gemini shipped) needed fixing again: the dataplane-level one got a new deliberately-non-streaming stub adapter (real, cheap, package-local); the HTTP-level integration test was deleted outright rather than kept alive with a fake fixture, since `buildPipeline`'s registry isn't test-injectable and no real provider is unsupported-for-streaming anymore — modifying production wiring just to preserve one stale test was rejected as backwards.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go test ./...` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure (confirmed unrelated via `go test ./cmd/gateway/... -v | grep FAIL`, only that one test). `internal/adapter/bedrock`'s stream tests: **13 passed** on first run. New `dataplane` tests (URL derivation, full binary sequence, exception frame): **6 passed**, including the full sequence test passing on the first run against a real `eventstream.Encoder`-built fixture. New `cmd/gateway` integration test: **1 passed** on first run, asserting the real `/converse-stream` path and `Accept: application/vnd.amazon.eventstream` header genuinely reached the mock upstream.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code. The two test breaks (both stale `"bedrock"`-as-non-streaming-example fixtures) were an expected, direct consequence of this feature working correctly, not a regression.

**Next steps / resume point:** The actionable, unblocked backlog is exhausted again — remaining known items are multi-modal content-block support (real but bigger blast radius across every adapter) and the founder-blocked PyPI trademark question. Worth a fresh check-in on priorities.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 1, item 1: real evals sandbox `--read-only`/`--tmpfs` hardening

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/rollout/sandbox.py` (module docstring, `run_in_sandbox`'s own docstring, `docker_args` gained `--read-only` + `--tmpfs=/tmp:rw,exec,nosuid,size=64m`). `evals/tests/test_sandbox_integration.py` (+2 tests: `test_run_in_sandbox_root_filesystem_is_read_only`, `test_run_in_sandbox_tmp_is_still_writable`). `THREAT_MODEL.md` (Evals Information Disclosure row updated from "Partial" naming this as unbuilt, to naming it real; new Review Cadence & Change Log entry). `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** First item of Tier 1 in the user-approved, value-ranked backlog list — closes the exact gap `THREAT_MODEL.md` already named by name ("Docker's own default `--rm` per-container writable-layer lifecycle applies, but this is Docker's default semantics, not a Kelvran-built ephemeral-filesystem guarantee (no `--read-only`/`--tmpfs`)").

**Decisions made:** `--read-only` alone would break any real task command that writes a scratch file (a common, ordinary thing for a sandboxed command to do, not an edge case) — paired with a `--tmpfs=/tmp` mount so ordinary temp-file usage keeps working while nothing persists past the container's lifetime, matching standard Docker security guidance for exactly this "immutable root + writable scratch" pattern. Sized the tmpfs at 64m and added `exec,nosuid` mount options — permissive enough not to break real usage, without being unbounded. Did not attempt to determine or special-case additional writable paths beyond `/tmp` for hypothetical future task commands — YAGNI; if a real task genuinely needs another writable mount, that's a concrete, evidence-based follow-up, not something to guess at now.

**Verification performed:** `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **7 passed** (5 pre-existing + 2 new), first run, no fixture changes needed. Full suite against a real Docker daemon: `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/ -v` → **160 passed, 1 skipped** (only the live-LLM-key-gated test remains skipped) — zero regressions, including the real end-to-end `test_rollout_against_a_real_docker_daemon` and `test_run_suite_against_a_real_docker_daemon` CLI-level tests. Default (non-Docker) gate: `uv run pytest tests/` → **150 passed, 11 skipped** (up from 9 — the 2 new tests are correctly Docker-gated too). `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`.

**Bugs found:** None — a clean, additive hardening change with zero interaction with any other existing behavior.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue Tier 1: `go-arch-lint`/`.importlinter` CI wiring, `otelhttp` middleware, a second (OpenAI) LLM-judge provider, per-exception-type Bedrock streaming errors.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 1, item 2: real `go-arch-lint` dependency-direction enforcement

**Files touched:** New `gateway/.go-arch-lint.yml`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new `go-arch-lint` step in the `gateway` job, before `build + test`). `Makefile` (`lint-gateway` target gained `&& go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check`). `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Dependency direction rules section — corrected 2 stale package names, rewritten to reflect the real, verified graph, "not actually wired" line updated to say where it's now wired). `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Third item of the user-approved, value-ranked Tier 1 backlog — closes the exact gap `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` had already named by name as unwired.

**Decisions made:** Before writing any config, built the real import graph directly (`grep -rlE '"github.com/kelvran/gateway/gateway/internal/'` across every non-test `.go` file, then per-file `grep` to attribute each cross-package import) rather than trusting `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own existing rules table — this caught 2 real doc-vs-code staleness instances already sitting in that exact table (`provideradapter`, never a real package — the real one is `internal/adapter`; a bare `gateway` component — the real packages are `internal/gateway/dataplane`/`internal/gateway/controlplane`) and one place the doc's own prior wording was ambiguous in a way reality resolves cleanly (`cache` has literally zero internal cross-package imports, a stricter leaf than "cache → {identity, telemetry, costaccounting}" could be read to imply). `.go-arch-lint.yml`'s workdir is `internal` (matching the real fe3dback upstream config convention, read directly from the tool's own repo in `$GOMODCACHE`, not guessed), with an explicit `../api/gatewayevents/v1` component added once `check` surfaced that `dataplane.go` genuinely imports the generated proto package from outside `internal/` — a real dependency this pass's first draft had missed, not a false positive. Two real go-arch-lint subtleties discovered only by reading its own source and iterating against real check output (not from documentation, which doesn't cover these): (1) declaring a leaf component with an explicit-but-empty `mayDependOn: []` produces a benign self-inspection notice whose presence causes `operation.go` to SKIP the real dependency check entirely (`if len(spec.Integrity.DocumentNotices) == 0 { result, err = o.specChecker.Check(...) }`) — the correct way to declare "depends on nothing" is to omit the component from `deps:` altogether, which `checker_imports.go`'s `AllowedProjectImports` defaults to empty (deny-all) for; (2) the tool grants no implicit same-component self-import right between two Go packages lumped under one declared component name — `cache`'s 3 real implementations (`inprocess`/`grpcserver`/`grpcclient`) needed 3 separate component declarations, each with an explicit `mayDependOn: [cache]`, not one shared `cache` component covering all 4 directories.

**Verification performed:** `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean, `OK - No warnings found`, `OmittedCount: 0` (every scanned file matched exactly one component). Sanity-checked the config isn't vacuously passing: temporarily injected a real illegal import (`internal/identity` → `internal/gateway/dataplane`) and confirmed `check` flagged it with the exact file/line (`identity.go:13`) before reverting and re-confirming a clean pass. Full gateway verification: `go build ./... && go vet ./... && golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`; `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure.

**Bugs found:** 2 real doc-vs-code staleness instances in `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own dependency-direction rules table (see Decisions above) — both fixed in this pass, not left for a future sweep.

**Files touched:** New `evals/.importlinter`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new `import-linter` step in the `evals` job, before `sync + test`, mirroring the existing `uvx ruff check .` step). `Makefile` (`lint-evals` target gained `&& uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports`). `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Package Layout's `.importlinter` line — corrected from "not built" to describing the real config). `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Fourth item of the user-approved, value-ranked Tier 1 backlog — the Python-side analogue of the `go-arch-lint` wiring just shipped for `gateway`, closing the exact gap `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` had already named as unbuilt.

**Decisions made:** Built the real import graph directly (`grep -rn "^from evals\|^import evals" evals --include="*.py"`) before writing any config, exactly like the `go-arch-lint` pass — confirmed a genuinely simple, 4-rank hierarchy: `cli` (top) → `rollout.scheduler`/`ingestion.decode` → `tracing`/`results_store`/`rollout.cache`/`rollout.sandbox`/`judge.*` → `models`/`stats` (bottom, pure leaves). Added 2 `independence` contracts beyond the base `layers` contract for boundaries worth guaranteeing explicitly: `judge`'s 3 scoring strategies (deterministic/llm_judge/providers) are conceptually swappable alternatives that should never couple to each other, and `rollout.cache`/`rollout.sandbox` are likewise meant to stay decoupled utilities. `evals/contracts/` (generated protobuf stubs) is deliberately excluded from every contract, discovered empirically rather than assumed: it has no `__init__.py` anywhere in the tree (a PEP 420 implicit namespace package), and `import-linter`'s `layers` contract genuinely cannot resolve it as a declarable layer — both `evals.contracts` and the deepest real leaf `evals.contracts.gatewayevents.v1.gatewayevents_pb2` failed with "module does not exist" against the actually-installed package before this was diagnosed and the entry dropped; no layering constraint makes sense for generated code nobody hand-edits anyway. Invoked via `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` rather than adding `import-linter` to `pyproject.toml`'s `dependency-groups.dev` — matches this project's own existing precedent for ruff (a CI-time-only static checker invoked via `uvx`, never a runtime or even dev dependency).

**Verification performed:** `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → `Contracts: 3 kept, 0 broken` against the real, unmodified codebase (16 files, 14 dependencies analyzed). Sanity-checked the config isn't vacuously passing: temporarily injected a real illegal import (`evals.models` → `evals.rollout.scheduler`, a leaf importing something several ranks above it) and confirmed the `layers` contract broke with the exact chain (`evals.models -> evals.rollout.scheduler (l.17)`) before reverting and re-confirming a clean pass. Full evals verification: `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`; `uv run pytest tests/` → **150 passed, 11 skipped**, zero regressions (this pass touches zero `evals/evals/*.py` source, only a new config file and docs).

**Decisions made:** `wrapHTTPServerSpan` uses no explicit `otelhttp.With*` option, relying entirely on the global `TracerProvider`/`TextMapPropagator` `telemetry.Init` already installs before `run()` constructs the mux — confirmed via reading `otelhttp`'s own `newConfig` source that this is the real, intended default (`Propagators: otel.GetTextMapPropagator()`, tracer resolved lazily per-request otherwise), not an assumption. The new integration test deliberately does NOT modify the existing `newIntegrationServer` helper (which every other integration test in the package uses and which asserts exact span counts in at least one place) — instead a new sibling helper, `newIntegrationServerWithOtelHTTP`, mirrors its exact pipeline/config construction but additionally wraps the mux exactly the way `run()` does, keeping this addition purely additive with zero risk of silently changing any other test's span-count assumptions. Proved real parent/child nesting, not merely "2 spans happen to exist": asserted the inner GenAI span's real `ParentSpanID` equals the outer otelhttp span's own `SpanID`, and both share one `TraceID` — otelhttp's own documented behavior (its span ends only after the wrapped handler returns) means the inner span appears first in the recorder's `Ended()` order, which the test relies on explicitly rather than assuming array order. Found and fixed one real, adjacent doc-vs-code staleness instance in `main.go`'s own package doc comment while touching the same lines (not a separate pass): it claimed streaming was "wired for the OpenAI and Anthropic adapters only," already false since Gemini/openaicompat/Bedrock all shipped real streaming earlier this session.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 1, item 5: second (OpenAI) LLM-judge provider for evals

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` (module docstring updated; `_compute_cost_usd` renamed to `_compute_anthropic_cost_usd`; new `OPENAI_DEFAULT_JUDGE_MODEL`, `_OPENAI_MODEL_PRICE_PER_MTOK_USD`, `_compute_openai_cost_usd`, `_OpenAICallModel`, `make_openai_call_model`). `evals/tests/test_providers.py` (imports updated for the rename; +8 new tests mirroring the Anthropic tests' exact structure). `evals/pyproject.toml` (+`openai>=3.8.0`, via `uv add`). `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Tech Stack's Judge SDKs row). `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-evals-llm-judge-provider-wiring.md` (Unresolved Questions — the "is a second provider worth adding" line updated from open question to "shipped, exactly as predicted"). `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Sixth item of the user-approved, value-ranked Tier 1 backlog — the RFC itself had already named this as a ready, unblocked follow-on ("`call_model`'s existing DI shape means it's a same-shaped follow-on, not a blocked one"), so this pass exists to actually build the function the RFC predicted, not to design anything new.

**Decisions made:** Before writing any code, confirmed the real, installed `openai==3.8.0` SDK's actual shape via `inspect.signature`/`model_fields` (`AsyncOpenAI.__init__`, `chat.completions.create`'s real params, `ChatCompletion`/`CompletionUsage`/`Choice`/`ChatCompletionMessage`'s real field names) — mirroring this project's own established discipline for the Anthropic provider, not assumed from memory of the OpenAI API shape. This caught two real, load-bearing differences from the Anthropic path: OpenAI's usage fields are `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens` (not `input_tokens`/`output_tokens`), and both `ChatCompletion.usage` and `Choice.message.content` are genuinely `Optional` in the real Pydantic model (Anthropic's equivalent fields are not) — handled explicitly rather than assumed always-present: missing usage produces an honest `None` cost (never a fabricated `0` or a crash), and `None` content raises a real `ValueError` since `judge()` cannot score an empty verdict. Live-fetched OpenAI's current `gpt-4o-mini` pricing ($0.15/MTok input, $0.60/MTok output) from `developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing` (the real redirect target of `platform.openai.com/docs/pricing`) rather than trusting a remembered figure, matching the existing Anthropic pricing constant's own "live-fetched, re-verify before production" convention. Renamed `_compute_cost_usd` to `_compute_anthropic_cost_usd` for symmetry now that a second provider-specific cost function exists — a pure rename, confirmed via the full test suite passing unmodified except for the two call-site updates the rename itself required. Deliberately did NOT wire a `--judge-provider` CLI flag into `cli.py` — the RFC's own Unresolved Questions line asked only "is a second provider ever worth adding," never for a provider-selection CLI surface, and building one nobody asked for would be scope creep beyond what this ranked backlog item actually named.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 1, item 6 (final): per-exception-type Bedrock streaming errors

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/stream.go` (new sentinel errors `ErrBedrockThrottled`/`ErrBedrockValidation`/`ErrBedrockServiceUnavailable`/`ErrBedrockInternalServer`/`ErrBedrockModelStreamError`; new `bedrockStreamExceptionError` helper; `Decode`'s message-type check now branches on `ExceptionMessageType` specifically before falling back to the old generic message for the `ErrorMessageType` case). `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/stream_test.go` (`TestDecodeExceptionMessageTypeReturnsError` replaced by `TestDecodeExceptionTypesMapToTypedSentinels` (5 sub-tests, one per real exception type) + `TestDecodeUnrecognizedExceptionTypeStillReturnsRealError` + `TestDecodeGenericErrorMessageTypeReturnsError`). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/bedrock_stream_test.go` (`TestHandleChatCompletionStreamBedrockExceptionFrameSurfacesAsError`'s assertion strengthened from a string-contains check to `errors.Is`). `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-bedrock-converse-stream.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Sixth and final item of the user-approved, value-ranked Tier 1 backlog — closes the exact follow-on `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-bedrock-converse-stream.md`'s Unresolved Questions had already named and deferred as "the honest, correct-if-blunt first pass."

**Decisions made:** Read `aws-sdk-go-v2`'s own `deserializers.go` directly (`awsRestjson1_deserializeEventStreamExceptionConverseStreamOutput`, the ConverseStream-specific dispatch function, not the two sibling functions for `InvokeModelWithBidirectionalStream`/`InvokeModelWithResponseStream`) to confirm the real, exact exception-type set ConverseStream itself can send: `internalServerException`, `modelStreamErrorException`, `serviceUnavailableException`, `throttlingException`, `validationException` — 5 values, not 6. The two sibling functions also dispatch on a 6th, `modelTimeoutException`, which ConverseStream's own function does not — a real, load-bearing distinction that would have been a plausible-sounding but wrong guess had this been assumed rather than verified against the real source. Each sentinel is a package-level `errors.New` value (mirroring `dataplane.go`'s own existing `ErrRateLimited`/`ErrBudgetExceeded`/etc. sentinel convention), wrapped via `%w` so `errors.Is()` works through any additional wrapping a caller adds. Kept the pre-existing generic-message fallback for AWS's separate `"error"` message-type (which carries no `:exception-type` header at all, per the real eventstream framing `eventstreamapi.ErrorMessageType` constant) — no sentinel is possible there, so it deliberately isn't forced into one. An unrecognized future exception type (AWS could add a 6th to ConverseStream later) still returns a real, informative error, just not a typed sentinel — forward-compatible by design, proven by a dedicated test.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `internal/adapter/bedrock`'s stream tests: all pass, including the new 5-way table-driven sentinel test and the 2 new forward-compat/generic-error tests. `internal/gateway/dataplane`'s Bedrock tests: all pass with the strengthened `errors.Is` assertion. Full suite: `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure. `evals` untouched by this pass.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code. Two pre-existing tests broke as a direct, expected consequence of the new sentinel-wrapped error messages no longer containing the literal substring `"exception"` — both fixed by strengthening their assertions to `errors.Is()` against the specific sentinel, not just restoring the old, weaker string-contains check.

**Next steps / resume point:** Tier 1 of the ranked backlog audit is now fully closed (all 6 items shipped). Tier 2 (medium-effort, no-external-blocker items: graceful SIGTERM shutdown, rolling-window budget reset, Score-level judge caching + real SPRT, admin API v1 slice, `rubric_axis`/multi-axis judge scoring, bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Vertex AI OAuth2 auth, Cache L2 extra normalization ops) is next — worth a fresh check-in with the user on priorities/pacing before continuing into a heavier tier.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 2, item 1: real graceful shutdown for cmd/gateway

**Files touched:** `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`run()` rewritten: `net.Listen`+`server.Serve` instead of `http.ListenAndServe`; new `signal.NotifyContext`-driven select loop; new `gracefulShutdownTimeout` constant; comments on the two existing defers updated from "best-effort" to "real on every exit path"). New `gateway/cmd/gateway/graceful_shutdown_integration_test.go` (`TestIntegrationGracefulShutdownDrainsInFlightRequestBeforeExiting`, `waitForListener`, `writeGracefulShutdownTestConfig`, `gracefulShutdownTestSecret`). `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-otel-tracing-agent-run-id.md` (Drawbacks + Unresolved Questions, both updated from "deferred" to "real, here's what shipped"). `docs/rfcs/2026-09-03-budget-persistence.md` (one adjacent stale caveat corrected). `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** First item of Tier 2 in the user-approved, value-ranked backlog — closes a gap the OTel RFC's own Drawbacks section had already named as "a real gap for `cmd/gateway` as a whole... fixing it is bigger than this RFC's scope," now sized and shipped as its own standalone unit.

**Decisions made:** Used the real, standard Go idiom confirmed via `go doc` before writing any code (`os/signal.NotifyContext`, `http.Server.Shutdown`, `http.ErrServerClosed`) rather than assumed from memory. Switched `http.ListenAndServe` to explicit `net.Listen` + `server.Serve(listener)` as a deliberate side effect — functionally identical in production, but the real reason was testability: `ListenAndServe` binds internally with no way for a caller to learn the resolved address, while `Serve` accepts a caller-provided listener. In the end the test still uses a fixed, distinctive port (`127.0.0.1:18761`) rather than `:0`, since `run()`'s signature intentionally wasn't changed to expose the bound address — a small, accepted tradeoff (rare port-collision risk in a sequential CI job) favored over widening `run()`'s contract just for one test. Fixed the drain timeout at 30s as a plain constant, not a config field — no request in this codebase's own test suite runs anywhere near that long, and a knob nobody has asked for yet would be premature (YAGNI). The new integration test self-signals the current test process via `syscall.Kill(os.Getpid(), syscall.SIGTERM)` — confirmed this is the same, legitimate technique Go's own `os/signal` package test suite uses for testing real signal delivery, not an unusual or risky hack — since `run()`'s `signal.NotifyContext` call registers process-wide, a real signal delivered to the test's own process is indistinguishable from one a real orchestrator (systemd/Kubernetes/Docker) would send. `run()` had zero prior test coverage of any kind (every other integration test in this package calls `buildPipeline` directly and builds its own `httptest.Server`, deliberately bypassing `run()`'s own wiring) — this is the first test to exercise the real function, requiring a real temp YAML config file on disk since `run()`'s signature takes a file path, not a `Config` struct.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. New test passes on first run, and again under `-race`. Sanity-checked it isn't vacuous: temporarily reverted `run()` to the old bare-`ListenAndServe` body (removing the now-unused `os/signal`/`syscall` imports to keep the revert compiling) and re-ran the same test — the real SIGTERM killed the entire test process outright (`signal: terminated`, the test binary itself exiting), the exact abrupt-exit failure mode this pass eliminates, not a hypothetical — then restored the real implementation and re-confirmed it passes. Full suite: `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure. `evals` untouched by this pass.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code. Found and fixed one adjacent, related doc-vs-code staleness instance in `docs/rfcs/2026-09-03-budget-persistence.md`, which repeated the same now-stale "best-effort, not a real SIGTERM" caveat about `pipeline.Close()` that this pass makes untrue.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue Tier 2: rolling-window budget reset, Score-level judge caching + real SPRT, an admin API v1 slice, `rubric_axis`/multi-axis judge scoring, bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Vertex AI OAuth2 auth, Cache L2 extra normalization ops.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 2, item 2: real rolling-window budget reset

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/budget/budget.go` (package doc comment; `Tracker` gained `periodStart map[string]time.Time` + `now func() time.Time`; new `resetIfNeeded`/`persistZeroIfStoreConfigured`; `Allow`/`SpentUSD`/`Record` all widened with a `resetInterval time.Duration` parameter). New `gateway/internal/budget/reset_test.go` (8 tests + `fakeClock`). `gateway/internal/identity/identity.go` (`VirtualKey` +`BudgetResetInterval time.Duration`). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`VirtualKeyConfig` +`BudgetResetIntervalSeconds int`, parsed via the existing `getInt` helper). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go` (+2 new tests; `TestLoadExampleConfig` gained a real assertion for the new field). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`buildPipeline` wires `BudgetResetIntervalSeconds` × `time.Second` into `BudgetResetInterval`). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go`, `streaming.go` (the 3 real `Allow`/`SpentUSD`/`Record` call sites, all threading `vk.BudgetResetInterval` through). `gateway/config.example.yaml` (a documented example on `team-alpha`). `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-virtual-keys-budgets.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`. Mechanically fixed ~45 pre-existing test call sites across `internal/budget/budget_test.go`, `persistence_test.go`, `internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming_test.go`, `dataplane_test.go`, `gatewayevents_test.go` to append the new parameter.

**Intent/summary:** Second item of Tier 2 in the user-approved, value-ranked backlog — closes the exact gap `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-virtual-keys-budgets.md`'s own Unresolved Questions had already named ("no rolling-window budget reset... no scheduler and no persistence to reset against yet"), now that budget persistence (the prerequisite the backlog audit itself flagged) has shipped.

**Decisions made:** Deliberately lazy (checked on `Allow`/`SpentUSD`/`Record` access, not a background ticker) — read `internal/ratelimit.TokenBucket`'s own real lazy-refill implementation first and mirrored its exact pattern (an injectable `now func() time.Time` clock, overridden directly by white-box tests rather than a new exported `WithClock` constructor, since `budget`'s own test files are already `package budget`). Chose a plain int-seconds config field (`BudgetResetIntervalSeconds`) over a duration string, matching this file's own existing `TTLSeconds` convention (`CacheConfig`/`CacheL2Config`/`CacheL3Config`) rather than introducing a new style. The one real, deliberate scope-narrowing decision: the reset window's own boundary (`periodStart`) is NOT itself persisted to the `Store` — only cumulative spend is, matching the budget-persistence RFC's real, already-shipped scope exactly, rather than widening the `Store` interface (which would have turned a "medium" item into its own mini-RFC). Named the resulting gap explicitly rather than hiding it: a restart resets a key's reset-window clock to the restart moment, extending that one window by at most the configured interval once — self-limiting, and not a new bypass, since a store-less `Tracker` already loses all budget state on every restart today. A second real decision found only by tracing the exact call order in `dataplane.go` (`SpentUSD` is called immediately before `Allow`, for the `budget_spent_usd` gatewayevents field): if only `Allow`/`Record` reset but `SpentUSD` didn't, the one request that crosses a reset boundary would report a stale pre-reset total in that field even though `Allow` right after it would already be evaluating a freshly-reset budget — so `SpentUSD` needed the exact same `resetIfNeeded` treatment, not just Allow/Record. Since `Allow`/`SpentUSD` otherwise never persist (by design, for read-path speed), a reset they trigger still needs to reach the `Store` somehow — added `persistZeroIfStoreConfigured` for exactly that narrow case, while `Record` needed no equivalent since its own existing persist-the-final-total call already reflects any reset that just happened. Fixed ~45 pre-existing test call sites via a small Python script that tracks paren-depth to find each call's own true closing paren (correctly skipping over nested `d("...")` argument calls) rather than a blind regex — this project's own established caution after a prior blind-`replace_all` mistake earlier in this session's history.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `internal/budget`'s full suite (32 tests, including all 8 new ones) passes on first run. Sanity-checked 6 of the 8 new tests are genuinely load-bearing: temporarily short-circuited `resetIfNeeded` to always return `false` and confirmed exactly the tests that exercise real reset behavior failed (with the exact expected wrong values — e.g. "SpentUSD after the window elapsed = 10, want 0"), while the two tests about non-reset behavior correctly still passed, before restoring. New config-parsing tests pass; `TestLoadExampleConfig`'s new assertion passes against the real, updated `config.example.yaml`. Full suite: `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` rootless-Docker failure. `evals` untouched by this pass.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code — a clean, additive feature (every new parameter defaults to 0, preserving prior behavior exactly at every pre-existing call site).

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue Tier 2: Score-level judge caching + real SPRT, an admin API v1 slice, `rubric_axis`/multi-axis judge scoring, bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Vertex AI OAuth2 auth, Cache L2 extra normalization ops.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 2, item 3: real Score-level (LLM-judge) result caching

**Files touched:** New `evals/evals/judge/cache.py` (`compute_score_cache_key`). New `evals/tests/test_judge_cache.py` (5 tests). `evals/evals/models.py` (`Score` +`score_cache_key`/`from_cache`). `evals/evals/cli.py` (new `_JudgeOutcome` dataclass, `_judge_with_cache` shared async helper, `_load_cached_scores`; `_judge_output`/`_judge_case` rewritten around the shared helper; `_score_and_record` (`rollout_cmd`) rewritten to call `_judge_with_cache` instead of `judge()` directly; new `--use-score-cache` flag on both `run_cmd`/`rollout_cmd`). `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (+3 tests). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-evals-score-cache.md`. `THREAT_MODEL.md` (Evals DoS row — one of its 3 "still not built" items closed), `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Third item of Tier 2 in the user-approved, value-ranked backlog — closes one of the three items `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Evals Denial-of-Service row had explicitly named as still not built after Run-level caching/early-stopping shipped (per-tier calibrated defaults and real SPRT are the two that remain, both genuinely needing separate design decisions this pass deliberately doesn't make).

**Decisions made:** Cache key is `(output, reference, scorer_id)` — the actual judged CONTENT, not `(eval_case_id, revision, scorer_id)` — deliberately, since caching on case identity alone would silently serve a stale verdict if a real rollout's sandbox output legitimately changed between invocations (flaky command, non-deterministic model output). `Score` gains `score_cache_key`/`from_cache`, built by directly mirroring `Run.cache_key`/`Run.from_cache`'s own already-established design (computed unconditionally for every real `llm_judge` score, so a *later* cached invocation can hit against an *earlier*, non-caching invocation's output — the same reasoning `Run.cache_key`'s own doc comment already gives). Never touched `judge()`/`llm_judge.py` — per this project's own repeatedly-reaffirmed invariant (first stated in the provider-wiring RFC, reaffirmed in the Score-model RFC), caching is a `cli.py`-level policy layered on the existing `call_model` DI seam, not a change to the judge's own zero-network-awareness contract. The one real design subtlety, found by tracing `rollout_cmd`'s exact call graph before writing any code: `_score_and_record` can be invoked from *inside* `run_suite`'s own already-running event loop when early-stopping is active, and the pre-existing `_judge_output`/`_judge_case` helpers drive their own internal `asyncio.run()` — calling either from that context would raise "cannot be called from a running event loop" (a real bug this project's own test suite already caught once, for the direct `await judge(...)` call this pass's `_judge_with_cache` now replaces). Solved by making the new shared helper a plain `async def`, awaited directly by both callers — `run_cmd` wraps it in its OWN `asyncio.run()` at its own call site (safe, since `run_cmd` never runs inside an event loop), while `rollout_cmd` awaits it directly, exactly mirroring the constraint the pre-existing code comment already documented for the code being replaced.

**Verification performed:** `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`; `.venv/bin/python -c "import evals.cli"` clean. `uv run pytest tests/test_judge_cache.py -v` → **5 passed**, first run. `uv run pytest tests/test_cli_integration.py -k score_cache -v` → **3 passed**, first run (both `run`/`rollout` prove zero new judge calls on a cache-hit second invocation; a third proves omitting the flag re-judges every time). Full suite: `uv run pytest tests/` → **166 passed, 11 skipped** (up from 158 passed — zero regressions, skip count unchanged). `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → still `3 kept, 0 broken` (17 files now, up from 16 — the new `judge/cache.py` module was picked up and caused no layering violations). Separately confirmed against a real Docker daemon: `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **65 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed. Sanity-checked the new tests aren't vacuous: temporarily short-circuited the cache-lookup line to always return `None` and confirmed exactly the 2 hit-detecting tests failed with the precise wrong call counts (`assert 4 == 2`), then restored and re-confirmed the full suite green.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code — a clean, additive feature layered entirely on top of `cli.py`'s existing DI seams, with zero changes to `judge()`/`llm_judge.py`/`Run`/the Rollout Scheduler itself.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue Tier 2: real SPRT/anytime-valid sequential testing, an admin API v1 slice, `rubric_axis`/multi-axis judge scoring, bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Vertex AI OAuth2 auth, Cache L2 extra normalization ops.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 2, item 4: multi-axis LLM-judge scoring

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/judge/llm_judge.py` (new `_JUDGE_PROMPT_TEMPLATE_WITH_AXIS`; `build_judge_prompt`/`judge()` gain optional `axis: str | None = None`). `evals/tests/test_llm_judge.py` (+4 tests). `evals/evals/judge/cache.py` (`compute_score_cache_key` gains optional `axis` param, conditionally included in the canonical JSON). `evals/tests/test_judge_cache.py` (+3 tests). `evals/evals/cli.py` (`_JudgeOutcome` +`axis` field; `_judge_with_cache` threads `axis` through; new `_parse_judge_axes`/`_judge_all_axes`; `_judge_case`/`run_cmd` and `_score_and_record`/`rollout_cmd` both rewritten around `_judge_all_axes`; new `--judge-axes` flag on both commands). `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (+6 tests). `evals/evals/models.py` (`Score.rubric_axis`'s docstring corrected — no longer "stays permanently `None` in v1"). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-evals-multi-axis-judging.md`. `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Fourth item of Tier 2 — the first of the three items resolved via an explicit brainstorming check-in (`AskUserQuestion`) rather than a unilateral choice, since the call-structure decision (one call per axis vs. one structured multi-axis call) is a genuine product trade-off, not a bug fix. The user chose one-call-per-axis over the recommended single-call design.

**Decisions made:** `llm_judge.py`/`judge()` gain the `axis` parameter directly, rather than layering axis-scoping onto `cli.py` (the pattern every prior RFC touching `evals` used for provider-wiring/caching) — because axis-scoping genuinely changes what's being asked of the judge (the prompt content itself), unlike provider selection or caching, which are call-site/memoization concerns that correctly stayed out of this module. `axis=None` reproduces the original `_JUDGE_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` byte-for-byte via a sibling template rather than parameterizing the one template with conditionals, so the existing template's text is provably untouched (`test_build_judge_prompt_with_no_axis_is_byte_identical_to_before`). `compute_score_cache_key`'s `axis` field is added to its canonical JSON dict only when not `None` — an early draft that always included it (as `null` when absent) would have silently changed the hash for every pre-existing call and invalidated every already-persisted `score_cache_key`; caught before any test was run by re-checking the draft against its own docstring's backward-compatibility claim, then fixed and proven with a test that independently recomputes the original 3-field hash by hand. Case-level pass/fail is AND across every configured axis (a response failing even one axis is not a passing response); any single axis's judge-call failure fails the whole case as `JUDGE_ERROR` with no partial per-axis `Score`s persisted — both chosen to mirror the pre-existing single-axis all-or-nothing semantics rather than inventing a new partial-failure state nobody asked for. `JudgeResult` itself gains no `axis` field — the caller already knows which axis it requested and is responsible for recording it, avoiding a second source of truth for the same fact.

**Verification performed:** `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!` (after fixing 2 line-length violations from the new per-axis echo lines). `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q` → **177 passed, 11 skipped** (up from 173 — zero regressions). `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → still `3 kept, 0 broken`. Separately confirmed against a real Docker daemon: `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **69 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed. Sanity-checked by temporarily replacing both `run_cmd`'s and `rollout_cmd`'s `passed = all(o.passed for o in outcomes)` with `passed = outcomes[0].passed`: the `rollout` AND-semantics test's original fixture data had its first axis already failing, so the broken implementation happened to produce the same (correct-looking) result — an accidental false-negative in the sanity check itself, caught by inspecting the "PASSED" result rather than trusting it, fixed by reordering the fixture so the first axis passes and a later axis fails, after which both the `run` and `rollout` AND-semantics tests failed for the exact right reason, then both lines were restored and the full suite re-confirmed green.

**Bugs found:** One, self-caught before shipping (the `compute_score_cache_key` backward-compatibility bug described above). One in the *test suite itself*, also self-caught: the first version of the `rollout` sanity-check's fixture data didn't actually distinguish the AND-semantics implementation from a "just use the first axis" bug, since both axes for the failing case were ordered fail-then-anything; fixed by reordering to pass-then-fail, which is now a real discriminating test.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue Tier 2: real SPRT/anytime-valid sequential testing, an admin API v1 slice, bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Vertex AI OAuth2 auth (deprioritized per the brainstorming check-in — "not needed right now"), Cache L2 extra normalization ops.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 2, item 5: Admin API v1 slice (`internal/admin`)

**Files touched:** New `gateway/internal/admin/admin.go` (`Handler`, auth middleware, GET/POST/DELETE handlers), `admin_test.go` (9 tests). `gateway/internal/identity/identity.go` (`Verifier.Keys()`), `identity_test.go` (+1 test). `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (`KeyLimiter` gains a `sync.RWMutex` + `Register`; `Allow` now denies rather than panics on an unregistered key), `limiter_test.go` (+4 tests). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`Pipeline.verifier` → `atomic.Pointer[identity.Verifier]`; new `UpsertVirtualKey`/`DeleteVirtualKey`, `ErrCannotDeleteLastVirtualKey`, `ErrVirtualKeyNotFound`), new `virtualkey_admin_test.go` (6 tests). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`AdminConfig`, parsed under `admin:`), `config_test.go` (+2 tests). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`run()` starts a second, separate `*http.Server` for the admin surface when `cfg.Admin.TokenEnv` is set; `defaultAdminListenAddr`), new `admin_integration_test.go` (2 real end-to-end tests). `gateway/.go-arch-lint.yml` (`admin` component). `gateway/config.example.yaml` (commented-out `admin:` example). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-admin-api.md`. `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-virtual-keys-budgets.md`, `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Fifth item of Tier 2, and the second of three items resolved via the user's own brainstorming check-in ("ship the recommended v1 slice") — closes the exact gap `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-virtual-keys-budgets.md`'s own Unresolved Questions had already named. Scope deliberately narrow, per the backlog plan's own recommendation: read-only config introspection, plus exactly one config section made live-mutable (virtual keys).

**Decisions made:** `identity.Verifier` stays immutable — a mutation builds a whole new `*Verifier` (current keys via the new `Keys()` accessor, plus the one change) and atomically swaps `dataplane.Pipeline`'s `atomic.Pointer[identity.Verifier]`, rather than making `Verifier` itself mutable with internal locking. Reused `identity.NewVerifier`'s own existing "at least one key required" error as the last-key-deletion guard instead of a second bespoke check. The most significant finding this pass: tracing `checkRateLimit`'s real call (`p.limiter.Allow(ctx, vk.ID)`, no burst/refill params) into `KeyLimiter.Allow` surfaced that an ID with no pre-built bucket dereferences a nil `*TokenBucket`'s mutex and panics (in-memory mode) or silently zero-rate-limits (Redis mode) — a real, previously-unreachable hazard, since `identity.Verifier` and `KeyLimiter` were always built from the same config in lockstep before this feature existed to make them diverge. Fixed with a new `KeyLimiter.Register`, called BEFORE the Verifier swap in `UpsertVirtualKey` so the ordering itself closes the race window, not just the individual pieces. Deliberately did NOT make `KeyLimiter.Allow` lazily self-register an unknown key with a guessed default — that would silently apply the wrong rate limit rather than loudly requiring the real, admin-supplied one. Admin auth is a hard-separate credential space (never delegates to `identity.Verifier`), served on its own `net.Listener` (never the client-facing mux) defaulting to loopback-only, with a hard startup failure if the configured token env var resolves empty — treating "start an unauthenticated admin surface" as categorically unacceptable, not a degraded-but-tolerable state the way a missing deployment API key is.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues` (fixed 3 `errcheck` + 1 `errorlint` findings from the new test/prod code before this). `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean, with the new `admin` component's `mayDependOn: [controlplane, dataplane, identity, ratelimit]` added. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` failure (confirmed unrelated, reproduces identically on unmodified `main`). 26 new tests total. Sanity-checked twice: (1) forced `Register` to ignore the real configured capacity (`ratelimit.KeyConfig{ID: rateLimit.ID}`, dropping burst/refill) — the two tests asserting real capacity/refill behavior failed with `dataplane: rate limit exceeded` on a key that should have had full capacity, then restored and re-confirmed green; (2) deliberately checked whether the sanity-check fixture data itself was discriminating (per the caution multi-axis judging's own sanity-check had already surfaced earlier the same day) before trusting the pass.

**Bugs found:** One real, previously-latent hazard in pre-existing code, found and fixed as a direct consequence of this feature (not present before, since nothing could make `identity`/`ratelimit` diverge until now) — the nil-`*TokenBucket`-panic/zero-capacity hazard described above.

**Next steps / resume point:** One item remains from the brainstorming session: real SPRT/anytime-valid sequential testing (replacing the two-checkpoint early-stopping rule in `evals/evals/stats.py`), which will likely need its own grounding-research pass given the statistical-correctness stakes. Also still open, lower priority: bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Cache L2 extra normalization ops. Vertex AI OAuth2 auth remains deprioritized per the brainstorming check-in.

## [2026-09-05] Tier 2, final item: real mixture-SPRT (anytime-valid) early-stopping

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/stats.py` (`two_checkpoint_early_stop` replaced by `mixture_sprt_early_stop`). `evals/evals/rollout/scheduler.py` (`EarlyStopConfig.min_trials` removed, `relative_mixing_variance` added; `_stop_after` now force-stops at `max_trials` as a resource ceiling, then delegates to the new stats function). `evals/evals/cli.py` (`--early-stop-min-trials` removed; `--early-stop-relative-mixing-variance` added). `evals/tests/test_stats.py` (whole early-stop section replaced, 20 tests). `evals/tests/test_stats_properties.py` (whole early-stop section replaced: 1 broad fuzz property, 1 null-simulation regression, 1 new power test). `evals/tests/test_scheduler.py` (6 early-stop tests rewritten, 1 new test for the max_trials-as-ceiling behavior). `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (3 early-stop tests updated for the new flag set and real stopping-trial counts). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-evals-mixture-sprt-early-stopping.md`. `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-evals-rollout-cost-mitigation.md` (Alternatives-Considered/Unresolved-Questions corrections), `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The last of three items resolved via the user's brainstorming check-in ("Replace two-checkpoint with true SPRT"). Grounding research (real web search against the primary/secondary literature, not assumed) found the prior RFC's own stated rejection reason for "real SPRT" — "needs an operator-supplied alternative pass rate" — is accurate for classical Wald SPRT specifically, but not for the mixture-SPRT (mSPRT) variant that project's own `two_checkpoint_early_stop` docstring already cited (Johari, Koomen, Pekelis & Walsh, KDD 2017) as the authoritative source for the failure mode it was built to avoid. mSPRT needs only `baseline_pass_rate` plus a tunable mixing-variance parameter that never affects the false-positive guarantee, resolving the prior RFC's blocker without the scope addition it worried about.

**Decisions made:** Fixed (never running-estimated) null-hypothesis variance `baseline*(1-baseline)`, for EXACT rather than merely asymptotic anytime-validity — an online-estimated variance is a common mSPRT variant elsewhere, but an independent implementer's own documentation explicitly flags real Type-I inflation at low sample counts without a substantial warmup, a cost this project has none to spend. `min_trials` removed entirely (not just deprioritized) — mSPRT's guarantee holds from trial 1, so keeping a floor "for safety" would misleadingly imply the test is less valid before it, which is false. `max_trials` redefined as a plain resource ceiling rather than removed, since unconditional per-group cost control is a real, independently-motivated engineering concern this project has consistently valued, unrelated to the statistical-validity question mSPRT fully resolves. Computed the decision in log-space rather than as a raw likelihood ratio, after `test_never_stops_at_zero_trials_and_never_raises_for_valid_input`'s own Hypothesis fuzzing caught a real `OverflowError` on an extreme deviation — this is exactly the kind of regression a fixed-example test would have missed (the specific failing input, `trials_run=18, successes_frac=1.0, baseline=0.01171875`, was never one anyone would have hand-picked).

**Verification performed:** `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q` → **181 passed, 11 skipped** (up from 177). `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!` (after shortening 2 over-long comment lines). `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → still `3 kept, 0 broken`. Separately confirmed against a real Docker daemon: `RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → **69 passed**, 0 skipped, 0 failed. Exact stopping-trial counts for every rewritten scenario test (scheduler and CLI) were computed by running `mixture_sprt_early_stop` directly against the real scenario data via a throwaway script, never guessed — an initial guess (reusing the old design's 0.01/0.99 baselines, expecting a stop at trial 2 to match the old min_trials=2 checkpoint) turned out to trigger a stop at trial 1 instead, caught before committing to the wrong assertion. The relative-mixing-variance "changes speed, not validity" unit test's first guessed values (0.1 vs 50.0 on a 6/10-successes case) also failed on the first real run — fixed by a numeric sweep finding an actually-true triple, not by adjusting the assertion to match a wrong intuition. Sanity-checked twice: (1) temporarily reverted the fixed null variance to a running `p_hat*(1-p_hat)` estimate and re-ran the null-simulation property test at low trial counts — empirical false-stop rate jumped to 100% (vs. the <15% ceiling the real test asserts), a dramatic confirmation of the exact risk the RFC's design section names, then restored; (2) confirmed the log-space fix produces bit-for-bit the same boolean decisions as the original direct formula on every input that doesn't overflow, via the hand-computed-reference test.

**Bugs found:** One real, self-caught-by-the-test-suite numerical bug (the `math.exp` overflow on an extreme deviation, described above) — caught by Hypothesis fuzzing before any release, not in production. Two self-caught wrong-guess mistakes in the test-writing process itself (assumed stopping trial counts and a mixing-variance comparison that turned out false on the first real run) — both caught immediately by actually running the assertions rather than trusting hand-derived intuition, and fixed with real, verified values before considering either test done.

**Next steps / resume point:** All three items from the brainstorming session are now shipped (multi-axis judging, Admin API v1, real mixture-SPRT early-stopping). Remaining Tier 2 backlog, lower priority, not yet started: bootstrap/Bayesian eval stats, Cache L2 extra normalization ops. No brainstormed or otherwise-approved work remains queued; the next session should re-run a backlog audit or ask the user for fresh direction rather than assuming continuation.

## [2026-09-05] Fresh backlog audit item 2: cache concurrent-miss stampede protection

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`Pipeline` gains `missGroup singleflight.Group`; new `cacheMissOutcome` struct + `runMissPath` method extracted from `HandleChatCompletion`'s inline miss-path body, wrapped in `p.missGroup.Do`). New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/cache_stampede_test.go` (2 tests). New `gateway/cmd/gateway/cache_stampede_integration_test.go` (1 real end-to-end test). `gateway/go.mod`/`go.sum` (`golang.org/x/sync` promoted from transitive to direct). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-cache-stampede-protection.md`. `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Second item of the fresh backlog audit's ranked list, and its top-ranked real feature item — closes `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Cache Denial-of-Service row ("cache-miss storms causing redundant expensive upstream calls... no request coalescing/singleflight/distributed-lock mechanism exists anywhere in the code"), a gap the audit independently re-verified was still real (not stale) directly against `checkCache` before ranking it.

**Decisions made:** Keyed the `singleflight.Group` on `l1Key` (exact-match), not `l2Key` (normalized) — a deliberate, named scope limit: broadening to `l2Key` would need writing into two different L1 keys from one shared execution, a genuinely separate design question, not solved in this pass. Confirmed `l1Key` already bakes in tenant ID (`cache.Key`'s own existing signature), so this change structurally cannot coalesce two different tenants' requests — proven with a dedicated test rather than only argued from the key's shape. Named, rather than hid, the real accepted tradeoff of using `golang.org/x/sync/singleflight` for this: only the leader (first) caller's own request context governs the shared guardrail checks and upstream call, so the leader's cancellation fails every coalesced waiter too, even if their own contexts are still live — the same tradeoff every production use of this exact library for HTTP coalescing accepts. Scoped this to single-instance (in-process) coalescing only, matching this project's own established "single-instance stepping stone, distributed version is later work" precedent from rate-limiting/budget. A real, adjacent, PRE-EXISTING finding surfaced while verifying this change introduces no NEW correctness issue: `finalize` already re-records the same computed cost against budget on every repeat cache hit today (not just the first real call, since a cached response's stored `Usage` still produces a nonzero cost) — this pass's coalesced-miss path exhibits the identical pattern, consistent with existing behavior, not a regression. Deliberately did not fix or change this — it's a separate, undecided budget/caching policy question, and fixing it as an uninvited side effect of a stampede-protection PR would be exactly the kind of scope creep this project's own conventions warn against; named explicitly in the RFC and here instead.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean (no new internal component; `golang.org/x/sync` is an external dependency, already permitted via `allow.depOnAnyVendor: true`). `go mod tidy` diff is minimal — exactly the one new direct-dependency line, `golang.org/x/sync` was already resolved transitively in `go.sum` so no new version conflict. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` failure. New tests run with `-count=3` to `5` under `-race` to rule out flakiness in the barrier-based concurrency synchronization pattern — zero flakes observed. Sanity-checked by temporarily bypassing `singleflight.Group.Do` (calling the closure directly) and re-running `TestHandleChatCompletionCoalescesConcurrentIdenticalCacheMisses`: failed with `upstreamCalls = 10, want exactly 1` — the exact wrong number for the exact right reason — then restored and re-confirmed the full suite green.

**Bugs found:** None introduced. One real, pre-existing (not caused by this change) budget/cache-hit cost double-counting pattern found while verifying — described above, deliberately left unfixed and named as a separate future item rather than folded into this PR.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue the audit's recommended order: (3) cache-hit provenance (layer/age/similarity) propagated into telemetry instead of a flat bool; (4) a real CI/CD gate for `evals report`; (5) a live metric at the rate-limiter fail-open path; then the remaining Medium/Large items. The newly-surfaced budget/cache-hit cost double-counting question (see Decisions above) is not yet on the tracked backlog list — worth adding explicitly next time `STATUS.md`'s ranked list is revisited.

Printed every mode's full report before any gate check, so a failure never hides a passing group's own real numbers. Scoped to `report_cmd` only — deliberately did not widen `run_cmd`/`rollout_cmd` with their own `--fail-under`, since the natural CI invocation shape already makes `report` the one command that needs to own the pass/fail decision.

Sanity-checked by temporarily changing the gate's comparison metric from the Wilson lower bound to the bare point estimate — the exact naive design this RFC's own Alternatives Considered section rejects — and re-running: 3 of the 5 new tests failed, each specifically because its fixture was chosen to diverge between the two metrics, then restored and re-confirmed the full suite green.

## [2026-09-05] Fresh backlog audit item 5: a live metric at the rate-limiter fail-open path

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/telemetry/telemetry.go` (new `meter`/`rateLimitFailOpenCounter`/`mustInt64Counter`/`RecordRateLimitFailOpen`; `Init` rewritten to build a `sdkmetric.MeterProvider` alongside the existing `TracerProvider`, sharing the exporter-kind switch; shutdown func made metrics-flush-failure-tolerant). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`checkRateLimit` calls `telemetry.RecordRateLimitFailOpen`). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/gatewayevents_test.go` (new `TestRateLimitFailOpenIncrementsMetricCounter`). `gateway/go.mod`/`go.sum` (`otel/exporters/stdout/stdoutmetric`, `otel/exporters/otlp/otlpmetric/otlpmetrichttp`, `otel/metric`, `otel/sdk/metric` promoted to/added as direct deps). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-ratelimit-fail-open-metric.md`. `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `docs/operations/TELEMETRY.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Fifth item of the fresh backlog audit's ranked list — closes the gap the audit found: `checkRateLimit`'s fail-open branch already logged and per-request-flagged a Redis backend error, but had no aggregate, alertable "how often is this happening" signal. Implementing it surfaced that this codebase had used OTel Tracing exclusively since inception — zero `MeterProvider`/`otel/metric` usage anywhere — making the audit's "Small effort" framing inaccurate. Resolved via an explicit brainstorming check-in (`AskUserQuestion`): given a choice between the full OTel Metrics pipeline, a lightweight admin-API-exposed in-process counter, or skipping the item, the user chose the full pipeline.

**Decisions made:** Mirrored the tracing pipeline's exact 3-exporter (`stdout`/`otlp`/`none`) shape for Metrics rather than inventing a different design for the second signal type — one `Config`, one exporter-kind decision, two providers. Obtained `meter`/`rateLimitFailOpenCounter` at package-init time exactly like the existing `Tracer`, relying on the same documented re-delegation guarantee (confirmed via `go doc`, not assumed, that `otel.Meter`'s behavior here is identical to `otel.Tracer`'s). `mustInt64Counter` panics on a construction error since the instrument name is a fixed, compile-time-known constant — a failure here can only be a real programming mistake. Found and fixed a real, genuine regression while verifying, not shipped over: adding the Metrics pipeline broke the pre-existing `TestInitOTLPExporterConstructsWithoutDialing`, because `sdkmetric.PeriodicReader.Shutdown` unconditionally attempts a final collect+export even with zero data points recorded, unlike the trace pipeline's batch span processor (nothing queued to flush with zero spans) — a real, external asymmetry between the two OTel Go SDK signal types. Fixed by making a metrics-flush-on-shutdown failure non-fatal (logged via `slog.WarnContext`, never propagated), while leaving the trace shutdown path's error handling exactly as it was — a deliberate design choice matching this codebase's own fail-open philosophy (the very path this item instruments: losing a few seconds of aggregate counter data on shutdown is an accepted tradeoff, unlike losing a span). Used `slog` directly in this dependency-free leaf package rather than adding an injected-logger parameter to `Init`, matching `internal/budget`'s own already-established convention for exactly this situation.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go mod tidy` diff minimal (4 packages moved indirect→direct, one new transitive hash for `otel/metric/x`). `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit` failure. New `TestRateLimitFailOpenIncrementsMetricCounter` installs a real `sdkmetric.ManualReader`-backed `MeterProvider` as the global provider, drives a real fail-open request through `HandleChatCompletion` via the existing `failingRedisBackend` fixture, then `Collect`s and asserts a real `kelvran.ratelimit.fail_open` data point of value 1 with the expected `kelvran.virtual_key.id` attribute. Sanity-checked twice: (1) temporarily removed the `RecordRateLimitFailOpen` call — the new test failed with "did not record a value of 1 for the expected key_id," then restored; (2) the shutdown fix itself was verified by direct before/after observation rather than assumed — the real dial error before the fix, a logged-but-non-fatal `telemetry_metrics_shutdown_flush_failed` warning after it.

**Bugs found:** One real, genuine regression, caught before shipping, not in production: the metrics-pipeline addition broke `TestInitOTLPExporterConstructsWithoutDialing` via the PeriodicReader-always-flushes-on-shutdown behavior described above — fixed as part of this same pass, not deferred.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue the audit's recommended order into the remaining Trivial/Medium items (TPM rate-limit dimension; soft-vs-hard budget-cap distinction — needs a quick product decision first; Golden/Regression Dataset promotion; `gen_ai.provider.name` enum validation; a Go/Python CI version matrix), then item 14 (Large, needs a short RFC first: multi-modal content field on the canonical `Message` schema). Two adjacent items surfaced in earlier passes remain untouched: the budget/cache-hit cost double-counting policy question, and `docs/operations/TELEMETRY.md`'s evals-tracing doc-staleness item.

## [2026-09-05] Fresh backlog audit item 17: cache hits and coalesced followers never re-charge budget

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`runMissPath` gains a `billable bool` return, set only inside the singleflight-invoked closure; `finalize` gains a `billable bool` param, gating `p.budget.Record`). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (mirrors the same `billable` plumbing — always `true` after a successful call, since streaming has no coalescing). New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/cost_double_counting_test.go` (2 tests). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-cost-double-counting.md`. `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Item 17, the ground-truthed decision-gated question from the batch research pass, resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: "zero charge" — cache hits and singleflight-coalesced followers never re-charge budget for cost they didn't actually cause. Closes the exact pre-existing pattern named (not introduced) while shipping cache stampede protection: `finalize` called `p.budget.Record` unconditionally whenever `err == nil`, regardless of whether the response was a genuine, unshared upstream call.

**Decisions made:** Kept `cost` itself unconditionally computed and reported via telemetry/the log line even when not billable — a deliberate choice to preserve real, informational "what this would have cost" data (useful for a cache-savings dashboard) while only the budget *ledger* stops double-counting. Derived `billable` from each path's own existing control flow rather than a new mechanism: a cache hit never sets it (stays `false` by zero value); `runMissPath`'s closure — the one singleflight actually invokes — sets it `true` as its first statement, so a follower's own copy stays `false` with zero shared state or synchronization, since `Group.Do` simply never runs a follower's closure at all. Deliberately did NOT derive this from `Group.Do`'s own `shared` return value — confirmed via `go doc` that `shared` is the identical value for the leader and every follower when there were 2+ callers, so it cannot by itself distinguish "I executed the real work" from "I received someone else's result." Deliberately did NOT touch `THREAT_MODEL.md` — this is a billing-accuracy fix, not a security mitigation; the bug it closes made the budget cap trip earlier than correct, the safe-side direction, not a consumption/DoS exposure.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker failure. New tests built their own nonzero-priced test pipeline (the shared `newTestPipeline*` helpers all use an empty price table, which can't distinguish "charged once" from "charged twice"). Sanity-checked by temporarily removing the `&& billable` gate: both new tests failed with the exact precise wrong dollar amounts (`0.022` instead of `0.011` for the repeat-cache-hit case; `0.11` instead of `0.011` for the ten-way coalesced case), then restored.

**Bugs found:** None introduced — this closes a real, pre-existing bug named in item 2's own entry, not a regression from this pass. ## [2026-09-05] Fresh backlog audit item 7: soft budget-cap warning threshold (log-only)

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/identity/identity.go` (`VirtualKey.BudgetWarnPercent float64`). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`VirtualKeyConfig.BudgetWarnPercent`, YAML parsing). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (wired into `identity.VirtualKey` construction). `gateway/internal/admin/admin.go` (`virtualKeyRequest.BudgetWarnPercent`, wired into the live-mutation path too). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (new `checkBudgetWarnThreshold`, called from `finalize`). `gateway/config.example.yaml`, `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go` (new field assertion). New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/budget_warn_threshold_test.go` (2 tests). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-budget-warn-threshold.md`. `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Item 7, resolved via `AskUserQuestion` (log-only chosen from 4 options) — closes the exact Unresolved Question `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-virtual-keys-budgets.md` left open at v1 ship: whether a soft budget-cap warning should exist alongside the existing hard cap.

**Decisions made:** `BudgetWarnPercent` is a *percentage of `BudgetUSD`*, not a second absolute USD value — deliberately, so it can't drift out of sync if `BudgetUSD` is ever changed later. Wired into both places a virtual key's budget fields exist (YAML config AND the live Admin API mutation surface) — adding it to only one would be a silent capability asymmetry against the Admin API's own "matches config" intent, the exact class of gap this project's own completeness discipline exists to catch. Reused item 17's `billable` gate — the warning check only runs on a genuine, unshared charge, so a cache hit or coalesced follower can never spuriously re-trigger it. Deliberately re-logs every occurrence rather than tracking "already warned this period" state, matching `checkRateLimit`'s own existing `ratelimit_backend_unavailable` precedent — new persisted per-period warn-state would be disproportionate to a log-only feature. Checked *post-charge* spend, not the pre-charge `budgetSpentAtDecision` value already threaded through `finalize` for `GatewayDecisionEvent` — the pre-charge value doesn't yet reflect the very request that might be the one crossing the line.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing rootless-Docker failure. New tests built their own nonzero-priced, logger-capturing pipeline (neither the shared helpers' empty price table nor `discardLogger` can prove a specific warning fired at a specific dollar amount). Sanity-checked by temporarily removing the `checkBudgetWarnThreshold` call: the positive test failed with the log buffer missing the expected line entirely, then restored.

**Bugs found:** None — new-feature implementation with no pre-existing code to regress.

Then item 14 (multi-modal `Message` content, needs a short RFC first).

**Files touched:** `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (config-time fail-fast check that `d.Provider` is a real registered adapter). `gateway/internal/telemetry/result.go` (`genAIProviderNameOverrides`/`genAIProviderName`, applied where `AttrGenAIProviderName` is set).
