**Decisions made:** Verified the real OTel semantic-conventions registry's well-known `gen_ai.provider.name` values directly via Context7 before implementing, rather than trusting the batch grounding pass's own claim at face value — the grounding pass turned out correct, but this was checked against a primary source specifically because the original RFC's own reasoning ("openai/anthropic already match the spec's known values") could have been read as implying the enum doesn't need namespacing at all, which would have contradicted the claim. Confirmed: `bedrock`/`gemini` genuinely need remapping to `aws.bedrock`/`gcp.gemini`; `openaicompat` has no well-known value in the registry at all, so it passes through verbatim rather than being forced into a wrong mapping — a self-hosted, wire-protocol-compatible runtime isn't a distinct GenAI provider in OTel's own vocabulary. Kept Kelvran's own internal provider identifiers (`Deployment.Provider`, `adapter.Registry`'s keys) completely unchanged — only the one OTel-facing attribute value differs from the internal string now; renaming the internal identifiers to match OTel's values instead was considered and rejected as a much larger, purely cosmetic ripple (config files, adapter registry, every existing test) for no real benefit.

New `TestBuildPipelineAcceptsEveryRealRegisteredProvider` runs a subtest per real provider (including `bedrock`'s AWS-specific env vars), proving the new config-time check never false-positives against a valid config, not just that it correctly rejects a bad one. Sanity-checked both halves independently: emptied `genAIProviderNameOverrides` — both new telemetry tests failed with the exact wrong unmapped string (`"bedrock"` instead of `"aws.bedrock"`); disabled the config-time check — the negative `buildPipeline` test failed with "returned nil error, want an error." Both restored.

## [2026-09-05] Fresh backlog audit: TPM (tokens-per-minute) rate-limit dimension

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/ratelimit/ratelimit.go` (`TokenBucket.HasBalance`/`Debit`). `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (`KeyConfig.TPMCapacity`/`TPMRefillPerSecond`; `KeyLimiter.tpmBuckets`; `AllowTPM`/`RecordTokens`; `Register` updated). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`VirtualKeyConfig` TPM fields + YAML parsing). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`keyConfigs` construction gains TPM fields; startup warning if TPM + Redis mode both configured). `gateway/internal/admin/admin.go` (`rateLimitRequest` TPM fields, wired into the live mutation path). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`checkRateLimit` gains the `AllowTPM` check; `finalize` calls `RecordTokens`, gated on `billable`). New `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter_test.go`/`ratelimit_test.go` additions (8 tests). New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/tpm_ratelimit_test.go` (3 tests). Extended `controlplane/config_test.go`. New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-tpm-rate-limit.md`. `gateway/config.example.yaml`, `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The last of the batch research pass's gateway-side implementable items — closes the "rpm/tpm" pairing `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own Request Lifecycle prose has named since before this feature existed, but only RPM had ever shipped.

**Decisions made:** Retrospective debit, not pre-call reservation — the only option given real token usage is unknown until after a request completes and no tokenizer exists in this codebase; named explicitly as a real limitation (only throttles the *next* request, never the one causing an overage), not hidden. Reused `TokenBucket` itself (two new methods) rather than a parallel type, since its refill/capacity math is exactly what TPM needs. Found and deliberately diverged from a pre-existing pattern rather than blindly mirroring it: `identity.VirtualKey.RateLimitBurst`/`RateLimitRefill` are write-only in production (verified via grep — set at construction, never read afterward except by a test-only helper) — TPM fields went only where actually consumed, not onto `identity.VirtualKey` as equally dead weight. Scoped to in-memory mode only in v1, matching this project's own repeated single-instance-first precedent — deliberately did not extend `RedisBackend` (would need a new Lua script and updates to its 2 existing test-fake implementers for a mode this pass doesn't need to support yet); a startup warning makes the limitation visible instead of silent. Gated `RecordTokens` on the same `billable` flag the cost-double-counting RFC introduced — a cache hit or coalesced follower must not double-debit tokens already counted once, the identical hazard already fixed for budget.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except two pre-existing, environmental rootless-Docker failures (see Bugs Found). New dataplane test `TestHandleChatCompletionCacheHitDoesNotDebitTPMBucket` needed a real distinguishing technique, not a direct balance read (no such accessor exists): a subsequent, genuinely different request's success (correct: balance stayed positive) vs. rejection (bug: balance incorrectly went negative) is the only observable signal `AllowTPM`'s bare boolean allows. Sanity-checked twice: removed `RecordTokens` entirely — the exhaustion test failed with "want ErrRateLimited" but got nil; removed only the `billable` gate — the cache-hit test failed with its third call incorrectly rejected. Both restored.

**Bugs found:** None introduced by this change. One genuinely new, adjacent finding, confirmed unrelated via the same stash-and-rerun discipline used earlier this session (identical panic on completely unmodified code, and this pass never touches `redislimiter` at all): `internal/ratelimit/redislimiter`'s own `TestMain` also requires a real Docker daemon, and had been silently test-cache-masked in every earlier `go test ./... -race` run this session — a second pre-existing, environmental failure, not a regression, now visible after `go clean -testcache`.

**Next steps / resume point:** Continue to Golden/Regression Dataset promotion (evals, independent of all gateway work this session), then item 14 (multi-modal `Message` content, needs a short RFC first) — the two remaining items from the fresh backlog audit's original ranked list.

## [2026-09-05] Fresh backlog audit: Golden/Regression Dataset promotion (`evals promote`)

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/cli.py` (`_append_case_to_suite`; new `promote_cmd`). New `evals/tests/test_promote_integration.py` (6 tests). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-evals-golden-regression-promotion.md`. `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The last real-implementation item from the fresh backlog audit's original ranked list — closes the "promote failing trace" arrow `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own architecture diagram has always drawn into a `[Golden/Regression Dataset]` box, with zero mechanism behind it until now.

**Decisions made:** A promoted case is a genuinely new, distinct `EvalCase` identity (`revision=1`, a new id derived from both the source case and the run) — never a new revision of the case that ran, since editing the original suite's own entry would conflate "this exact case, evolving" with "a new, derived case pinning one specific failure." `--tier golden` excluded from `click.Choice` entirely rather than a runtime check — Click itself makes it structurally impossible for the command body to ever reach a golden-tier assignment, directly enforcing (not just documenting) `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Spoofing row. `--tier regression` requires a real failing `Score` when `--scores` is given (promoting a passing run to "regression" tier has nothing to regression-test); `--tier drift_sample` has no such requirement, since its purpose is general distribution sampling, not failure-tracking specifically — the two tiers' different intents genuinely warranted different validation, not a one-size-fits-all check. New `_append_case_to_suite` matches `_load_cases`'s own pre-existing plain-JSON-array suite format rather than reusing `results_store.py`'s JSONL append pattern — two deliberately different persistence shapes for two different kinds of file (a small, human-reviewable suite vs. an ever-growing results log), not an oversight.

**Verification performed:** `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q` → 192 passed (up from 186), 11 skipped. `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!` (one line-length fix along the way, wrapped rather than suppressed with `noqa` — confirmed via grep that this codebase has never used `noqa` anywhere). `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → still `3 kept, 0 broken`. Separately confirmed against a real local Docker daemon (`RUN_DOCKER_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/test_scheduler.py tests/test_cli_integration.py tests/test_sandbox_integration.py -v` → 74 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed). New `test_promote_integration.py` kept as a separate file rather than appended to `test_cli_integration.py`, which is already well past this project's own 800-line file-size guideline. Sanity-checked by temporarily disabling the regression-tier failing-Score check: the corresponding test failed with the promotion incorrectly succeeding (exit code 0 where a nonzero was expected), then restored.

**Bugs found:** None — new-feature implementation with no pre-existing code to regress.

**Next steps / resume point:** The fresh backlog audit's original ranked list is now fully closed except item 14 (multi-modal `Message` content on the canonical schema) — Large, explicitly needs a short RFC first per the audit's own framing, not started. No further approved work is queued; the next session should ask for fresh direction (item 14, a new backlog audit, or something else) rather than assuming continuation.

## [2026-09-06] Fresh backlog audit item 14 (final item): multi-modal content on the canonical `Message` schema

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/adapter/types.go` (`ContentPart`; `Message.Parts`). `gateway/internal/adapter/anthropic/anthropic.go` (`ContentSource`; `contentPartToBlock`). `gateway/internal/adapter/gemini/gemini.go` (`InlineData`/`FileData`; `contentPartToPart`). `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/bedrock.go` (`ImageBlock`/`DocumentBlock`/`ByteSource`; `contentPartToBlock`/`mediaTypeToFormat`). `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/openai.go` (`Message.Content` → `json.RawMessage`; `nativeContentPart`/`nativeImageURL`; `contentToNative`/`contentFromNative`). `gateway/internal/adapter/openaicompat/openaicompat.go` (identical mirror of the openai package's change, per its own "near-verbatim copy" convention). 6 test files updated for the `Content string`→`json.RawMessage` change in shared native-Message-construction call sites outside the adapter packages (`internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane_test.go`, `guardrail_test.go`; `internal/admin/admin_test.go`; `cmd/gateway/integration_test.go`, `cache_stampede_integration_test.go`, `graceful_shutdown_integration_test.go`). New test files per adapter (`*_test.go` additions) plus new `internal/gateway/dataplane/multimodal_cache_guardrail_test.go` and `cmd/gateway/multimodal_integration_test.go`. New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-06-gateway-multimodal-content.md`. Dated correction notes added to `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-gemini-adapter.md`/`2026-09-04-bedrock-adapter.md`. `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The final item from the fresh backlog audit's original ranked list — closes the multi-modal gap both the Gemini and Bedrock adapter RFCs explicitly deferred at ship time as "out of scope, a real future item."

**Decisions made:** Verified every provider's real wire shape directly via Context7 before designing (OpenAI's real OpenAPI spec, the Anthropic Go SDK's type definitions, AWS's own Converse API docs) — not trusted from memory or the original backlog audit's own framing alone, matching this session's established discipline for exactly this kind of claim. A load-bearing finding from reading this codebase's own adapter code (not the wire-format research): Anthropic/Gemini/Bedrock already represent content as an array of typed parts/blocks internally — only OpenAI/openaicompat needed new wire-level union handling. Kept the canonical `Message.Content string` completely unchanged and added an additive `Parts []ContentPart` field, rejecting the alternative of making `Content` itself a wire-level string-or-array union (true byte-for-byte OpenAI client compatibility, but would touch dozens of existing call sites across every adapter, `dataplane.go`'s serialization functions, and hundreds of tests, for a compatibility property with no concrete consumer today) — named explicitly in the RFC as a deliberate, smaller-blast-radius choice, not an oversight. Confirmed (not assumed) that cache-key correctness and guardrail coverage come for free from `dataplane.serializeMessages`'s pre-existing blind full-struct `json.Marshal` — required zero changes to that function or the guardrail call site, verified with dedicated tests including a real sanity-check-by-breaking. Scoped each adapter's own real mismatch as a named, typed-error limitation rather than a guessed mapping: Bedrock's Converse API has no generic-URL image/document source (only inline `bytes` or an `s3Location` with an optional `bucketOwner` the canonical schema has no equivalent for) — verified directly via Context7 before choosing to reject URL-based parts outright rather than ship a possibly-wrong S3 mapping; OpenAI/openaicompat's Chat Completions API has no native document content-part type at all, so a `document` part returns a real error there specifically, not silently mapped to `image_url` or dropped. `FromProvider`/response-direction content stays text-only in every adapter (matching the Gemini RFC's own prior "image-generation... first pass is text/tool-use only" precedent for this exact class of limit) — a native array-shaped response (OpenAI/openaicompat) returns a real, typed error rather than silently discarding non-text parts.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean (after fixing 7 real compile breaks `go vet` caught in test files outside the adapter packages that constructed `openai.Message{Content: "..."}`/`openaicompat.Message{Content: "..."}` literals directly — genuine regressions from the `Content string`→`json.RawMessage` change, found and fixed before considering this done, not silently left broken). `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go mod tidy` → empty diff (stdlib `encoding/json` only). `gofmt -l .` → clean. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing, environmental rootless-Docker failures already documented in the TPM item's own entry. Every existing adapter's golden/regression fixture test passed byte-for-byte unmodified, including OpenAI/openaicompat's — the load-bearing proof that the `Content` type change is genuinely additive for the common text-only case, not just argued to be. Sanity-checked the cache-key claim specifically (not just the feature generally): temporarily excluded `Parts` from JSON marshaling via its own struct tag (`json:"-"`) — the cache-key-variance test failed with the exact expected wrong upstream-call count, then restored.

**Bugs found:** None introduced into shipped behavior — the 7 compile breaks above were caught by `go vet` before any test ran, not a runtime bug that shipped.

**Next steps / resume point:** The fresh backlog audit's entire original ranked list is now fully closed. The one real, adjacent finding surfaced this pass (`chatCompletionsHandler`'s unbounded `io.ReadAll(r.Body)`, named in `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s DoS row) is not yet on a tracked backlog list — worth adding explicitly next time one is compiled. **Intent/summary:** Item 2a of the approved Phase 2 upgrade roadmap, closing `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md` Finding 2 exactly as scoped: Kong/Envoy/LiteLLM all treat active (traffic-INDEPENDENT) synthetic health-probing as a genuinely separate mechanism from the traffic-DERIVED statistical circuit breaker (Envoy outlier detection, LiteLLM `allowed_fails` cooldown) — only the former needs no production-traffic-volume floor, so only it ships now. This narrows, not overturns, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s prior router deferral ("no circuit breaker, no health/cooldown tracking"); the traffic-derived half correctly stays deferred.

**Decisions made:** Split the feature across the package boundary the plan itself flagged as ambiguous — `router.Router` gained only health-STATE bookkeeping (`ReportProbeResult`/`IsHealthy`, consulted by `Select`), staying the zero-I/O, adapter-agnostic leaf it already was; the probe LOOP that actually issues a request lives in `dataplane.Pipeline` instead, since only `dataplane` has each `Deployment`'s `BaseURL`/`Provider`/credentials and the registered `adapter.Adapter` — `router.Deployment` is deliberately decoupled from `dataplane.Deployment` (mirroring `ratelimit.KeyConfig`/`identity.VirtualKey`'s own precedent), so it structurally can't issue a real request itself. This meant zero new package and zero new `go-arch-lint` component were needed — the plan's own "or a new `internal/router/health` package" hint was considered and explicitly rejected in the RFC's Alternatives section, since `dataplane` already legitimately depends on both `router` and `adapter` today. A real correctness subtlety found only by hand-tracing the smooth-WRR algorithm before writing any test: bounding the unhealthy-skip search loop at `len(deployments)` calls (the naive choice) is provably wrong under a skewed weight configuration (e.g. weight 50 vs. weight 1) — the schedule can return the high-weight deployment many times in a row before ever reaching the low-weight one, so a `len(deployments)`-call bound can starve a healthy low-weight deployment from ever being found. Fixed by bounding on `sumW` (the sum of every deployment's normalized weight in the group — the schedule's real, exact full-cycle length, guaranteeing every distinct deployment is visited at least once), proven by a dedicated `TestSelectSkipsUnhealthyDespiteHeavilySkewedWeight`. `Select` fails OPEN (returns a known-bad deployment rather than `("", false)`) if every deployment for a model is unhealthy at once, mirroring `internal/ratelimit`'s own Redis-fail-open precedent — a known-bad deployment is still a better answer than incorrectly surfacing `dataplane.ErrNoDeployment` for a model that IS configured. `ProbeDeployments` calls `p.callDeployment` directly (bypassing auth/cache/guardrail/budget/rate-limit entirely) with a minimal one-token `{Role: "user", Content: "ping"}` request — a probe is not real client traffic, is never cached, never billed, belongs to no virtual key. `RunHealthProbeLoop` deliberately does not probe immediately at start (only after the first interval elapses), so a restart storm never synchronizes an extra probe burst with real traffic resuming. Per the user's own approved decision, thresholds are conservative (3 consecutive failures / 2 consecutive successes, 300s interval) — deliberately not LiteLLM's own aggressive single-failure default, since Kelvran has no production traffic yet to calibrate a more aggressive one; all three values configurable via a new `health_probe:` YAML section, `<= 0` resolving to these defaults inside `router.HealthConfig.normalized()` (called once in `router.New`), mirroring `AdminConfig.ListenAddr`'s own "operational default resolved by the consumer" precedent.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `gofmt -l .` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean, no new component. `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker failures (`TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit`; `internal/ratelimit/redislimiter`'s `TestMain` panic) — confirmed unrelated, this pass never touches `internal/ratelimit`. 6 new pure unit tests in `internal/router` (the N-of-M load-bearing proofs, a non-consecutive-never-excludes proof, the fail-open proof, and the skewed-weight correctness proof). 2 new Pipeline-level tests in `internal/gateway/dataplane` driving `ProbeDeployments` directly (deterministic, no real elapsed time) against a mocked `UpstreamCaller`, then proving real `HandleChatCompletion` calls (each with distinct content, forcing a genuine cache miss and fresh routing decision every time) never reach an excluded deployment and do reach it again after recovery. 1 new real end-to-end integration test in `cmd/gateway` — two real deployments for one model, each backed by its own real `httptest.Server` (one always 500s, one always succeeds), over real HTTP on both the client and upstream sides, mirroring `cache_stampede_integration_test.go`'s own "prove it end-to-end" precedent; confirms 20 real client requests sent only after the 3rd consecutive probe failure trips the threshold produce zero calls to the failing upstream and 20 to the healthy one. Sanity-checked the single most load-bearing line (`ReportProbeResult`'s unhealthy-transition condition) by temporarily short-circuiting it to `if false && ...`: all three test layers failed for the exact right reason (router: 3 tests; dataplane: both new tests; cmd/gateway: the integration test, "real client requests reaching the excluded 'bad' upstream = 19, want 0"), then reverted — confirmed via `grep` that the revert left zero trace, full suite re-passed. `evals` untouched by this pass — `uv run pytest tests/` (192 passed, 11 skipped) and `ruff check .` (clean) both confirmed unaffected.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code. One design mistake caught and fixed BEFORE any test was written, not after: an initial plan to bound `selectHealthy`'s skip-search loop at `len(deployments)` calls was hand-traced against a skewed-weight example first and found provably insufficient (see Decisions above) — fixed to use `sumW` before implementing, then proven correct by a dedicated test rather than assumed safe.

**Next steps / resume point:** Item 2a of Phase 2 is complete, verified, and committed on `phase2/2a-gateway-health-probing`, not yet merged. Remaining Phase 2 items per the approved roadmap: gateway OTel GenAI metrics (`gen_ai.client.token.usage`/`gen_ai.client.operation.duration` histograms), gateway error-type/multi-hop fallback chains, cache cross-instance telemetry, evals judge-panel interface RFC — each still needs its own short RFC before implementation, per this project's own established discipline. The traffic-derived statistical circuit breaker (Envoy outlier detection / LiteLLM `allowed_fails` cooldown) remains correctly deferred, not scheduled, pending real production traffic to calibrate a volume floor against.
## [2026-09-07] Tier 2 (report Finding 3): gen_ai.client.token.usage + gen_ai.client.operation.duration OTel Metrics

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/telemetry/telemetry.go` (new `mustFloat64Histogram` helper; `tokenUsageHistogram`/`operationDurationHistogram` package-level instruments built the same package-init-time way as the existing `rateLimitFailOpenCounter`/`cacheL3GateOutcomeCounter`; `GenAITokenTypeInput`/`GenAITokenTypeOutput` constants; new `RecordChatCompletionMetrics(ctx, ChatCompletionResult)`). `gateway/internal/telemetry/result.go` (`ChatCompletionResult` gained `RequestModel`, `Duration`, `Billable`, `ErrorType`; new `AttrGenAITokenType`/`AttrErrorType` constants). `gateway/internal/telemetry/result_test.go` (extended, in place, the existing `TestRecordCacheL3GateOutcomeIncrementsPerGateAndOutcome` — did not add a second, separate test function — with three `RecordChatCompletionMetrics` scenarios sharing its one `otel.SetMeterProvider` swap window: billable success, non-billable cache-hit replay, and failure). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`HandleChatCompletion` now captures `start := time.Now()` before the span starts; `finalize` gained a `duration time.Duration` parameter, builds one shared `telemetry.ChatCompletionResult` passed to both `RecordChatCompletionResult` and the new `RecordChatCompletionMetrics`, and a new `errorTypeFor(outcome)` helper reusing `outcomeFor`'s existing error classification). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (`HandleChatCompletionStream`: identical `start`/duration-threading change). New RFC: `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-genai-metrics.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Implements plan item 2b (report Finding 3, `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md`): the two OTel GenAI semantic-convention histograms (`gen_ai.client.token.usage`, `gen_ai.client.operation.duration`), additive to `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-ratelimit-fail-open-metric.md` which had explicitly named this as future work. Directly serves `PRD.md`'s cost/governance-accountability differentiation thesis with concrete, spec-backed instrument names, matching Envoy AI Gateway v1.1's own shipped precedent (both instruments plus a ready-made Grafana dashboard).

**Decisions made:** (1) Reused `dataplane.finalize`'s existing per-request `ChatCompletionResult` capture rather than adding a second data-gathering point, per the plan's own explicit instruction — both `RecordChatCompletionResult` (span attrs) and `RecordChatCompletionMetrics` (the two histograms) now consume the identical struct value. (2) `gen_ai.client.operation.duration` is measured from `time.Now()` at `HandleChatCompletion`/`HandleChatCompletionStream`'s own entry point, not derived from the span's own timing — verified by reading the OTel Go API that `trace.Span` has no clean, provider-agnostic way to read back its own start time, so the caller captures it explicitly and threads `time.Since(start)` into `finalize` via the deferred closure. This measures the gateway's full request boundary (auth/rate-limit/budget/cache/router/upstream/guardrails), not narrowly the upstream call, matching every other per-request signal in this codebase. (3) `gen_ai.client.token.usage` is gated on `billable` (the same parameter `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-cost-double-counting.md` already threads through `finalize` for `budget.Record`/`limiter.RecordTokens`), deliberately NOT on `CacheHit` — a real, considered decision, not a mechanical copy: a histogram accumulates across many requests, so replaying a cache hit's already-counted token count into it (as the span attribute correctly does, since that's informational per-request data) would inflate an aggregate token-throughput query every time that entry was served. `billable` is false for both cache hits and coalesced singleflight followers, closing both cases the same cost-double-counting RFC already identified. (4) `error.type` is computed once via a new `errorTypeFor(outcome)` helper reusing `outcomeFor`'s existing sentinel-error classification (lowercased, `OUTCOME_` prefix stripped, e.g. `ErrRateLimited` → `"rate_limited"`), not a second, independent error taxonomy. (5) Instrument name strings (`gen_ai.client.token.usage`, `gen_ai.client.operation.duration`, `gen_ai.token.type`, `error.type`) are hardcoded string constants, not sourced from `go.opentelemetry.io/otel/semconv`'s incubating GenAI module — matching `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-otel-tracing-agent-run-id.md`'s identical prior decision for the trace attributes, since that module's Go API surface still tracks the spec's own "Development" (not "Stable") maturity for `gen_ai.client.token.usage` specifically.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go mod tidy` → empty diff (OTel Metrics SDK packages were already direct dependencies as of the fail-open-metric RFC; no new dependency needed). `go test ./... -race`: every package `ok` except the two already-documented, environmental rootless-Docker failures (`cmd/gateway`'s `TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit`, `internal/ratelimit/redislimiter`'s `TestMain` panic) — confirmed by name and by re-reading their failure output ("rootless Docker not found") that both are the exact pre-existing, unrelated failures the task named, and that neither touched package is part of this diff. A significant sub-finding surfaced while writing the test: this package's global OTel meter delegates to a real `MeterProvider` **exactly once per test binary process** (`go.opentelemetry.io/otel/internal/global`'s `delegateMeterOnce`, confirmed by reading the SDK source directly and reproducing empirically with a throwaway experiment file: a second `otel.SetMeterProvider` swap in a second test function, observing a *different* instrument off the same shared package-level `meter`, silently sees zero data points because the delegation already resolved to the first swap's provider). Both packages that could plausibly host a new swap-based metrics test (`telemetry`, `dataplane`) had already spent their one delegation on prior RFCs' own tests (`TestRecordCacheL3GateOutcomeIncrementsPerGateAndOutcome`, `TestRateLimitFailOpenIncrementsMetricCounter`), so the only collision-free option was extending the existing `telemetry`-package test in place — done, with a doc-comment explaining exactly why, rather than silently working around it. Sanity-checked the load-bearing double-counting-avoidance logic: temporarily changed `if !r.Billable { return }` to `if false { return }` in `RecordChatCompletionMetrics`; the extended test failed with exactly the expected two messages ("non-billable cache-hit scenario recorded an input/output token.usage data point — must be suppressed"); reverted, confirmed zero trace remains (`git diff` on the file is purely additive, no leftover artifact) and the full suite is green again.

**Bugs found:** None in existing code. One design pitfall caught and fixed during implementation, not a shipped bug: an earlier draft of `RecordChatCompletionMetrics` called `append(attrs, ...)` twice in separate branches sharing `attrs`'s backing array across two `metric.WithAttributes` calls — functionally correct given synchronous, non-concurrent execution (each `Record` call consumes/copies its attribute set before the next `append` runs), but a real footgun not worth relying on; changed to give each token-type data point its own copied slice before this was ever exercised by a real test.

**Next steps / resume point:** Item complete and committed on `phase2/2b-gateway-genai-metrics` (base `d056de5`). No other cross-item conflicts identified. ## [2026-09-07] Gateway Phase 2 item 2c: error-classified, multi-hop fallback chains

**Files touched:** New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback.go` (`UpstreamHTTPError`; `FallbackClassContentPolicy`/`FallbackClassContextWindowExceeded`/`FallbackClassGeneric`; `classifyFallbackError`; `fallbackTargets`; `(*Pipeline).attemptFallbackChain`). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`Deployment.FallbackChains`; `runMissPath`'s fallback block; `NewHTTPUpstreamCaller`/`NewHTTPUpstreamStreamCaller`'s status>=300 branches now construct `*UpstreamHTTPError`; package doc + `fallbackInfo` doc corrected). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (`streamDeploymentWithFallback` now shares the same chain-walking helper, `stop` checked before every hop). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`DeploymentConfig.FallbackChains`; independently-defined `fallbackClass*` constants; `parseFallbackChains` — comma-separated strings, since this file's YAML-subset parser has no list support). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`FallbackChains` copied into `dataplane.Deployment`; new `validateFallbackChainTargets` referential-integrity check after the deployments loop). `gateway/config.example.yaml` (realistic example on `gpt4o-primary`: context-window → `claude-opus-primary`, content-policy → `claude-opus-primary,gemini-flash-primary`). `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` (router section + Request Lifecycle line, both corrected from stale "single fallback"/"no model-group chains" prose). New tests: `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback_test.go`, `fallback_chain_integration_test.go`, 2 new tests in `streaming_test.go`, 3 new + 1 extended test in `controlplane/config_test.go`, new `gateway/cmd/gateway/fallback_chain_validation_test.go`. New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-error-classified-fallback-chains.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Closes the fallback half of `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md` Finding 5 (the LiteLLM half — Kong's multi-dimensional rate-limit matching is the other half, Medium-Large, deferred to Phase 3b since it touches `internal/ratelimit`'s key shape, untouched here). Kelvran's router had exactly one undifferentiated fallback attempt regardless of error type (`runMissPath`'s `p.nextDeployment(req.Model)` retry) — this ships separate, coexisting fallback lists keyed by error class plus in-order multi-hop chains, matching LiteLLM's own shape.

**Decisions made:** Classification is a best-effort, provider-agnostic heuristic over a new minimal `*UpstreamHTTPError`'s status+body TEXT, not a structured field — verified LIVE against Anthropic's and OpenAI's current API docs (not trusted from training-data memory, per this codebase's own established discipline) that no provider exposes a clean, stable error-code enum for content-policy/context-window conditions; Anthropic's real docs confirm every 4xx validation failure collapses to one generic `invalid_request_error` type. Precedence resolved by new `fallbackTargets`: a deployment with `len(FallbackChains) == 0` (the default) falls through to the EXACT pre-existing router-based single-fallback behavior — the hard backward-compatibility guarantee, proven not just argued, since every pre-existing fallback test (`TestHandleChatCompletionFallsBackOnUpstreamError`, `TestGatewayEventFallbackDetailPopulatedOnFallback`, both streaming fallback tests, the tenant-isolation fallback test) passed completely unmodified. A deployment that DID opt in gets its classified error's own list, or `generic` as a catch-all fallthrough if that class's own list is empty, or no fallback at all for an unconfigured class — never a silent revert to the old behavior for part of an explicit config. `fallback_chains:` YAML is a comma-separated string per class, not a list — this file's hand-rolled parser explicitly supports no lists at all (deployments themselves are modeled as a mapping for the same reason), and an ordered chain can't be expressed as a mapping the way the existing `allowed_models` set can. `fallbackInfo`'s wire shape (and `api/gatewayevents/v1`) is completely untouched — `from`/`reason` still capture only the FIRST abandoned deployment/error, by design; which hop ultimately served is already visible via existing per-request OTel attributes, so no new proto field earns its complexity. Extracted a single shared `(*Pipeline).attemptFallbackChain` used by both `runMissPath` (buffered) and `streamDeploymentWithFallback` (streaming) rather than duplicating the multi-hop loop twice, since streaming's existing single-fallback code had the exact same shape already; streaming's `stop` closure re-checks `firstChunkSent` before every hop, not only the first, extending the pre-existing "never risk duplicated content" rule across the whole chain. Explicitly reconciled with `docs/rfcs/2026-09-04-weighted-routing.md`'s prior rejection of "model-group fallback chains" (DECISIONS.md, 2026-09-04): that RFC deferred cross-model fallback to a future, dedicated RFC rather than banning it permanently, and named a materially different, *automatic* LiteLLM-style mechanism than this RFC's *explicit, opt-in, per-deployment* named-target lists — written out in full in the RFC's Context section rather than silently diverging from a settled decision.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean (confirms `dataplane`/`controlplane` stayed sibling packages — `FallbackChains` is independently typed in each, connected only in `cmd/gateway`, no new cross-package import). `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `gofmt -l .` → clean. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the two pre-existing, environmental rootless-Docker failures (`TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit`; `internal/ratelimit/redislimiter`'s own `TestMain` panic) — confirmed by exact name match against the pre-flagged list, unrelated to this change. New tests: `fallback_test.go` (unit-level classifier/precedence/chain-walking, 8 tests), `fallback_chain_integration_test.go` (4 full-`Pipeline` tests — per-class routing against 4 same-model deployments specifically so plain round-robin would have picked differently, multi-hop success, multi-hop full-exhaustion-then-error with no extra router-fallback attempt beyond the configured chain, explicit no-config backward-compat), 2 new streaming parity tests, 3 new + 1 extended `controlplane` config tests, 2 new `cmd/gateway` startup-validation tests. Sanity-checked the load-bearing class-routing test by temporarily forcing `classifyFallbackError` to always return `FallbackClassGeneric`: the content-policy and context-window subtests failed with the exact expected wrong deployment served (routed to `generic-alt` instead of their own configured target), then reverted — `git diff` on the file showed zero trace of the breakage before committing.

**Bugs found:** None introduced into shipped behavior — new-feature implementation, and every pre-existing fallback-related test (buffered, streaming, tenant-isolation, GatewayDecisionEvent-detail) passed unmodified, itself the regression proof.

**Next steps / resume point:** This item, 2c, is complete and committed to `phase2/2c-error-classified-fallback-chains`. Flagged explicitly for the orchestrator: this item and 2b (OTel GenAI metrics) both very likely touch `dataplane.go`'s `runMissPath`/`finalize` area — this pass kept the diff to `runMissPath`'s fallback block specifically (not `finalize`, not the metrics-adjacent code) and left `fallbackInfo`'s struct shape/`finalize`'s signature completely untouched, but the orchestrator should still merge 2b and 2c in a deliberate order and diff-review the merge, not assume a clean auto-merge. Kong's multi-dimensional rate-limit matching (the other half of Finding 5) remains deferred to Phase 3b, untouched by this pass.
## [2026-09-07] Phase 2 item 2d: cache cross-instance duplicate-work / effective-hit-rate telemetry

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/telemetry/telemetry.go` (new `InstanceID`/`computeInstanceID`; `service.instance.id` added to the OTel `Resource` in `Init`; `RecordCacheL3GateOutcome` now also attributes `kelvran.instance.id`). `gateway/internal/telemetry/result.go` (new `AttrKelvranInstanceID` constant). New `gateway/internal/telemetry/cachecorrelation/cachecorrelation.go` + `cachecorrelation_test.go` (pure `Event`/`Result`/`Analyze` correlation logic, 11 unit tests over synthetic multi-instance fixtures). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`checkCache` gains a `tenantID` parameter and per-layer-check emission via new `logCacheCrossInstanceCheck`; `checkLexicalCache` gains an `l1Key` parameter and emits on its two real-search-completed paths only). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (both call sites updated to match the new signatures). New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/cache_cross_instance_telemetry_test.go` (4 full-pipeline tests proving the live emission side). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/cache_l2_test.go` (existing `TestL2HitPromotesIntoL1` updated for `checkCache`'s new signature). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`run()` logs `InstanceID` once at startup). `gateway/.go-arch-lint.yml` (new `cache-correlation` component for `telemetry/cachecorrelation`, registered as a pure leaf like `telemetry` itself). `docs/operations/TELEMETRY.md` (two new rows: the cross-instance log signal, and instance identity; also removed a stale claim — "the first and only use of OTel Metrics" — that was already false before this session, since Finding 1's `kelvran.cache.l3.gate_outcome` counter predates it). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-cache-cross-instance-telemetry.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Item 2d of the approved Phase 2 roadmap — reads `docs/upgrade-research/cache-2026-09-06.md` Finding 5 in full: singleflight-style coalescing (`dataplane.go`'s `missGroup`, keyed on `l1Key`) is inherently single-process; the literature is consistent that distributed coordination introduces new failure modes rather than being a low-risk scope upgrade, so the burden is on demonstrating real cross-instance duplicate work / effective-hit-rate loss before ever building a distributed/shared cache. Confirmed via `docs/operations/DEPLOY.md` and `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` that Kelvran runs single-instance only today, so the real phenomenon can't be measured directly — this ships the measurement infrastructure only (event schema + live emission + a standalone, tested correlation function), explicitly not a distributed cache, not a live correlator, not any new runtime dependency, matching the plan's own "instrument now, decide later" framing.

**Decisions made:** `InstanceID` is `hostname:pid` (stdlib `os.Hostname`/`os.Getpid`), not a random UUID — confirmed via grep that `github.com/google/uuid` is only an indirect, test-tooling dependency today with zero production imports, so promoting it to direct for this alone would be a real, avoidable new dependency edge; Kelvran's real deployment model already gives every replica its own hostname. `cachecorrelation.Analyze` counts ANY peer event (hit or miss) as opening a valid cross-instance window, not only an explicit peer HIT, diverging deliberately from Finding 5's own literal one-line phrasing ("a repeat of a hit") — named explicitly in the RFC's Design and Alternatives-considered sections: this pipeline is write-through (`writeCache` runs immediately after every genuine miss), so a peer's own first-ever miss already means that instance's cache holds the value from approximately that timestamp until `timestamp+ttl`; restricting to peer-hits-only would undercount the dominant real case of two near-simultaneous first requests landing on two different instances. `checkLexicalCache` (L3) does NOT get its own exact-key correlation `Event` scheme distinct from L1/L2 — L3's match is a fuzzy near-duplicate search with no deterministic lookup key of its own, so it reuses the REQUEST's own `l1Key` as its correlation identity (an explicit, named simplification: "was this exact request servable from any cache resource on this instance," not "l1Key is a real L3 storage key") and, separately/additionally, gets `instance_id` added to the already-shipped `kelvran.cache.l3.gate_outcome` ablation counter (Finding 1) — both were shipped, neither is mutually exclusive. The correlation event is a structured LOG line, deliberately never an OTel metric attribute: `cache_key` is an unbounded-cardinality SHA-256 hash, and attaching that to a metric is a well-documented cardinality-explosion anti-pattern, unlike the small fixed vocabularies (`gate`, `outcome`, `instance_id`) this codebase's existing counters already use. `checkCache`/`checkLexicalCache` only emit an event when the backend genuinely answered (`getErr == nil`, or a completed — not bypassed/errored — L3 search) — a backend error or volatile-bypass tells this telemetry nothing about real presence and must never be misreported as a definite miss. Tenant boundary is checked explicitly in `Analyze`'s own grouping (`groupKey{tenantID, key}`) rather than trusted implicitly from `Key` alone, mirroring this codebase's own cross-tenant-isolation discipline even though this is measurement code, not the cache itself.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean (new `cache-correlation` component, a pure leaf). `go mod tidy` → empty diff (stdlib-only). `gofmt -l .` → clean. `go test ./... -race` (after `go clean -testcache`) → every package `ok` except the two pre-existing, already-documented, environmental rootless-Docker failures (`TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit`, `internal/ratelimit/redislimiter`'s own `TestMain` panic) — confirmed unrelated, this pass touches neither package. New `internal/telemetry/cachecorrelation` package: 11 unit tests over synthetic multi-instance `Event` fixtures per the plan's own Verify line, covering cross-instance detection, same-instance exclusion, expired-window exclusion, future-peer causality exclusion, explicit-hit-as-peer, tenant isolation, key isolation, hits-never-count-as-misses, `EffectiveHitRateLoss`'s zero-vs-real-fraction distinction, and multiple-qualifying-peers-still-counts-once. New `internal/gateway/dataplane/cache_cross_instance_telemetry_test.go`: 4 full-pipeline tests proving live emission — L1 miss-then-hit, an L2 hit reporting L2's own distinct TTL (not L1's), an L3 hit reusing the SAME `cache_key` as that request's own L1 miss (the cross-layer identity proof — caught and fixed a real test bug along the way: the per-request `chat_completion` summary log line also carries its own `cache_layer` field, which was wrongly matching the L3 extraction condition and clobbering the captured value back to empty; scoped the extraction to `cache_cross_instance_check` lines specifically), and confirmation a volatile-bypass request logs no L3 event at all. `evals`: `uv run pytest tests/` → 192 passed, 11 skipped (unaffected — this item is gateway-only); `ruff check .` → clean. Sanity-checked the correlation logic's own load-bearing exclusion rule by temporarily removing the same-instance guard from `hasOpenCrossInstanceWindow`: 7 of 11 tests failed immediately for the exact expected reason (same-instance/wrong-tenant/wrong-key cases incorrectly counted as cross-instance-avoidable), then reverted and reconfirmed all 11 pass — `git diff` shows zero trace of the temporary break.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped, non-test code. One real bug in the test I was writing myself, caught and fixed before this was considered done (see Verification performed): the new `TestHandleChatCompletionLogsL3HitWithRequestsOwnL1Key`'s key-extraction loop was accidentally matching the unrelated `chat_completion` summary log line (which also carries a `cache_layer` field on any hit) and clobbering the correctly-extracted L3 key back to empty — fixed by scoping extraction to lines whose `msg` is specifically `cache_cross_instance_check`.

**Next steps / resume point:** This closes the "instrument half" of Finding 5/item 2d exactly as scoped — the plan's own explicit next step (deciding whether to ever build a distributed/shared cache) is intentionally NOT started, and is gated on real multi-instance production traffic existing, which it does not yet. When that traffic exists: write the small, mechanical glue (parse `cache_cross_instance_check` JSON log lines into `[]cachecorrelation.Event`, call `Analyze`, look at `EffectiveHitRateLoss()`) — no design work needed, `Analyze` is already built and tested. No other Phase 2 item was touched in this pass.
## [2026-09-07] Phase 2 item 2e: Evals judge-panel interface RFC — widen judge()/Score.scorer_type

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/judge/llm_judge.py` (`CallModel` type alias; `judge()`'s `call_model` param widened to `CallModel | list[CallModel]`, normalized internally, `NotImplementedError` for a panel of >1, `ValueError` for an empty list). `evals/evals/models.py` (`ScorerType` gains `"llm_judge_panel"`; `Score`'s docstring updated). `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` (docstring correction — `judge()`/`llm_judge.py` are no longer claimed "never modified", since this RFC is exactly the first real change to that invariant; the zero-SDK-imports/fully-testable-without-a-key property is unchanged and re-stated). `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Data Model section's `Score` prose updated for the new `scorer_type` value and the `skeptic_panel`→`llm_judge_panel` rename; Tech Stack table's Judge SDKs row corrected to no longer claim `judge()`/`llm_judge.py` are untouched). New `evals/tests/test_llm_judge.py` additions (3 tests) and `evals/tests/test_models.py` addition (1 test). New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-evals-judge-panel-interface.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Phase 2 item 2e from the approved 2026 upgrade roadmap (`docs/upgrade-research/evals-2026-09-06.md`'s Finding 2) — widens `judge()`'s single-`call_model`-callable signature and `Score.scorer_type`'s closed `Literal` so a future v2 multi-judge skeptic panel is additive to these two seams instead of a retrofit, while explicitly NOT building the panel itself (reducer, cross-judge voting, aggregation logic all remain a real v2 non-goal per `PRD.md`). Directly resolves `PRD.md`'s long-open "Open Questions Carried Forward" bullet ("Whether the skeptic-panel protocol for Evals should be pairwise-comparison-based or independent-refutation-based... final call deferred to the RFC that accompanies building it") with Inspect AI's concrete `multi_scorer()` shape.

**Decisions made:** `judge()` accepts either a bare `call_model` or a `list[call_model]` — normalized internally (`panel = call_model if isinstance(call_model, list) else [call_model]`) so every existing production call site (`evals.cli`'s `_judge_with_cache`, the only real caller) and every existing test needed zero edits; verified by actually running the full suite, not assumed from the type signature. A panel of exactly one (bare or length-1 list) scores identically to before this change; a panel of more than one raises `NotImplementedError` naming the RFC — deliberately loud rather than silently scoring with only `panel[0]` and discarding the rest, matching this codebase's own "`None`/error means not applicable, never a fabricated stand-in" convention (`Run`/`Score`'s own docstrings) applied to control flow instead of a data field. `Score.scorer_type` gains `"llm_judge_panel"` as a genuinely new, distinct value (never overloading `"llm_judge"`), since `report --scores` groups and gates by `scorer_type` and blending a single-judge verdict with a future multi-judge aggregate under one label would silently merge two different reliability profiles into one gate. Named `llm_judge_panel` deliberately diverging from `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s original Data-Model-sketch spelling (`skeptic_panel`), matching Inspect AI's own naming instead — documented directly in `ARCHITECTURE.md` so the sketch and the real type don't silently diverge in name only. Panel-size/quorum decided now, not left silent, per the research report's own named open question: default reducer is majority (>50% quorum, extending Kelvran's already-shipped `--judge-axes` AND-combine logic naturally), panel starts at 2 disjoint model families (Kelvran's existing Anthropic+OpenAI dual-provider wiring in `evals/judge/providers.py` already satisfies PoLL's disjoint-families finding with zero new provider work). Deliberately did NOT add a `Score.metadata` field for Inspect AI's `score.metadata.panel` per-judge-vote pattern yet — with zero real writer today, that would be exactly the "dead code with no consumer" anti-pattern `Span`'s own docstring already rejects; deferred to the RFC that actually builds the panel. Left `PRD.md`'s own "Open Questions Carried Forward" bullet unedited (matching this project's established precedent — e.g. `docs/rfcs/2026-09-02-virtual-keys-budgets.md`'s soft-budget-cap bullet, resolved later without editing the original) rather than rewriting it in place.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -q` → 196 passed, 11 skipped (up from the pre-existing 192-passed baseline by exactly the 4 new tests added; every pre-existing test with a real `judge()`/`scorer_type` call site — `test_llm_judge.py`, `test_llm_judge_integration.py`, `test_judge_cache.py`, `test_deterministic_judge.py`, `test_providers.py`, `test_cli_integration.py`, `test_promote_integration.py`, `test_results_store.py` — passed unmodified). `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`. `uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → `3 kept, 0 broken`, unchanged. Sanity-checked the one load-bearing new behavior (the loud-failure guard against silently faking panel scoring): temporarily deleted the `len(panel) > 1: raise NotImplementedError` block — `test_judge_rejects_a_panel_of_more_than_one_call_model` failed with "DID NOT RAISE NotImplementedError", confirming the test genuinely depends on the guard; reverted, `git diff` on the file after reverting showed zero trace of the temporary break, full suite re-run green.

**Bugs found:** None — interface-widening with no pre-existing behavior to regress; one proactive doc-vs-code staleness fix along the way (`providers.py`'s docstring and `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s Tech Stack row both previously asserted "`judge()`/`llm_judge.py` themselves remain untouched" — true before this RFC, false after, corrected in this same pass rather than left to be discovered later, per `AGENTS.md`'s own promoted Gotcha about this exact recurring doc-staleness class).

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 2 item 2e is complete, verified, and committed to `phase2/2e-judge-panel-interface` (not merged — orchestrator integrates). The actual v2 multi-judge panel (reducer implementation, cross-judge voting, `Score.metadata.panel` field, a real `make_*_call_model()`-backed panel wired into `evals.cli`) remains fully unbuilt and un-scheduled — this item's own RFC is the design to build against whenever that work starts. Remaining Phase 2 items (health-probing, OTel GenAI metrics, error-type fallback chains, cross-instance cache telemetry) are gateway-side and independent of this change.
