## [2026-09-07] Phase 3, item 3a: gateway pre-completion token-counting viability research

**Files touched:** New `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-pretoken-counting-2026-09-07.md` only. No code touched — this item is a research/design pass, per the approved plan text.

**Intent/summary:** Closes `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md` Finding 4's own named "concrete next step": for each of Kelvran's 5 real adapters, does a real, current, pre-completion token-counting endpoint exist, is it fast/cheap enough to add to every request's critical path, and does calling it itself count against the provider's own rate limits — the exact risk `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-tpm-rate-limit.md` cited a tokenizer gap for when it rejected a pre-call reserve+reconcile TPM design. This item's own workflow run made zero progress across all 6 retry attempts (its worktree was auto-removed as unchanged, no branch was ever created) — completed directly in the main session instead of re-delegating to a fresh background attempt, to avoid compounding the same stall.

**Decisions made:** Confirmed, per adapter, by direct `WebFetch` against each vendor's own current, live documentation (never trusted from training-data memory): Anthropic's `count_tokens` is free and — critically — "token counting and message creation have separate and independent rate limits; usage of one does not count against the limits of the other," stated verbatim in Anthropic's own current docs — the strongest, most decisive fact in the whole report, since it directly answers the exact risk this research was tasked to check. `openaicompat`/vLLM's `/tokenize` is confirmed, by reading vLLM's own current source (`vllm/entrypoints/serve/tokenize/serving.py`), to be genuinely local CPU-side tokenizer computation (`engine_client = None` for its own "CPU-only render server" path) — no GPU/model inference involved, architecturally the cheapest of all five to call, though `openaicompat` as an adapter category cannot guarantee every self-hosted runtime an operator points it at has an equivalent endpoint (only vLLM was checked). Bedrock's `CountTokens` and Gemini's `countTokens` are both confirmed real and free/no-charge, but neither vendor's own fetched docs state whether calling them shares the same per-account rate-limit quota as real inference calls — reported honestly as genuinely unresolved (Tier 3), not guessed either way, and named as the single most consequential open question for a future pass. OpenAI is confirmed to have **no** such endpoint at all — the current live text-generation guide contains zero reference to any pre-call token-counting capability; client-side `tiktoken` remains the only answer. A secondary, real finding surfaced while reading Bedrock's docs: Bedrock's own `CountTokens` is not even internally consistent — some Anthropic-on-Bedrock models (cross-Region-inference-only) require a materially different endpoint (`bedrock-mantle`, Anthropic's own `count_tokens` shape, a different auth scheme) than the standard `bedrock-runtime` path. Concluded the honest answer is provider-dependent, not "build it" or "don't": Anthropic and vLLM/openaicompat are strong, de-risked candidates to prototype first; Bedrock/Gemini need their own rate-limit-interaction question resolved before either is provably safe; OpenAI would need either a client-side tokenizer or no pre-call reservation at all. Sketched (did not build) what a follow-on RFC revisiting the TPM RFC's rejected design would need: a per-adapter capability table, not one uniform design, plus a reconciliation step given Anthropic's own explicit "the count is an estimate, may differ slightly from the real, billed count" caveat.

**Verification performed:** No code changes to verify. Every claim in the report is either a direct citation of a fetched, dated vendor doc or a direct reference to Kelvran's own adapter source (`gateway/internal/adapter/*/*.go`, read directly to confirm exactly which real upstream API each of the 5 adapters currently calls) — the two kept visibly separate throughout, per this project's own established research-reporting discipline. No source found anywhere in this pass documents concrete latency numbers for any of the three cloud providers' own count-tokens endpoints — reported as a genuine gap, not filled with an assumed characterization.

**Bugs found:** None — research-only pass, no pre-existing code touched.

**Next steps / resume point:** This item is complete and committed directly to `main` via `phase3/3a-pretoken-counting-research` (already merged as part of this same integration pass). The two most consequential open questions — Bedrock's/Gemini's real rate-limit interaction, and real measured latency for any of the three cloud endpoints — remain genuinely unresolved and would need their own dedicated follow-up before any pre-call TPM RFC could responsibly cover all 5 adapters uniformly.

## [2026-09-07] Phase 3, item 3b: multi-dimensional rate-limit matching (per-model overrides)

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (`KeyConfig.PerModel map[string]ModelRateLimit`; `KeyLimiter.perModelBuckets`; new `AllowForModel`, `allow` (shared internal helper), `buildPerModelBuckets`, `perModelBackendKey`; `Register` updated to add/remove per-model buckets). New `limiter_test.go` additions (5 tests). `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`VirtualKeyConfig.PerModelRateLimits`, `ModelRateLimitConfig`, new `parsePerModelRateLimits`). `config_test.go` additions (2 new tests + 1 extended `TestLoadExampleConfig` assertion). `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go`/`streaming.go` (`checkRateLimit` gains a `model` parameter, threaded from both `HandleChatCompletion`/`HandleChatCompletionStream`). New `multidim_ratelimit_test.go` (2 full-pipeline tests). `gateway/internal/admin/admin.go` (`rateLimitRequest.PerModel`; wired into `upsertVirtualKeyHandler` — see Bugs found). `admin_test.go` additions (2 tests). `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (bridges `VirtualKeyConfig.PerModelRateLimits` into `ratelimit.KeyConfig.PerModel`). `gateway/config.example.yaml`, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`. New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-multi-dimensional-rate-limits.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Item 3b of the approved Phase 3 roadmap — the consumer × model dimension of Kong's multi-dimensional rate-limit matching (`docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md` Finding 5), scoped down from the report's own fuller consumer×model×provider×header×path shape per the plan's own explicit authorization. This item's own background workflow attempt was interrupted mid-run (a real `[Request interrupted by user]` event, not a prompt or logic failure) after writing all the core production code but before writing any RFC, any test, or fixing one real completeness gap — recovered directly from its worktree's uncommitted changes rather than discarded and re-run from scratch.

**Decisions made:** `AllowForModel(ctx, keyID, model)` checks `PerModel[model]` first, deciding against that override bucket instead of — never alongside — the key's own default bucket; `Allow(ctx, keyID)` becomes a thin wrapper over the same internal `allow(ctx, keyID, "")`, so every pre-existing caller of `Allow` needed zero changes. A `PerModel` entry with `Capacity <= 0` is treated as absent (falls through to the default bucket), never as "unlimited," mirroring `TPMCapacity`'s own `<= 0` convention. Enforced in BOTH in-memory and Redis mode — a real, deliberate improvement over TPM's own in-memory-only v1 scope limit: `RedisBackend.Allow`'s existing per-call `(key, capacity, refill)` signature already supported this dimension for free, so restricting it to in-memory mode the way TPM was would have been a needless scope limit with no offsetting implementation-cost savings. The Redis-mode override key (`keyID + "\x00model=" + model`) uses a NUL-byte-tagged separator specifically to avoid a real collision hazard (`"a:model=b"` vs. key `"a"`, model `"b"` under naive `":"`-joining) — mirroring `internal/cache/key.go`'s own established precedent for the identical class of problem. Both YAML (`rate_limit.per_model.<model>.{burst,refill_per_second}`) and the live Admin API (`rate_limit.per_model` on the upsert request) require both fields **positive** — no "0 means gateway default" fallback exists for a per-model entry the way it does for the key's own top-level burst/refill, so a missing/non-positive value is a real config-load or 400 error, not a silent zero-capacity always-blocked bucket.

**Verification performed:** `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean. `gofmt -l .` clean. `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`. `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean (no new component). `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing, already-documented environmental rootless-Docker failures. 5 new `internal/ratelimit` tests, 2 new `internal/gateway/controlplane` tests, 2 new full-`HandleChatCompletion`-pipeline `internal/gateway/dataplane` tests, 2 new `internal/admin` tests exercising the real live HTTP mutation surface. Sanity-checked the single most load-bearing line (the in-memory `allow()` branch's per-model bucket lookup) by temporarily disabling it (`if false && model != ""`): exactly the two in-memory-mode-dependent tests failed for the right reason, the two full-pipeline/Admin-API tests exercising the same code path also failed as expected, and — correctly — the Redis-mode test stayed green, since the break was scoped to the in-memory branch only, proving precise, non-overlapping test coverage rather than one test accidentally covering everything. Reverted; `git diff` confirmed zero trace remained.

**Bugs found:** One real, non-cosmetic completeness gap, found and fixed during recovery, not shipped over: the interrupted implementation's `admin.go` had already written the `PerModel` field, its type, and an extensive doc comment describing validation that supposedly happened in `upsertVirtualKeyHandler` — but the handler's actual body never read, validated, or wired `req.RateLimit.PerModel` into `rateLimitCfg.PerModel` at all. The live Admin API surface for this feature was completely non-functional despite reading as fully documented — caught by reading the diff directly rather than trusting the doc comments' own claims, fixed by wiring the conversion/validation into the handler, and covered by 2 new tests (one proving a POSTed override is actually enforced end-to-end, one proving a non-positive value is rejected with 400) that would have failed loudly against the original, unfixed code.

**Next steps / resume point:** Item 3b is complete, verified, and merged into `main` (part of this same integration pass). Provider/header/path matching (the rest of Kong's own multi-dimensional shape) remains named, scoped-out future work in the RFC's own Alternatives section — not cheaply addable at `checkRateLimit`'s current (virtual key, model)-only view of a request without a materially larger change.

## [2026-09-07] Phase 3, item 3c: provider-side prompt caching vs. Kelvran's own cache research

**Files touched:** New `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-provider-prompt-caching-2026-09-07.md` only. No code touched — research-only pass.

**Intent/summary:** Closes `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md` Finding 6 — the original research round found zero surviving claims on whether Anthropic/Bedrock/Gemini/OpenAI prompt/context caching is complementary to or redundant with Kelvran's own multi-layer cache, a genuine gap this pass was tasked to actually resolve. This item's own background workflow attempt was interrupted mid-run but had already committed its complete, real work to its own worktree branch before being cut off — recovered by inspecting the worktree directly rather than re-running from scratch.

**Decisions made:** Confirmed, by direct inspection of Kelvran's own code (not merely inference), the precondition every provider-side caching mechanism needs: `normalizeMessages` (feeds L2/L3 key computation) operates on a copy and never touches the caller's own `req.Messages`; `guardrails.Check` is read-only on both the pre-call and post-call paths (a `grep` for any assignment into `req.Messages[` across `internal/gateway/dataplane/` returns zero matches); `ToProvider` is byte-faithful for the plain-text case per an already-passing test. Together these prove — not just restate as plausible — that a genuine Kelvran cache miss forwards the client's exact original bytes upstream, so whatever automatic provider-side caching (OpenAI pre-GPT-5.6, Gemini implicit, Bedrock implicit) is willing to do proceeds exactly as it would for a direct client, with zero code change needed. The more consequential, previously-undiscovered finding: Kelvran's canonical `ChatRequest`/`Message`/`ContentPart` schema carries no field for Anthropic's `cache_control`, Bedrock's `cachePoint`, or GPT-5.6-on-Bedrock's `prompt_cache_breakpoint` (confirmed: a repo-wide `grep` for all three returns zero matches) — meaning three of the four providers' *opt-in* caching mechanisms are not merely unexploited but structurally unreachable through Kelvran today, since the opt-in signal has nowhere to travel through the schema. A second, smaller, genuinely-open finding: OpenAI's own docs state cache entries "live on specific machines rather than being globally shared" and an optional `prompt_cache_key` field influences routing toward a warm cache — a field Kelvran also never sets, a real but unmeasured opportunity given Kelvran already has a natural candidate value (`vk.ID`). Recommends documenting the confirmed-complementary finding now (no code change), correcting a separately-found, real doc-vs-code staleness (`gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s claim of an `additionalModelRequestFields`-style adapter escape hatch describes zero real code, confirmed by the same kind of repo-wide grep), and naming the schema-gap fix as real, cross-adapter future work needing its own RFC — explicitly not built in this research-only pass.

**Verification performed:** No code changes to verify. Every claim is either a direct fetch of a vendor's own current documentation (Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, Bedrock, all cited with exact URLs) or a direct reference to a specific Kelvran source file/line, the two kept visibly separate throughout — never blended into one unlabeled sentence, per this project's own established discipline.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped code — this pass found a real doc-vs-code staleness (the `additionalModelRequestFields` claim) but, per its own explicit research-only scope, named it for a future pass rather than fixing it here.

**Next steps / resume point:** This item is complete and merged (part of this same integration pass). A future, separate implementation item — not urgent, not built here — would add an opt-in `CacheControl`-style field to the canonical schema and thread it through Anthropic/Bedrock/GPT-5.6-on-Bedrock's `ToProvider` implementations; this needs its own RFC weighing the benefit against the real cross-adapter schema-change cost, not a rushed addition.

## [2026-09-07] Phase 3, item 3d: competitor gap research — Portkey, TensorZero, Bifrost, Helicone

**Files touched:** New `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-competitor-gaps-2026-09-07.md` only. No code touched — research-only pass.

**Intent/summary:** Closes the genuine research gap `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-2026-09-06.md`'s own Caveats/Open-questions sections named — these four competitors produced zero surviving claims in the original adversarial pass, an absence of evidence explicitly not to be read as evidence of absence. This item's own background workflow attempt was interrupted mid-run but had already committed its complete work before being cut off — recovered from its worktree branch directly.

**Decisions made:** Confirmed, via direct `WebFetch`/`gh api` retrieval against each vendor's live current documentation (substituting for Exa web search, which hit a hard rate limit on every attempted call this pass — itself a plausible, concrete explanation for why the original pass returned zero claims on these four specifically), that all four ship real, substantive, on-topic material — the original gap was a research-tooling limitation, not evidence of nothing to find. Headline finding: none of the four unify governance/cost enforcement at the level of a multi-step run the way Kelvran's `agent_run_id` already is — TensorZero's `episode_id` is the closest analog found anywhere in either research pass (a first-class, explicitly-multi-step-workflow grouping concept), and even there, TensorZero's own docs confirm budget/rate-limit enforcement scopes to tags or API-key IDs, never to `episode_id` itself — the strongest external evidence available that "agent-run-level cost/governance accountability" is a real, unclaimed differentiator, not a redundant restatement of something already shipped elsewhere. The one genuinely new, concrete, adoptable finding: Bifrost's real, shipping, Apache-2.0, Go-native hierarchical budget cascade (Customer→Team→Virtual-Key→Provider-Config, every applicable level deducted simultaneously per request, any single level's exhaustion blocks the request) is real production precedent for the exact `org→team→user→key→session` shape `DESIGN.md`/`gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` already sketch and explicitly mark target-only — Portkey's own, independent Integration→Provider cascade (via its Virtual-Keys→Model-Catalog migration) is convergent second-vendor evidence for the same pattern. Helicone's own enforcement-capable feature (client-declared, header-based rate limits) was considered and explicitly rejected as a poor fit for Kelvran's operator-controlled trust model, named as a deliberate rejection, not an oversight. Recommends adding "hierarchical/scoped virtual-key budgets" as a new Phase-4-candidate backlog item, citing Bifrost/Portkey as real external precedent for a design Kelvran had previously only sketched with zero outside validation — not built in this research-only pass.

**Verification performed:** No code changes to verify. 15 documentation pages fetched across the four vendors, cross-referenced against Kelvran's own current source (`internal/identity/identity.go`, `internal/budget/budget.go`, `internal/ratelimit/ratelimit.go`, `DESIGN.md`, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`) for an apples-to-apples comparison against `PRD.md`'s own stated differentiation thesis, not a generic feature list. Confidence framing calibrated honestly throughout: single-source-fetch claims are labeled "confirmed by direct fetch," never conflated with the original report's own 3-vote adversarial standard.

**Bugs found:** None — research-only pass, no pre-existing code touched.

**Next steps / resume point:** This item is complete and merged (part of this same integration pass). The hierarchical/scoped virtual-key budgets candidate this research surfaced remains unscheduled — Medium-Large, touches `internal/identity` and `internal/budget` directly, would need its own RFC, and has zero real production multi-tenant demand signal yet to prioritize it against.

## [2026-09-07] Phase 3, item 3g: evals production-trace-ingestion transport research

**Files touched:** New `docs/upgrade-research/evals-trace-transport-2026-09-07.md` only. No code touched in either `gateway/` or `evals/` — research-only pass.

**Intent/summary:** Closes `docs/upgrade-research/evals-2026-09-06.md` Finding 6 — the original round found no single proven transport pattern for a separate Python eval service to sample a live gateway's OTel traces, a genuine, still-open architecture question explicitly flagged as "still undecided." This item's own background workflow attempt was interrupted mid-run but had already committed its complete work to an uncommitted-but-present state in its worktree before being cut off — recovered directly and committed.

**Decisions made:** Confirmed, by direct repo inspection, Kelvran's real current shape: a single-process, 2-span trace per request (a GenAI span nested inside an outer `otelhttp` HTTP-server span, never a multi-service/multi-hop trace), `ParentBased(AlwaysSample())` (100% of requests traced today), a real, already-tested `gatewayevents_v1` structured-log field already carrying `trace_id`/`span_id`, and zero cross-service transport of any kind in Kelvran's documented topology. Compared three transports against this real shape, not a generic survey: OTel Collector tail sampling requires a mandatory two-tier, stateful architecture per the processor's own docs — real overkill given Kelvran's trace shape has no multi-hop value for tail sampling's whole reason to exist; a stateless probabilistic sampler is materially cheaper but still a wholly new deployable. A dedicated gateway-exposed sampling API has no real precedent anywhere surveyed (LiteLLM, the closest analog, is push-only; the one general precedent, Jaeger's query API, deliberately lives on a separate storage tier, never the emitting application) and would add new hot-path statefulness plus a cluster-aware-aggregation problem given Kelvran's real multi-replica Kubernetes deployment. Log-based ingestion via the already-real, already-tested `gatewayevents_v1` field is the only option where both the payload and its consumer already exist — the only missing piece is a boring, off-the-shelf log-shipping step (Vector/Fluent Bit), never touching gateway's request-serving code path. One real, load-bearing caveat named and correctly scoped to Kelvran's *current* state, not left as a vague warning: Datadog's own docs confirm trace-ID-carrying logs and traces are sampled independently in general, but this doesn't bite Kelvran *today* specifically because tracing is still at 100% — it becomes a real risk only if/when gateway ever samples spans below 100% independently of its always-on JSON logs, named as a concrete future revisit trigger, not a present problem. Recommended shipping the log-shipper option, explicitly declining both other options for now, with three concrete, non-calendar revisit triggers.

**Verification performed:** No code changes to verify. Every claim is either a direct fetch of a real, current, cited primary source (OTel's own Collector/tail-sampling/probabilistic-sampler docs, LiteLLM's logging docs, Jaeger's API docs, Langfuse's own ingestion docs, Vector's file-source docs, Datadog's logs-traces-correlation docs) or a direct reference to Kelvran's own repo state, kept visibly separate.

**Bugs found:** None — research-only pass, no pre-existing code touched.

**Next steps / resume point:** This item is complete and merged (part of this same integration pass). The concrete next step this report names: pick a destination for the recommended log shipper (object storage vs. a queue) and a sampling rule on top of it — both explicitly left as real, open decisions for whoever owns Kelvran's real log-aggregation infrastructure, not decided abstractly here.

## [2026-09-07] Phase 3, item 3h: Evals Sandbox Pool decision-record RFC (asyncio-over-Ray + real trigger, no implementation)

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-evals-sandbox-pool-deferred-decision.md` only. `evals/evals/rollout/scheduler.py` and `evals/evals/rollout/sandbox.py` are confirmed byte-identical to their pre-existing state (empty `git diff --stat` both immediately and across the full branch diff) -- this item's explicit scope was RFC-only, no Sandbox Pool, no `asyncio.gather`, no concurrency-model change.

**Intent/summary:** Closes `docs/upgrade-research/evals-2026-09-06.md` Finding 5's asyncio-vs-Ray fork and its own explicitly-left-open trigger-threshold question, per the approved plan text for this item, so it isn't re-derived from scratch whenever real concurrent-rollout demand eventually shows up.

**Decisions made:** Re-verified Inspect AI's current concurrency model live against its own docs (`inspect.aisi.org.uk/parallelism.html`, `/eval-sets.html`, fetched 2026-09-07) rather than trusting the prior research report's summary from memory, per this item's own explicit instruction -- confirmed asyncio/AnyIO (not Ray/multiprocessing/an actor framework) is still the real shipped model, and surfaced a more specific, directly-on-point config knob than the original report cited: `max_sandboxes` (Docker-provider default "twice the number of processors"), the near-exact precedent for the `max_concurrent_sandboxes` knob this RFC proposes. Also caught and named a real, honest correction to the original report's own framing: Inspect Flow's docs describe management-at-scale (specs/sweeps/log-indexing), not confirmed distributed/cluster compute -- flagged explicitly as inferred-not-confirmed rather than restated as settled fact. Resolved the report's own open trigger-threshold question (previously left as "is it a minutes threshold or a rollout-count figure?") with a compound, falsifiable proposal instead of re-parking it: a precondition (CI's `--fail-under` gate must first actually run real, Docker-sandboxed `evals rollout` cases -- verified that today's CI smoke step uses the non-sandboxed `evals run` against a 3-case fixture and never touches Docker at all, so the wall-clock quantity isn't even measurable yet) plus a two-part threshold once that precondition holds: CI wall-clock exceeding 5 minutes (a reasoned 5x multiple of `docs/testing/TESTING.md`'s own already-declared "under a minute" PR-blocking ceiling for every other integration-test row) OR the regression-tier case count exceeding 30 (cross-checked against `scheduler.py`'s own `DEFAULT_SANDBOX_TIMEOUT_S=30`'s worst-case math, landing at the same real point as the wall-clock number under realistic assumptions -- a deliberate cross-check between the two numbers, not two independently-guessed figures). The Design section pre-derives the future implementation shape without building it: a `max_concurrent_sandboxes: int = 1` keyword on `run_suite` (default reproduces today's exact sequential behavior, matching `cached_runs`/`early_stop`/`span_sink`'s own established "additive, off-by-default" convention), `asyncio.Semaphore`-bounded concurrent `run_in_sandbox()` calls (never touching `sandbox.py`'s own per-call timeout/kill logic, which stays orthogonal), and two real subtleties named explicitly for the future implementer rather than left for them to rediscover: output-ordering (pre-allocate result slots by original case index rather than append-on-completion, to keep `run_suite`'s existing order contract provably unchanged) and an early-stop tally race (concurrent cases from the same repeated-trial group need a lock around `group_tallies`, and concurrency necessarily softens -- without invalidating -- the mSPRT's "stop by exactly the Nth trial" ergonomic guarantee). Reaffirmed Ray/multiprocessing rejections are consistent with `PRD.md`'s own 10K+-rollout non-goal and `AGENTS.md`'s asyncio-for-I/O-bound-orchestration convention, not a new judgment call.

**Verification performed:** No code changes to verify -- confirmed instead that `git diff --stat evals/evals/rollout/scheduler.py evals/evals/rollout/sandbox.py` is empty (both immediately after writing the RFC and across the entire branch diff against its base commit `5099651`), and `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ -q` -> `196 passed, 11 skipped`, identical to the pre-existing baseline recorded in this file's own most recent evals-touching entry (Phase 2 item 2e) -- proving this pass introduced zero behavioral change anywhere in `evals/`.

**Bugs found:** None -- documentation-only pass, no pre-existing code touched or regressed.

**Next steps / resume point:** Item 3h is complete, verified, and committed to `phase3/3h-evals-sandbox-pool-deferred-decision` (not merged -- orchestrator integrates). The Sandbox Pool itself remains fully unbuilt and correctly un-scheduled: the next real step is wiring a genuinely Docker-sandboxed `evals rollout` regression suite into the CI `--fail-under` gate (replacing or supplementing today's non-sandboxed 3-case `evals run` smoke step) -- only after that precondition holds does this RFC's wall-clock/case-count trigger become a measurable, checkable condition rather than a still-hypothetical one.

## [2026-09-07] Next-items backlog Phase 1: gateway provider-side opt-in prompt caching (Bedrock, Anthropic, OpenAI)

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-provider-prompt-caching.md`. Code: `gateway/internal/adapter/types.go` (new `CacheControl` type; field on `Message` and `ContentPart`), `gateway/internal/adapter/anthropic/anthropic.go` (+test +golden fixture), `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/bedrock.go` (+test), `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/openai.go` (+test), `gateway/internal/adapter/gemini/gemini_test.go` (new proof-of-non-effect test only, `gemini.go` itself untouched), `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Implements the RFC-gated future item `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-provider-prompt-caching-2026-09-07.md` named: Anthropic's `cache_control`, Bedrock's `cachePoint`, and OpenAI's `prompt_cache_key` were all structurally unreachable through Kelvran because the canonical schema had no field to carry any of their opt-in markers (confirmed there by a repo-wide zero-match `grep`).

**Decisions made:** (1) Exposed a `CacheControl{TTL, Key}` marker on both `Message` and `ContentPart` — the same superset-schema pattern the multimodal RFC (`docs/rfcs/2026-09-06-gateway-multimodal-content.md`) already established for `ContentPart` itself — rather than a single request-level boolean, so Anthropic's/Bedrock's real per-block/per-section addressability isn't under-served. (2) Anthropic's `Request.System` restructured from a plain `string` to `[]SystemBlock`, critically changing the underlying behavior too: each canonical `role:"system"` message now becomes its own `SystemBlock` instead of being flattened via `strings.Join(..., "\n\n")` — without that, two system messages couldn't be marked independently. `ContentBlock` gained `CacheControl *CacheControlWire`; a message-level marker attaches to that message's own last generated block (Anthropic's own idiomatic "cache up to here" pattern) unless a more specific part-level marker already claimed that exact block. (3) Bedrock's real `cachePoint` mechanism is a *standalone checkpoint block*, not a block property — `SystemContentBlock`/`ContentBlock` both gained a `CachePoint` field, wired via a small helper (`appendCachePointIfNeeded`/`appendSystemCachePointIfNeeded`) that guards against emitting two empty-content checkpoints back-to-back, for system, tool-result, and regular message/part positions. (4) OpenAI's `prompt_cache_key` is derived *only* from a caller-supplied `CacheControl.Key` (via a new `findCacheKey` scan over messages then parts, first-match-wins) — never fabricated from a hash or a Kelvran-internal value, since a content hash would be actively counterproductive (defeats "route repeat callers to the same machine"). `openaicompat` deliberately not touched — `prompt_cache_key` is tied to OpenAI's own multi-machine hosted infrastructure. Tool-definition-level `cache_control` (Anthropic/Bedrock both support caching `tools[]` itself) deliberately left out of scope, named not silently dropped. (5) Gemini: re-confirmed directly by reading `gemini.go` in full — zero fields resembling a cache marker anywhere, both of Gemini's real mechanisms (implicit: fully automatic, zero request field; explicit: a wholly separate out-of-band `CachedContent` object-creation API) bypass the request schema entirely. Left completely untouched, proven by a new test showing `CacheControl` set anywhere in a request produces byte-identical `ToProvider` output to the same request with no marker at all.

**Verification performed:** From `gateway/`: `go build ./...` clean; `go vet ./...` clean; `gofmt -l .` empty; `go mod tidy` empty diff; `golangci-lint run ./...` → `0 issues`; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → `OK - No warnings found`; `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing, environment-only rootless-Docker failures (confirmed identical on baseline `main`). New tests: 6 in `anthropic`, 6 in `bedrock`, 3 in `openai`, 1 in `gemini`. Sanity-checked-by-breaking 5 of the most load-bearing new assertions (Gemini's unaffected-ness, Anthropic's system-restructuring, Anthropic's/Bedrock's no-overwrite/no-duplicate guards, OpenAI's `findCacheKey`) — each broke the right test for the right reason, reverted with zero trace. Existing golden fixtures updated only where the wire *shape* changed (Anthropic's `"system"` string → one-element array of blocks); Bedrock's needed zero changes. Re-verified independently by the orchestrator after merge: `go build`/`vet`/`test -race`/`golangci-lint`/`go-arch-lint`/`gofmt`/`go mod tidy` all clean, plus a full root `make verify` — identical two pre-existing Docker-only failures, nothing new.

**Bugs found:** None in pre-existing code.

**Next steps / resume point:** Merged into `main`, verified, worktree/branch cleaned up. Named-but-not-built future extensions from the RFC: tool-definition-level `cache_control`; deriving OpenAI's `prompt_cache_key` from Kelvran-internal tenant identity (`vk.ID`) instead of requiring a caller-supplied key (would need either threading tenant identity through the `Adapter` interface or a dataplane post-processing step). No production traffic exists yet to measure real cache-hit-rate impact.

## [2026-09-07] Next-items backlog Phase 2: evals production-trace ingestion via object storage (S3)

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-evals-trace-ingestion-object-storage.md`, new `docs/operations/vector-gatewayevents-s3.yaml` (real Vector DaemonSet config: `kubernetes_logs` source → `remap` transform → `sample` transform → `aws_s3` sink), new `evals/evals/ingestion/object_store.py` (`boto3`-backed, S3 only). `evals/evals/cli.py` (new `evals ingest --source s3://... --out <path>` command). `evals/.importlinter`, `evals/pyproject.toml`/`evals/uv.lock` (new `boto3>=1.35.0` — the first place `evals` talks to a cloud-provider API directly), `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/rfcs/2026-09-03-api-gatewayevents-contract.md` (dated note resolving its "stopgap transport" caveat for the object-storage leg). New tests: `evals/tests/test_object_store.py` (9 unit tests, fake-boto3-based), `evals/tests/test_ingest_integration.py` (5 CLI integration tests via `CliRunner`), `evals/tests/fixtures/gatewayevents_object_storage_sample.jsonl`.

**Intent/summary:** Closes the two explicitly-open questions `docs/upgrade-research/evals-trace-transport-2026-09-07.md` left unresolved (concrete object-storage destination and sampling rate), building against the project owner's already-made destination-class decision (object storage, not a queue) rather than re-litigating it.

**Decisions made:** S3 over GCS — reuses `gateway`'s existing Bedrock-adapter AWS IAM credential model instead of a second cloud identity system; GCS named as a structurally symmetric, not-yet-built follow-on. Layout: `gatewayevents/v1/dt=<date>/hr=<hour>/vk=<virtual_key_id>/` — version-prefixed, date+hour-partitioned, tenant-partitioned by virtual-key. Auth-failed events get a `_unauthenticated` sentinel rather than an ambiguous empty path segment. Sampling: 100% of every non-`OUTCOME_OK` outcome (rare, diagnostically valuable), 10% of `OUTCOME_OK` sampled consistently per `trace_id` — flagged explicitly as reasoned-but-uncalibrated, no real production traffic exists yet. Retention: a single 90-day S3 Lifecycle rule, no multi-tier Glacier — KISS/YAGNI, small metadata-only objects with an unpredictable-recency access pattern don't benefit from a cold tier. Vector over Fluent Bit — one directly-checkable VRL parse→sample→partition pipeline; every config surface confirmed against Vector's own current docs via Context7, not assumed. `evals ingest` re-serializes each decoded event via `google.protobuf.json_format.MessageToJson` rather than forcing `GatewayDecisionEvent` (a protobuf message) into `results_store.py`'s pydantic-only JSONL mechanism. A decode failure on one line increments an error counter and continues rather than aborting the whole run, mirroring `evals rollout`'s "one case's failure never aborts the suite" precedent.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/` → 210 passed, 11 skipped (14 net-new tests over the 196/11 baseline). `uv run ruff check .` → clean. `uvx --from import-linter lint-imports` → 3 contracts kept, 0 broken. Sanity-checked-by-breaking 4 load-bearing lines (gzip branch, scheme-check guard, decode try/except) — each broke the right test for the right reason, reverted with zero trace. One real bug found and fixed within this same pass's own new code (not pre-existing): `ingest_cmd`'s original call let a raised `ValueError` propagate unhandled through Click instead of surfacing a friendly message — fixed by wrapping in `try/except ValueError` → `click.ClickException`, matching the codebase's established `_judge_case`-style pattern; caught by the test written for that exact scenario. Re-verified independently by the orchestrator after merge: `uv sync --all-extras` (new `boto3` dependency installs cleanly), `uv run pytest tests/` → 210 passed/11 skipped, `ruff check .` clean, `lint-imports` → 3 kept/0 broken, plus a full root `make verify` (buf lint/breaking clean, no `api/` changes) — identical two pre-existing gateway Docker-only failures, nothing new.

**Bugs found:** One, in this pass's own new code — see above.

**Next steps / resume point:** Merged into `main`, verified, worktree/branch cleaned up. Explicitly left open per the new RFC's own Unresolved Questions: a GCS sibling ingestion path (mechanical follow-on, not needed until a real GCS requirement exists); real calibration of the 10% `OUTCOME_OK` sampling rate and 90-day retention window once actual production traffic exists; whether ingested events feed `evals promote` unchanged or need their own review path; reconciling the shipper config's `app: gateway` pod-label assumption against real `deploy/k8s/` manifests once they're written (they don't exist yet). **Intent/summary:** Works through the deferred Kong-style provider/header/path rate-limit matching gap `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-multi-dimensional-rate-limits.md` and `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md` both named as the remaining scope after `KeyConfig.PerModel` shipped, concretely enough to schedule real implementation later — but does not implement anything now, per this item's own explicit scope.

**Decisions made:** Re-read the real current pipeline rather than trusting the prior RFC's summary: `checkRateLimit(ctx, vk, model)` takes exactly a virtual key and model string; the raw `*http.Request` exists one layer up in `main.go` and only `Authorization`/OTel trace-context are threaded any further today; exactly one route (`/v1/chat/completions`) is registered, so path-matching has zero live discriminating power yet. Most substantive finding: `Router.Select` is a stateful smooth-WRR selector — peeking a provider for rate-limiting before real routing would consume an extra WRR tick and isn't guaranteed to match the real routing decision, making "provider" matching a genuine two-option design fork (a new non-consuming `Router.Peek` vs. reordering deployment selection earlier) rather than simple plumbing — left open rather than picked, since the right answer needs real measurement that doesn't exist yet. Recommends against building all three dimensions in one pass: header/provider should wait for a real operator use case, path should wait for a second HTTP route to exist. Config schema should extend `KeyConfig` into a rule-list shape (mirroring `PerModel`'s own "extend, don't parallel-build" precedent), not introduce a parallel section.

**Verification performed:** `git diff --stat gateway/` and `git diff --name-only main` both confirm zero `.go` files changed anywhere in the repo for this item. Every code-location claim in the RFC (`checkRateLimit`'s exact signature/line, both call sites, `main.go`'s header handling, the single registered route, `Router.Select`/`selectHealthy`'s WRR mechanics) was re-read directly from current source, not carried over from the prior RFC's own description of the same gap. Sanity-check-by-breaking does not apply — no code was written.

**Bugs found:** None — design/research pass only.

**Next steps / resume point:** Not scheduled. If/when header or provider matching is picked up as real implementation work, re-verify the `Router.Select`/WRR-cursor mechanics against the then-current `internal/router` code before committing to either the `Peek` or the reorder option. Path matching should be revisited fresh once a second HTTP route actually exists, not built blind now.

## [2026-09-07] Next-items backlog round 3, Phase 1: gateway CacheControl auto-populate for system prompts (Anthropic, Bedrock)

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-cache-control-auto-populate.md`. Code: `gateway/internal/adapter/types.go` (new `ChatRequest.DisableCacheControlAutoPopulate bool` tagged `json:"-"`, +test), `gateway/internal/adapter/anthropic/anthropic.go`/`bedrock/bedrock.go` (+`effectiveSystemCacheControl` helper each, +tests, golden fixtures updated), `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/openai_test.go`/`gemini/gemini_test.go` (existing non-effect tests extended, no new standalone ones), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (+`DeploymentConfig.DisableCacheControlAutoPopulate`, +`getBool` helper), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go`/`streaming.go` (+`Deployment.DisableCacheControlAutoPopulate`, threaded at all 3 `ToProvider` call sites), new `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/cache_control_auto_populate_test.go`, `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (config→dataplane bridging), `gateway/config.example.yaml`, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Resolves the prompt-caching RFC's own "should Kelvran auto-populate `CacheControl` on a client's behalf" Unresolved Question, per the project owner's already-decided policy (auto-mark the system prompt by default). This pass designed and implemented the concrete mechanism.

**Decisions made:** Heuristic scoped to system messages only, never user/assistant/parts/tools — a system prompt's repetition across turns is a structural guarantee of the schema itself, and Anthropic's 4-breakpoint-per-request limit plus real cache-write cost make a wrong guess non-free. Multiple system messages marked independently, no "only the last one" special-casing. Default marker is the cheapest tier (provider default TTL), never the extended tier, since it's applied without the caller asking. Precedence: an explicit, caller-supplied marker (non-nil, even zero-valued) always wins; auto-populate only fills a completely-unset system message, and only when the deployment hasn't opted out. Per-deployment opt-out defaults to `false` (auto ON) so every pre-existing deployment config is unaffected; a per-request opt-out was rejected as unnecessary since the caller already has the fully-general explicit-`CacheControl` mechanism. The opt-out reaches the adapter via a new wire-unreachable `ChatRequest` field, not by widening the `Adapter` interface — mirroring the original RFC's own rejected alternative for OpenAI's tenant-derived-key idea.

**Verification performed:** `go build`/`vet` clean, `gofmt -l .` empty, `go mod tidy` empty diff, `golangci-lint`/`go-arch-lint` clean, `go test ./... -race` clean except the same two pre-existing environment-only Docker failures (confirmed via `git stash` against the unmodified baseline). New tests across `internal/adapter`, `anthropic`, `bedrock`, `openai`, `gemini`, `controlplane`, `dataplane`. Sanity-checked-by-breaking 5 load-bearing lines (the `dataplane.go` threading line, both adapters' explicit-wins branch, `config.go`'s `getBool` wiring) — each broke the right test(s) for the right reason, each reverted clean. ## [2026-09-07] Next-items backlog round 3, Phase 2: wire evals ingest output into evals promote

**Files touched:** New `evals/evals/ingestion/mapping.py` (`gateway_decision_event_to_eval_case_and_run`), new `evals/tests/test_ingest_promote_integration.py` (5 tests). Modified: `evals/evals/cli.py` (`ingest_cmd` gains optional `--suite`/`--results` flags; `_append_case_to_suite` refactored into a batched `_append_cases_to_suite` + thin wrapper), `evals/evals/ingestion/__init__.py`, `evals/.importlinter`, `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-evals-trace-ingestion-object-storage.md` (dated `Resolved` addendum).

**Intent/summary:** Closes the trace-ingestion RFC's own open question — whether `evals ingest` output feeds `evals promote` unchanged or needs a separate review path — per the project owner's decided policy (no separate review path).

**Decisions made:** Diagnosed first rather than assuming a fit. `promote_cmd` already accepts a `Run`+`EvalCase` pair from any source (joins only on IDs, never inspects provenance), and `--tier drift_sample` already requires zero judge `Score` — so the real gap was that `ingest` never persisted anything into that shape at all, not that `promote` was too narrow. A raw `GatewayDecisionEvent` genuinely carries no prompt/completion content (confirmed against the real `.proto`), so the new mapping is honest rather than fabricating: `Run.stdout=""`, `EvalCase.reference=None`, `Run.status` derived from `Outcome`, `Run.latency_ms=0.0` (reusing the same "no genuine timing" sentinel `run_suite` already uses for a skipped/cache-hit `Run` — no model schema change needed), `EvalCase.tier` always `"drift_sample"`. New `--suite`/`--results` flags on `ingest` are strictly additive (must be given together; omitting both reproduces the exact original decode-only behavior). `promote --tier regression` deliberately still runs unchanged against ingested data (no special-casing, per policy) but can never honestly clear its own failing-`Score` precondition — proven by a test asserting the identical rejection every other source already gets.

## [2026-09-07] Next-items backlog round 3, Phase 3: gateway retry-storm DoS mitigation

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-gateway-retry-storm-mitigation.md`, new `gateway/internal/ratelimit/backoff.go`/`backoff_test.go` (`EqualJitterBackoff`, `RetryBackoff`), new `gateway/internal/ratelimit/concurrency.go`/`concurrency_test.go` (`ConcurrencyLimiter`), new `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/retry_after.go` (`RetryAfterError`, `attachRetryAfter`), new `gateway/cmd/gateway/concurrency_limit_integration_test.go`. Modified: `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback.go`/`fallback_test.go` (per-request circuit breaker + inter-hop delay, `attemptFallbackChain` gained a leading `ctx` param), `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go` (concurrency-cap wiring), `gateway/internal/identity/identity.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go`, `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go`, `gateway/config.example.yaml`.

**Intent/summary:** Closes `THREAT_MODEL.md`'s Gateway DoS row's previously-undesigned "retry storms in agentic traffic" gap, which carried no trigger condition — the one genuinely open, un-gated security item the latest backlog scan surfaced.

**What was found by reading the code first:** `attemptFallbackChain` had zero delay between hops and resolved chain targets directly via `p.deploymentsByName`, never consulting `router.IsHealthy` — meaning the already-shipped active health-probing protected the primary pick and the old single-fallback path, but **not** an explicitly-configured `fallback_chains` target. A real, previously-undocumented composition gap, closed as part of this design, not a separate fix. `writeErrorResponse` set no `Retry-After` anywhere. Nothing bounded concurrent in-flight requests per key. `agent_run_id` is confirmed entirely client-supplied, unverified Baggage — load-bearing for the concurrency-cap scoping decision below.

**Decisions made:** (a) Equal-jitter backoff (deliberately not AWS's "most effective" full jitter, specifically because full jitter has no deterministic floor to write a real-elapsed-time test against) + `Retry-After` on rate-limited/concurrency-capped/upstream-error rejections only — excludes budget-exceeded (no reset-time query exists, a wrong short value would be actively misleading) and guardrail/auth/model-not-allowed (policy decisions a retry never resolves). (b) A per-virtual-key concurrency cap, deliberately scoped to the authenticated virtual-key ID only, never `agent_run_id` — keying on an unverified, client-rotatable value would let the exact adversary this RFC targets bypass the cap trivially. Folded into the same `OUTCOME_RATE_LIMITED`/429 bucket as the existing rate limiter rather than a protobuf schema change. Wired at both `HandleChatCompletion` and `HandleChatCompletionStream` (skipping streaming would make the cap bypassable via `"stream": true`). Static-config-only in v1 — the live Admin API does not yet register a cap. (c) A per-request circuit breaker in `attemptFallbackChain`: skips any target `router.IsHealthy` already marked unhealthy (nil-guarded, so "no router at all" behaves like "never reported, default healthy," keeping every pre-existing bare-`Pipeline{}` test passing); stops the walk after 3 consecutive real failures even with targets remaining; an equal-jitter inter-hop delay before the second and later attempts only, never the first (preserving the common single-fallback case's exact pre-existing latency).

**Verification performed:** `go build`/`vet` clean, `golangci-lint` → `0 issues` (one real staticcheck SA4000 finding fixed along the way in a new test), `go-arch-lint` clean (no new component), `gofmt`/`go mod tidy` clean, `go test ./... -race -timeout 300s` clean except the same two pre-existing environment-only Docker failures. New tests at three layers, including two real-elapsed-time tests that actually sleep and measure wall-clock spacing rather than asserting on a computed value in isolation, and a real HTTP concurrency-cap integration test (3 blocked concurrent calls, a 4th rejected with 429+`Retry-After` while they're outstanding, a 5th succeeding after release, plus per-key isolation). Sanity-checked-by-breaking 3 load-bearing lines (the concurrency cap's threshold check, the fallback breaker's failure-count check, the retry-eligibility switch) — each broke the right test for the right reason, reverted clean. Re-verified independently by the orchestrator after merge (auto-merged cleanly against Phase 1's `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go`/`main.go` touches — confirmed by direct code read that both features' threading logic coexists correctly at all 3 `ToProvider` call sites): identical clean chain, plus a full root `make verify`.

**Bugs found:** One in this pass's own new integration test (not production code): the per-key-isolation test's shared mock-upstream handler blocked on the release channel for every request, not just the first, making the "different key succeeds immediately" assertion race a 60-second upstream timeout instead of proving genuine immediate success — fixed with an `atomic.Bool` guard. Separately, a real production-code composition gap (see "What was found" above) was found and closed as part of this same design, not shipped over.

**Next steps / resume point:** Merged into `main`, verified, worktree/branch cleaned up. Named future work: wiring `ConcurrencyLimiter.Register` into the live Admin API's `UpsertVirtualKey`; a cross-instance/distributed concurrency cap (single-process only today, same stepping-stone posture as this project's other rate-limiting/budget/cache-stampede features); an honest `Retry-After` for budget-exceeded once `budget.Tracker` exposes a real reset-time query; a verified/authenticated `agent_run_id` as a prerequisite before ever enforcing a per-agent-run dimension. This closes the "next items" backlog round 3 plan's Phase 0-3 in full.

## [2026-09-07] Closing the original evals research's Finding 1 gap: CI-gate tier default, category-scoped critical gate, flaky tolerance

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-evals-cigate-refinements.md`, new `evals/tests/fixtures/ci_gate_example.json`, new `evals/tests/test_report_gate_helpers.py` (15 tests), new `evals/tests/test_cigate_integration.py` (18 tests). Modified: `evals/evals/models.py` (`EvalCase.flaky`, `Score.tier`/`Score.tags`/`Score.flaky`, all backward-compatible defaults), `evals/evals/cli.py` (`report_cmd` gains `--tier`/`--category-fail-under`; new `_filter_scores_by_tier`/`_gate_eligible`/`_parse_category_fail_under` helpers; all 4 `Score(...)` construction sites in `run_cmd`/`rollout_cmd` denormalize the new fields; `promote_cmd` carries `flaky` forward), `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `evals/tests/test_models.py` (+9), `evals/tests/test_promote_integration.py` (+2).

**Intent/summary:** A cross-check of the original `docs/upgrade-research/evals-2026-09-06.md` against everything shipped since found this was the one genuinely open item with zero blocker across all three original research reports (gateway/cache/evals) — Finding 1's own three concrete recommendations (default the CI gate to `--tier regression`, a promptfoo-style category-scoped critical gate, a DeepEval-style `flaky=True` tolerance) had never been built, only the bare `--fail-under` plumbing itself (Phase 1e). Closed as one coherent pass since all three touch the same `report_cmd` gate-computation path and the same `EvalCase`/`Score` schema — three separate uncoordinated changes would have collided on the same functions.

**Investigation:** Confirmed directly before designing: `EvalCase` already had `tier`/`tags` (from the golden/regression/drift_sample promotion work); `promote_cmd` already had `--tier` but `report_cmd` had none at all; `Score` had no `tier`/`tags`/`flaky` field, so `--scores` mode had no way to know a persisted Score's case classification.

**Decisions made:** Denormalize `tier`/`tags`/`flaky` onto `Score` at write time (mirroring `cache_key`'s own "compute once, at write time" precedent) rather than joining back to a `--suite` file at report time, which would introduce a real staleness failure mode. `flaky` lives on `EvalCase` (a durable, case-level declaration mirroring DeepEval's own `LLMTestCase(flaky=True)`), not `Run`, since *why* a case is flaky doesn't change trial-to-trial. Category tagging reuses `EvalCase.tags` directly (no new field) with a recommended-but-unenforced `category:` naming convention. `--tier`/`--category-fail-under` scoped to `--scores` mode only. Category gates never blend `scorer_type`, apply within whatever `--tier` filter is active, and a category tag with zero matches hard-fails rather than silently no-op'ing. Flaky exclusion is uniform across the aggregate and every category gate — excluded from every gate *computation*, included in every printed line (an operator always sees the real numbers, flaky included, for transparency), with a `(N flaky excluded from gate)` note. `evals/evals/stats.py` deliberately left unchanged — every new gate reuses the existing `wilson_interval` call directly; a new per-category entry point would cross the `evals.models`/`evals.stats` import-linter layer boundary for no real benefit.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/` → 266 passed, 11 skipped (up from 224/11 — 42 new tests, zero regressions). `ruff check .` clean. `lint-imports` → 3 kept, 0 broken. Independently re-verified by the orchestrator after merge (not just trusting the agent's report): ran the exact new CI-equivalent command locally (`evals run --suite tests/fixtures/ci_gate_example.json --scores ...` then `evals report --scores ... --tier regression --fail-under 0.35 --category-fail-under "category:safety:0.15"`) — real exit 0. The printed aggregate line showed `(3/4)` with a Wilson lower bound of 0.3006 (below the 0.35 threshold), which looked at first like a bug — traced directly through `report_cmd`'s code and confirmed this is intentional: the printed line displays the *full* group including the flaky case for transparency, while the actual `gate_checks` entry appended just above it uses the flaky-*excluded* eligible set (3/3, lower bound 0.4385) — the real gate check that determines the exit code is entirely separate from the print statement. Sanity-checked-by-breaking all three mechanisms (`_gate_eligible`, `_filter_scores_by_tier`, the category-gate enforcement condition) — each broke real, correctly-attributed tests and a real CLI exit-code flip, each reverted with an identical `git diff --stat` before and after.

Two self-caught issues during the implementing agent's own manual verification (fixed before commit): a cosmetic double-prefixed `[category:category:safety]` print label, and a real test-suite gap (the original tests didn't actually prove a category gate fires when `--fail-under` is entirely omitted) — closed with a new dedicated test.

## [2026-09-07] Building the regression-tier eval corpus, Phase 0: taxonomy & process conventions RFC

**Files touched:** New `docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-evals-regression-corpus-conventions.md` only. No code touched — this phase is process/convention only, per the approved plan.

**Intent/summary:** Four parallel `/deep-research` passes (`docs/upgrade-research/evals-regression-dataset-{methodology,sizing,bootstrapping,taxonomy-maintenance}-2026-09-07.md`) converged on the same conclusion: the regression-tier CI-gate machinery is real and sophisticated, but the corpus itself is still a 4-5-case smoke-test fixture, and no convention existed for taxonomy, review gating, or stale-case correction. This RFC establishes those conventions before any real case is added, so Phase 1/2 (mining dev history, adversarial scanning) have a defined taxonomy to tag against from the start.

**Decisions made:** A starter `category:` taxonomy (`routing`/`cache`/`cost`/`guardrail`/`streaming`/`judge`) plus an orthogonal `provenance:` axis (`dogfood`/`hand-written`/`synthetic`) — both plain `EvalCase.tags` values, zero schema change, matching the existing `category:safety` convention exactly. A mandatory human-review checkpoint before every `evals promote --tier regression` (no automatic promotion), mirroring the strongest real precedent the research found (LangChain's candidate-then-SME-review workflow) and how `golden`-tier cases are already excluded from auto-promotion. A `deprecated`+`supersedes:<old-case-id>` tag convention for correcting a stale case without ever mutating the append-only promotion mechanism — explicitly named as original design in the RFC itself, since the taxonomy research confirmed no real platform documents this exact scenario for an append-only system (Braintrust's updates are merge-upserts against a stable ID, the opposite pattern). Reactive-trigger review cadence, not a fixed calendar — a specific tiered monthly/quarterly refresh schedule was explicitly refuted as documented practice by the same research.

**Verification performed:** Doc-only — no tests. Confirmed directly against the current `EvalCase`/`Score` schema (`evals/evals/models.py`) that `tags: list[str]` already supports every convention above with zero schema change, and against `evals promote`'s real current behavior (`evals/evals/cli.py`) that nothing in this RFC contradicts the existing promotion mechanics or the just-shipped CI-gate refinements.

**Bugs found:** None — doc-only pass.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 1 (mine dev history into hand-authored `EvalCase`s) and Phase 2 (supplement `category:guardrail` with open-source adversarial scanning) are next, dispatched via a dynamic workflow across 2 isolated worktrees, now that the taxonomy both depend on is defined. Phase 3 (wiring real CI thresholds) remains explicitly gated on reaching a meaningful case count (N≈100-150 aggregate, N≈30-50 per category) — not scheduled this round.

## [2026-09-08] Research-driven corpus growth: 28→83 cases, including two serious real TOCTOU races found and independently re-verified

**Files touched:** New `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cache_adversarial.json` (13 cases), `regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json` (12 cases), `regression_corpus_routing_chaos.json` (15 cases). `regression_corpus_dogfood.json` grown 15→30 (streaming/judge/telemetry/harness). No production code touched by any of the 4 agents — every temporary Go/Python verification script was deleted before commit, confirmed via `git status --porcelain` showing zero residual diff in each case.

**Intent/summary:** Three parallel `/deep-research` passes (cache attack patterns, cost-abuse patterns, routing/chaos patterns) gathered real, cited external material — CVEs, academic papers, production postmortems — for the categories thinnest in the corpus. A 4-agent case-authoring round then converted that research into `EvalCase`s, with an explicit instruction to verify every claim against Kelvran's *real current code*, never assume the research's framing transfers directly, and honestly document (not fix) any real gap found — matching the guardrail corpus's established pattern.

**Decisions made / findings, ranked by severity:**

1. **Two 100%-reproducible TOCTOU races — the most serious finding of this session.** `gateway/internal/budget.Tracker.Allow`/`Record` and `gateway/internal/ratelimit`'s TPM `AllowTPM`/`HasBalance`→`RecordTokens`/`Debit` are both check-then-act pairs with no lock or reservation spanning them. The cost-abuse agent proved this empirically (not just by reading code) with a temporary barrier-based concurrent-goroutine Go test — every goroutine passes the check before any goroutine records, honestly modeling the real gap being the full upstream LLM completion latency — and got 20/20 allowed against a $1 cap, real spend $20, across 5 consecutive runs. A naive (non-barriered) version of the same probe first showed no race (sanity-checking that the barrier technique itself, not a rigged test, is what surfaces this). RPM's `TokenBucket` and the Redis Lua-script backend were confirmed genuinely atomic under the identical probe. **I independently re-derived this from scratch** before trusting it: wrote my own fresh two-barrier concurrent test (not copied from the agent's), confirmed 20/20 allowed / $20 spent across 5 runs, deleted the test, confirmed zero trace via `git status --porcelain`. Not fixed in this pass — encoded as 2 of the new `regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json`'s 3 FAIL cases.
2. **Cache L3-lite negation/polarity-flip false-positive.** A query pair differing only by "withhold" vs. "administer" (or any negation/polarity-reversing edit) measures Jaccard/MinHash similarity 0.953 — above the 0.9 floor — and `dataplane.Fingerprint` (which extracts only numbers, dates, and capitalized multi-word sequences) produces an identical fingerprint for both, so the entity hard-gate offers zero protection; a shorter version of the same pair (~9 words) scores only 0.328 and correctly misses, so the bug specifically manifests as query length grows. The same mechanism causes a referent-shift false positive when the shifted referent is a common noun ("the antibiotic" vs. "the antiviral") — in contrast, the identical scenario with a *capitalized* referent is correctly rejected even at higher similarity. Verified via direct reading of `key.go`/`entities.go`/`lexical.go` plus temporary Go tests computing the real Jaccard/fingerprint values (not the research paper's cosine-similarity number, which uses a different metric and doesn't transfer). Encoded as 2 of 5 FAIL cases in the new `regression_corpus_cache_adversarial.json` (8/13 PASS overall — real, verified defenses against delimiter injection, truncated-hash collision, missing tenant discriminators, and capitalized-referent shifts).
3. **Streaming upstream caller has zero timeout anywhere.** `NewHTTPUpstreamStreamCaller` is constructed with `&http.Client{}` (zero-value `Timeout`), and `streaming.go` has zero `context.WithTimeout`/`WithDeadline`/`SetReadDeadline` calls — a stalled/delayed streaming upstream hangs indefinitely, bounded only by the client disconnecting, unlike the non-streaming path's real 60s bound. Found by the routing-chaos agent while verifying a Chaos-Monkey-style injected-delay scenario against the real code.
4. **Router health state is fed only by the synthetic probe loop — real-traffic failures never update it.** `router.ReportProbeResult` has exactly one call site in the whole codebase. Concurrently-arriving requests each independently retry an already-degrading (but not-yet-probe-confirmed-unhealthy) deployment via their own per-request circuit breaker, with zero cross-request failure-signal sharing until the next scheduled probe — a concrete pin of `router.go`'s own already-partially-named "traffic-derived circuit breaker... deferred" scope limit against this research's specific AWS-DynamoDB/Slack-2021 convergence scenarios.
5. **Zero per-deployment rate-limit/concurrency-cap construct exists anywhere** — `ratelimit`/`router` are keyed exclusively by virtual-key ID (optionally × model), never by deployment. A Slack-2021-style convergence of many *different* virtual keys onto one fallback-target deployment is bounded only by each key's own independent limits, with nothing capping that deployment's own aggregate load — a genuinely new finding, not previously named in either package's doc comments.
6. **`ConcurrencyLimiter` has no distributed counterpart** (already self-documented as a v1 scope limit in `concurrency.go`'s own doc comment, re-confirmed rather than assumed) — a multi-instance gateway enforces the cap independently per instance, unlike the Redis-backed RPM/TPM dimension.

Every real gap above was verified against Kelvran's actual current code (direct reading plus temporary, deleted-before-commit Go/Python scripts, several with an explicit sanity-check-by-breaking control proving the test harness itself can detect a real race/gap, not just report a vacuous pass) — never assumed from the research documents' own framing. Where Kelvran's shipped mechanisms correctly defended against a researched scenario, that was verified and encoded as a real PASS, not skipped.

**Verification performed:** All 4 branches merged cleanly (zero conflicts — each touched either a new file or, for the dogfood growth, the one file nothing else touched). Corpus-wide: 83 total cases across 5 files, all validated via `EvalCase.model_validate`, zero duplicate ids within or across files (independently checked by the orchestrator). Independently re-ran every fixture through the real CLI after merging: dogfood 30/30, guardrail 12/13, cache_adversarial 8/13, cost_abuse 9/12, routing_chaos 11/15 — every number matched the agents' own claims exactly. `cd gateway`: `go build`/`vet`/`test -race` clean except the same two pre-existing environment-only Docker failures (confirmed unchanged, since no production code was touched by any of the 4 agents). `cd evals`: `uv run pytest tests/` → 266 passed, 11 skipped (unchanged baseline); `ruff check .` clean; `lint-imports` → 3 kept, 0 broken. **The TOCTOU race (Finding 1) was independently re-derived from scratch by the orchestrator**, not merely trusted: wrote a fresh, differently-structured two-barrier concurrent Go test in `internal/budget`, reproduced 20/20 allowed / $20 spent across 5 runs, deleted the test, confirmed zero trace via `git status --porcelain`.

**Bugs found:** Six real, previously-undocumented gaps, ranked above by severity — none fixed in this pass, all encoded as honest FAIL cases in the new corpus files, matching the guardrail corpus's established pattern. The two TOCTOU races (Finding 1) are the highest-priority real follow-on this session has surfaced.

**Next steps / resume point:** Merged into `main`, verified, worktree/branch cleaned up. Corpus now stands at 83 cases — much closer to Phase 3's N≈100-150 aggregate target, though still short; CI wiring remains correctly unscheduled. **Highest-priority real follow-on, strongly recommended for a dedicated fix round:** the two TOCTOU races (a per-key lock spanning the whole Allow-until-Record window, or an atomic reserve-then-reconcile step, for both `budget.Tracker` and `ratelimit`'s TPM dimension). Other named, real, not-yet-fixed candidates: the streaming-caller timeout gap; whether/how real-traffic failures should feed router health state (already named as deferred pending a traffic-volume floor); a per-deployment concurrency/rate ceiling; a distributed `ConcurrencyLimiter` backend; the cache negation/polarity-awareness gap in the entity-fingerprint extractor.

## [2026-09-08] TOCTOU fix + streaming-timeout fix + cache-negation scope decision + corpus growth round (28→94 total, 4 branches merged)

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/budget/budget.go` (+`Reserve`/`Reconcile`/`reservationAmountLocked`/`billedCount`), `gateway/internal/budget/toctou_fix_test.go` (new), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/ratelimit.go` (+`ReserveTPM`/`ReconcileTPM`/`reservationAmountLocked`/`billedTokens`/`billedCount`), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (+`KeyLimiter.ReserveTPM`/`ReconcileTPM`), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/toctou_fix_test.go` (new), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go`/`streaming.go` (wire `Reserve`/`Reconcile` into `HandleChatCompletion`/`HandleChatCompletionStream`/`finalize`), `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (+`streamIdleTimeout` constant), `gateway/cmd/gateway/streaming_upstream_idle_timeout_test.go` (new), new `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-gateway-budget-ratelimit-toctou-fix.md`. `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_{cache_adversarial,cost_abuse,routing_chaos,guardrail}.json` (+11 cases, 2 TOCTOU cases bumped to revision 2/PASS, 1 streaming-timeout case bumped to revision 2/PASS).

**Intent/summary:** Direct follow-through on the prior round's own explicit recommendation: fix the two 100%-reproducible TOCTOU races before continuing corpus growth. Ran as 4 parallel worktree-isolated dynamic-workflow agents — TOCTOU fix, streaming-timeout fix, a cache-negation scope investigation, and a further corpus-growth round — merged sequentially into `main`.

**Decisions made:** The TOCTOU fix uses **reserve-then-reconcile**, not a per-key mutex spanning the whole request (which would have serialized every request per key, including the full upstream call latency): `Reserve` performs the exact same cap/balance check the old `Allow`/`HasBalance` did, but immediately follows an "allowed" result with a provisional debit of a conservative reservation estimate — all under one uninterrupted lock acquisition, closing the exact window the race lived in. `Reconcile` undoes the reservation and applies the real cost in its place, atomically, on every return path including error. The old `Allow`/`Record` and `HasBalance`/`Debit` pairs are deliberately left byte-for-byte unmodified — correct, self-contained primitives `Reserve`/`Reconcile` are built on top of, not replaced. Reservation sizing had to resolve a real tension with an already-established constraint from `docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-tpm-rate-limit.md`, which explicitly rejected pre-call reserve+reconcile for TPM specifically because no tokenizer exists in this codebase (so "estimate the real cost up front" was already ruled out as an approach): resolved via each key/bucket's own historical average real cost/tokens once any billing history exists (`billedCount > 0`), or the full remaining headroom/balance on a cold start — a named, accepted, self-resolving conservatism trade-off (a cold-start burst can never exceed the cap, and the conservatism stops the moment any real cost is reconciled), not a workaround of the tokenizer constraint. The streaming-timeout fix uses a **reset-on-progress idle window** (`context.WithCancel` + `time.AfterFunc`, reset via a wrapping `idleTimeoutReader` on every real `Read`), deliberately not a whole-call `http.Client.Timeout` — SSE is legitimately long-lived, so a whole-call bound would kill a slow-but-healthy multi-minute stream exactly as readily as a genuinely stalled one; this bounds both a pre-headers stall and a mid-stream stall without bounding total legitimate stream duration. The cache-negation investigation empirically tested 3 candidate fixes for the negation/polarity-flip gap (all reverted, none shipped) and concluded it's a genuine permanent limitation, consistent with the cache's own stated lexical-not-semantic design boundary (`docs/rfcs/2026-09-03-cache-l3-lite-lexical-hard-gated.md`) — mirrors the guardrail corpus's case 13 pattern exactly. Corpus growth added 2 real new FAIL cases (mid-stream budget-allow-not-rechecked; health-probe-no-backoff-on-repeated-failure) and 1 real new FAIL (instant-full-weight-recovery-no-ramp), plus PASS cases and guardrail/cache additions, growing the corpus 83→94.

**Verification performed:** All 4 branches merged with zero conflicts — git's line-level merge handled disjoint JSON-array-append edits across 3 fixture files and a disjoint `dataplane.go` region (TOCTOU wiring vs. streaming-timeout wiring) with no manual resolution needed; confirmed via `go build ./...` immediately after the streaming-timeout merge. Corpus-wide: all 94 cases across 5 files re-validated via `EvalCase.model_validate`, zero duplicate ids (independently checked, not trusted from Agent D's own claim). Full chains: `cd gateway`: `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go mod tidy` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → OK; `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing environment-only Docker/Redis-container failures (rootless Docker unavailable in this sandbox, confirmed unrelated). `cd evals`: `uv run pytest tests/` → 266 passed, 11 skipped; `ruff check .` → all checks passed; `lint-imports` → 3 kept, 0 broken. **Independently re-derived and verified both TOCTOU fixes myself**, not merely trusted from either fix branch's own test file: wrote two fresh, differently-constructed concurrent Go tests (a single-channel start-gate instead of the fix branch's own barrier design, different assertions — checking the final spent/token total lands exactly on the cap/balance, not just counting `allowed==true` calls) for `budget.Tracker.Reserve`/`Reconcile` and `ratelimit.TokenBucket.ReserveTPM`/`ReconcileTPM` respectively. Both confirmed cold-start admission bounded to exactly 1 of 50 concurrent callers, reproducible across 5 runs each, with the post-reconcile total landing exactly on the cap/balance. Also independently re-confirmed the OLD `Allow`/`Record` pair still races when called directly (20/20 admitted against a $1 cap, my own two-barrier construction, matching the original bug's exact shape). Sanity-checked-by-breaking the budget fix specifically: temporarily split `Reserve`'s single lock acquisition into two (check, then a separate re-lock to mutate) — my test caught the injected race (2/50 admitted) in 1 of 5 runs, confirming real detection power before restoring the fix with `cp` from a backup and confirming zero `git diff`. Both scratch test files deleted after use, confirmed via `git status --porcelain` showing zero residual diff in either package.

**Bugs found:** None new beyond what this pass fixed/scoped. The two TOCTOU races found by the prior round are now fixed and independently re-verified. The streaming-timeout gap found by the prior round is now fixed. The cache-negation gap found by the prior round is now confirmed, via direct empirical testing, to be a permanent limitation rather than an unaddressed fixable gap.
