## [2026-09-08] Post-recovery weight ramp for router health-probe recovery (branch `realfixes/router-recovery-weight-ramp`)

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/router/health.go` (+`HealthConfig.RecoveryRampSteps`/`RecoveryRampInitialPercent`, `deploymentHealth.ramping`/`rampStep`/`rampCredit`, `admitRampedTurn`, `selectHealthy` gains the ramp-admission check), `gateway/internal/router/recovery_ramp_test.go` (new, 3 tests), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (+`HealthProbeConfig.RecoveryRampSteps`/`RecoveryRampInitialPercent` and their YAML parsing), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go` (+1 test), `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (wires the two new fields into `router.New`), `gateway/config.example.yaml` (documents the two new `health_probe:` keys), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md` (+Fixed entry), `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_routing_chaos.json` (`chaos-recovery-instant-full-weight-no-ramp` bumped to `revision: 2`, `output`/`reference` updated to the real fixed behavior), `DECISIONS.md` (+entry).

**Intent/summary:** Fixed the real, previously-documented-as-FAIL gap the prior round's corpus growth found: the instant a deployment's `HealthyThreshold`-th consecutive successful probe flipped `ReportProbeResult`'s `healthy` verdict to true, `selectHealthy` treated it as 100% eligible for its full configured `Weight` immediately — no ramp, no dampening, a known real-world thundering-herd-on-recovery failure mode (Slack's 2021 postmortem, cited by the corpus case itself).

**Decisions made:** Ramp progress is driven by subsequent PROBE OUTCOMES, not wall-clock time — `router` is deliberately I/O-free with zero clock dependency anywhere (per its own package doc comment), and a probe-outcome-driven design gets "a failure during the ramp restarts it at the initial percentage" for free from the exact same consecutive-counter machinery `UnhealthyThreshold`/`HealthyThreshold` already use, without a fake-clock or real-sleep testing burden a time-based design would need. Chose linear ramp in 20-point steps (`RecoveryRampInitialPercent`=20, `RecoveryRampSteps`=4 → 20/40/60/80/100%) — round, operator-legible numbers within the requested 10-25% starting range, not a mathematically-forced choice. The weight-reduction mechanism itself (`admitRampedTurn`) is a Bresenham-style (pixel-rasterization / rate-limiter) accumulator applied in `selectHealthy` alongside the pre-existing `IsHealthy` skip, deliberately layered OUTSIDE `wrr.go`'s own smooth-WRR cursor math rather than reconstructing `modelState`'s gcd/maxW/sumW with a scaled-down integer weight for the ramping deployment — a weight-rescaling design was tried first (on paper) and rejected for two concrete reasons: the common default weight of 1 has no fractional resolution to ramp through at all, and rebuilding the schedule on every ramp-step change would have to reset the shared WRR cursor, distorting the smoothness guarantee for every OTHER deployment in the group too, not just the ramping one. A probe failure during the ramp window resets `rampStep`/`rampCredit` back to the initial percentage (still ramping, but restarting its caution) rather than letting it keep advancing on the strength of a streak that's already broken; a failure that re-trips `UnhealthyThreshold` clears `ramping` outright, since `Select` already excludes an unhealthy deployment entirely and the next recovery re-initializes ramp state fresh regardless. Added `health_probe.recovery_ramp_steps`/`recovery_ramp_initial_percent` as optional YAML keys (not left as code-only defaults) to stay consistent with `unhealthy_threshold`/`healthy_threshold`'s own operator-tunable precedent, even though the task itself didn't require it.

**Verification performed:** Derived the three required tests' exact expected counts empirically by running the real implementation (a temporary scratch test, deleted before commit) rather than hand-predicting them — the naive hand-derived expectation (a clean 1-in-5 = 20% acceptance ratio) turned out to be wrong once composed with `selectHealthy`'s existing "return immediately on first admit" retry loop (an admitted turn consumes fewer underlying `next()` calls than a rejected one, which itself falls through to the next candidate in the same outer `Select` call) — the real, reproducible steady-state ratio for two equal-weight deployments at 20% is exactly 1-in-6 (100/600), not 1-in-5; confirmed fully deterministic and reproducible via `go test -count=3`. `TestSelectFullyRampedDeploymentMatchesUnmodifiedProportionalWeightMath` reuses `TestSelectProportionalForWeightedDeployments`'s own exact 200/100/100-over-400-calls numbers as its target, proving byte-identical post-ramp weight math regardless of whatever WRR cursor phase the ramp period left behind (provable in general: any window of `sumW` consecutive raw `next()` calls contains each name exactly `weight` times, for any starting phase, since the underlying state machine is fully periodic with period `sumW` from the very first call). Sanity-checked by breaking: temporarily neutered `admitRampedTurn` to unconditionally `return true` (the pre-fix, no-ramp behavior) — `TestSelectGrantsReducedShareImmediatelyAfterRecovery` and `TestSelectRampShareIncreasesToFullWeightOverRecoveryWindow` both failed for the right reason (`counts["a"] = 300, want 100` — i.e. the deployment got its full configured share immediately), while `TestSelectFullyRampedDeploymentMatchesUnmodifiedProportionalWeightMath` correctly still passed (ramping-or-not is irrelevant once fully ramped); restored the real fix and confirmed `git diff` on `health.go` showed only the intended fix, zero breaking-trace residue. Re-verified the corpus fixture's own new `output` numbers (333/2000=16.65% immediately after recovery, 1000/2000=50.00% after the ramp completes) against the real, now-fixed code via a second temporary scratch test reproducing the case's original weight-5/weight-5/2000-call scenario at real default thresholds (`HealthConfig{}`), also deleted before commit. Full gateway chain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .` (empty), `go mod tidy` (empty diff), `golangci-lint run ./...` (0 issues), `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` (OK), `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing, environment-only rootless-Docker failures (`TestIntegrationTwoGatewayInstancesShareOneRedisRateLimit`, `redislimiter`'s own `TestMain`), confirmed pre-existing and unrelated. Full evals chain: `uv run pytest tests/` → 266 passed, 11 skipped (unchanged from baseline); `ruff check .` → all checks passed; independently re-validated the edited fixture via `EvalCase.model_validate` over all 17 entries in the file, zero duplicate ids.

**Bugs found:** None new. Confirmed my own initial mental model of the ramp's expected acceptance ratio was wrong (see Verification above) before it reached a committed test — caught by actually running the code rather than trusting the hand derivation.

**Next steps / resume point:** This closes the `chaos-recovery-instant-full-weight-no-ramp` FAIL case from the prior round's corpus growth (now `revision: 2`, PASS). Real, not-yet-fixed candidates named by earlier rounds and still open: a per-deployment rate-limit/concurrency cap; a distributed `ConcurrencyLimiter` backend; whether/how real-traffic (not just synthetic-probe) failures should feed router health state; the mid-stream budget-recheck gap and health-probe-no-backoff-on-repeated-failure gap. Branch `realfixes/router-recovery-weight-ramp` left unmerged for the orchestrator to review/merge, per this session's own instructions (no push, no PR, no merge performed here).

## [2026-09-08] Mid-stream runaway-completion guard, health-probe backoff, and corpus growth round 3 (94→112, 3 real gaps fixed + 2 new severe gaps found)

**Files touched (streaming guard):** New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streamrunaway.go`/`streamrunaway_test.go`, new `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-gateway-streaming-runaway-completion-guard.md`. Modified `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streamaccumulator.go` (+`totalContentLen()`), `streaming.go` (`streamDeploymentWithFallback`/`streamDeployment`/`streamDeploymentBedrock` gain a `keyID` param + a per-call `upstreamCtx`; runaway check added to both decode loops), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json` (case bumped revision 1→2).

**Files touched (probe backoff):** New `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/health_probe_backoff_test.go`, new `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-gateway-health-probe-backoff.md`. Modified `dataplane.go` (+`probeMu`/`probeSchedule`/`now` fields on `Pipeline`, new `probeDueDeployments`/`rescheduleDeployment`, `RunHealthProbeLoop` now calls `probeDueDeployments` instead of `ProbeDeployments`), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_routing_chaos.json` (case bumped revision 1→2).

**Files touched (corpus growth round 3):** `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_{dogfood,guardrail,cache_adversarial,cost_abuse}.json` (+5/+5/+3/+5 = +18 new cases; the routing_chaos file was deliberately left untouched by this agent per its own explicit sibling-coordination instruction, since both routing_chaos fixes land in the same file).

**Intent/summary:** Direct follow-through on the prior round's own explicit recommendation: fix the 3 real gaps that round's corpus growth found (mid-stream budget/TPM overrun, health-probe-no-backoff, instant-full-weight-recovery — the last already covered in the entry above), and continue growing the corpus toward Phase 3's N≈100-150 target. Ran as 4 parallel worktree-isolated dynamic-workflow agents, merged sequentially into `main`.

**Decisions made (streaming guard):** Since no tokenizer exists anywhere in this codebase (`docs/rfcs/2026-09-05-gateway-tpm-rate-limit.md`), the guard uses a documented, named proxy — accumulated output character length — checked once per decoded chunk batch against a ceiling of `req.MaxTokens * 4 chars/token * 10x slack multiplier` (both constants named and justified: 4 chars/token is the standard English-text heuristic; 10x accounts for the proxy's own imprecision plus "defense in depth, not primary enforcement" — a well-behaved provider already enforces `MaxTokens` as its own hard cap) when the client set `MaxTokens`, or a fixed 750,000-character absolute floor (sized comfortably above the ~100K-128K real output-token ceilings of the largest-context configured providers) when it didn't. Crossing the ceiling logs `streaming_runaway_guard_triggered` (key_id/deployment/provider/model/accumulated_chars/ceiling_chars — never a generic error masking a bug elsewhere), cancels a NEW per-call `upstreamCtx` (a child of the caller's `ctx`, never the caller's own context) so `finalize`/the post-call guardrail check are provably unaffected, and lets the stream finish via the existing `finishStreamedResponse` path — a truncated-but-valid stream, never a hang or corrupted frame. Wired into BOTH of `streaming.go`'s genuinely separate decode loops (SSE-framed and Bedrock's binary `ConverseStream`) per the task's own instruction not to assume one path covers the other; the cache-hit `writeFakeStream` replay path is deliberately untouched (no live upstream connection to guard).

**Decisions made (probe backoff):** Backoff state lives in `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane` (via new `Pipeline.probeSchedule`/`probeMu`/`now` fields), deliberately NOT `gateway/internal/router` — that package is documented as an adapter-agnostic, I/O-free leaf that must never gain a probe-scheduling concept, and `IsHealthy`/`ReportProbeResult` already expose everything the new scheduling logic needs. Interval doubles per consecutive unhealthy reschedule, capped at 8x the OPERATOR'S OWN configured interval (not a fixed absolute duration, since the interval itself is operator-tunable — an absolute cap could land at or below a longer-than-default configured cadence, inverting the whole point of backing off). `ProbeDeployments` (unconditional, every-deployment-every-call) is deliberately left unmodified as a real, still-useful "force a full pass right now" primitive existing tests already depend on; only `RunHealthProbeLoop`'s own tick-to-tick production wiring now calls the new backoff-aware `probeDueDeployments` instead. New `now func() time.Time` field on `Pipeline` mirrors `budget.Tracker.now`'s own established fake-clock-injection convention, so backoff growth is provable deterministically without sleeping on real elapsed time.

**Decisions made (corpus growth round 3):** Explicitly avoided the 3 case ids the 3 sibling fix agents owned this round, per its own coordination instruction, to guarantee zero merge conflict on those specific lines. Found 2 further genuinely serious, previously-undocumented real gaps (see `DECISIONS.md`'s matching entry for full detail): **`fallback_chains` is billed and rate-limited against the client's originally-requested model, never the model that actually served the response** (`finalize`'s cost calculation and `checkRateLimit`'s `PerModel` enforcement both key on `req.Model`, which `callDeployment` deliberately overwrites back onto `resp.Model` too) — verified end-to-end via a real `HandleChatCompletion`-level probe: a fail-over from `gpt-4o-mini` to `gpt-4o` records ~4% of the real cost, and records a fully-exhausted `PerModel` cap on `gpt-4o` as silently bypassed by the exact same fallback path; and **a single zero-width space breaks `CreditCardDetector`'s regex entirely**, downgrading a real Luhn-valid card number's `Block`-tier verdict to a mere `Warn`-tier hidden-Unicode hit — verified with a paired control case (the identical evasion against `EmailDetector` does NOT succeed, for a structural reason: no checksum on an email's local part, so a truncated match is still independently valid, unlike a credit-card number's Luhn validity). A third, lower-severity gap (`dataplane.isVolatileQuery` scanning the entire message history including the system prompt, forcing an L3-lite cache-bypass false positive whenever a system prompt merely mentions a volatility keyword) was also found and documented. None of these 3 fixed in this pass — out of scope per the task's own instructions; all 3 encoded as honest FAIL cases.

**Verification performed:** All 4 branches merged; 2 real, trivial conflicts on `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md` (independent bullet-append conflicts, both branches' entries kept side-by-side) hand-resolved — every other file (including 2 separate `regression_corpus_routing_chaos.json` edits from different branches) auto-merged with zero conflicts. `go build ./...` confirmed after each merge step. Full chains: `cd gateway`: `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go mod tidy` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → OK; `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` (including new tests in `dataplane` and `router`) except the same two pre-existing, environment-only rootless-Docker failures. `cd evals`: `uv run pytest tests/` → 266 passed, 11 skipped; `ruff check .` → all checks passed; `lint-imports` → 3 kept, 0 broken. Corpus-wide: all 112 cases across 5 files re-validated via `EvalCase.model_validate`, zero duplicate ids (independently checked). Ran each new test file verbosely to confirm real pass, not just aggregate "ok": `TestProbeDueDeploymentsBacksOffIncreasinglyWhileDeploymentStaysUnhealthy`/`ProbesHealthyDeploymentEveryIntervalWithNoBackoff`/`RecoveredDeploymentResumesNormalIntervalImmediately`, `TestStreamRunawayCharsCeiling` (5 subtests), `TestHandleChatCompletionStreamRunawayGuardCutsOffExcessiveCompletion`/`UnaffectedForOrdinaryStream`/`CutsOffExcessiveCompletionBedrock`, `TestSelectGrantsReducedShareImmediatelyAfterRecovery`/`RampShareIncreasesToFullWeightOverRecoveryWindow`/`FullyRampedDeploymentMatchesUnmodifiedProportionalWeightMath` (×3 runs each) — all pass. Independently sanity-checked the recovery-ramp fix myself (not just trusted the branch's own claimed sanity-check): temporarily neutered `admitRampedTurn` to unconditionally return `true`, confirmed `TestSelectRampShareIncreasesToFullWeightOverRecoveryWindow` failed for the exact right reason (`counts["a"] = 300, want 100` — full weight granted instantly, reproducing the pre-fix bug), then restored the real fix and confirmed `git diff` showed zero trace.

**Bugs found:** Two further genuinely serious, previously-undocumented real gaps this round (fallback-chain billing/rate-limit-bypass; credit-card ZWSP evasion), plus one lower-severity cache false-positive gap — see Decisions made above. All 3 real, honestly documented as FAIL cases, none fixed in this pass.

**Next steps / resume point:** Merged into `main`, verified, all 4 worktrees removed and all 4 branches deleted. ## [2026-09-08] fallback_chains billing/rate-limit fix + guardrail ZWSP digit-run evasion fix (both highest-priority gaps from the prior round)

**Files touched (fallback fix):** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (+`realServingModel`/`checkFallbackTargetRateLimit` helpers; `finalize`'s cost calc and `ChatCompletionResult.ResponseModel` now use `realServingModel`; `runMissPath`'s `attemptFallbackChain` call site gains a new `rateLimitOK` closure arg), `fallback.go` (`attemptFallbackChain` gains a `rateLimitOK func(model string) bool` parameter + its skip check, placed after the health check and the `consecutiveFailures` circuit breaker), `streaming.go` (same new arg at its own `attemptFallbackChain` call site), `fallback_test.go` (+`TestAttemptFallbackChainSkipsRateLimitedTargetsWithoutAttemptingThem`, 7 pre-existing call sites updated), new `crossmodel_fallback_billing_test.go` (3 `HandleChatCompletion`-level integration tests), `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`, `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json` (2 cases bumped revision 1→2, +1 new TPM-scoping FAIL case).

**Files touched (ZWSP fix):** new `gateway/internal/guardrail/normalize.go` (`stripHiddenUnicode`/`remapMatch`) + `normalize_test.go`; `creditcard.go`/`ssn.go`/`iban.go`/`phone.go` (each `Detect` now runs against the stripped text and remaps matches back to original offsets) + one new test file per detector (12 new tests total); `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md`; `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_guardrail.json` (1 case bumped revision 1→2).

**Intent/summary:** Direct follow-through on the prior round's own explicit recommendation: fix the two highest-priority real gaps that round's corpus growth found — the `fallback_chains` cross-model billing/rate-limit bypass and the credit-card zero-width-space evasion. Ran as 2 parallel worktree-isolated dynamic-workflow agents, merged sequentially into `main`.

**Decisions made (fallback fix):** `realServingModel(dep, fallbackModel)` returns `dep.Model` whenever a real deployment actually served the response, falling back to the caller-supplied `fallbackModel` otherwise — NOT `dep.Model` unconditionally, because `dep` is the zero-value `Deployment{}` on a cache hit (no deployment is ever resolved) and on a total-upstream-failure path (`runMissPath` discards the last-attempted fallback target and returns `Deployment{}`); using `dep.Model` unconditionally would have silently zeroed cost on every cache hit. For `ResponseModel` specifically, the substitution is additionally gated on `err == nil`, since streaming's `streamDeploymentWithFallback` (unlike the buffered path) returns the last-attempted non-zero `dep` even on total failure, which would otherwise mislabel `ResponseModel` with a deployment that never produced anything — violating that field's own "empty whenever no response was ever produced" invariant. `callDeployment`'s deliberate `resp.Model = req.Model` echo (the client-facing HTTP response body) is unchanged — this fix only touches internal cost/observability accounting, never what the client sees. `api/gatewayevents/v1`'s `GatewayDecisionEvent` proto was checked directly and confirmed to have no `response_model` field at all (only `requested_model`) — no proto change made; the real fix landed on `telemetry.ChatCompletionResult.ResponseModel`'s OTel span attribute instead, correcting an imprecise assumption in the original corpus case's own research_ref. `attemptFallbackChain`'s new `rateLimitOK` check is placed deliberately AFTER the health check and circuit breaker, so a target skipped for either of those reasons never wastes a real rate-limit consumption (`AllowForModel` is a real consume-on-success check, verified directly against `limiter.go`, not a peek). TPM investigated for real (`ratelimit.KeyConfig`/`limiter.go`/`ratelimit.go` read in full) and explicitly scoped out: no `PerModel` dimension exists for TPM at all today, so there's nothing to mirror, and properly fixing it needs TPM's Reserve-then-Reconcile bookkeeping re-targeted mid-chain (release/re-reserve/thread-back-out across hops) — documented as a new, honestly-scoped FAIL case rather than rushed.

**Decisions made (ZWSP fix):** Investigated whether `SSNDetector`/`IBANDetector`/`PhoneDetector` share `CreditCardDetector`'s exact structural vulnerability (a contiguous `\d`-run regex with zero tolerance for an injected zero-width character) via temporary, deleted-before-commit probe tests — confirmed all three genuinely vulnerable the identical way. Fixed all four with ONE shared mechanism (`normalize.go`) rather than four independent patches: `stripHiddenUnicode` reuses `promptinjection.go`'s existing `hiddenUnicodeRanges` set verbatim (never a second, possibly-inconsistent definition of "invisible Unicode"), and `remapMatch` converts a match found in the stripped text back to the ORIGINAL text's own byte offsets — deliberately using `origOffsets[end-1]+1`, not `origOffsets[end]`, for the end boundary, so hidden characters immediately after the match's last byte are never silently folded into the reported span. On clean input (no hidden-Unicode characters), the mapping is the identity, so all four fixed detectors are byte-for-byte behavior-identical to before on every ordinary input. `EmailDetector` investigated and confirmed structurally NOT vulnerable (already shown by the corpus's own sibling control case): an email's local part carries no checksum, so the regex engine simply re-anchors one position later after a truncated prefix and still matches independently, unlike a credit-card number's Luhn validity, which breaks the moment any digit is excised from the match.

**Verification performed:** Both branches merged; 1 real, trivial conflict on `gateway/changelog/unreleased.md` (independent bullet-append, both branches' entries kept side-by-side) hand-resolved; everything else auto-merged with zero conflicts. Full chains: `cd gateway`: `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go mod tidy` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → OK; `go test ./... -race` → every package `ok` (including new tests in `dataplane` and `guardrail`) except the same two pre-existing, environment-only rootless-Docker failures. `cd evals`: `uv run pytest tests/` → 266 passed, 11 skipped; `ruff check .` → all checks passed; `lint-imports` → 3 kept, 0 broken. Corpus-wide: 113 cases across 5 files (112 + 1 new TPM-scoping FAIL case), zero duplicate ids. Ran every new test file verbosely to confirm real pass, not just aggregate "ok": all 5 new `crossmodel_fallback_billing_test.go`/`fallback_test.go` tests, and all 15 new `normalize_test.go`/`creditcard_test.go`/`ssn_test.go`/`iban_test.go`/`phone_test.go` tests (×2-3 repeated runs) — all pass. **Independently sanity-checked the rate-limit-bypass fix myself** (not just trusted the branch's own claimed sanity-check): temporarily disabled the new `rateLimitOK` skip check inside `attemptFallbackChain` (replaced the check with a no-op), confirmed BOTH `TestAttemptFallbackChainSkipsRateLimitedTargetsWithoutAttemptingThem` (unit-level: wrong deployment called, "b" instead of skipping to "c") AND `TestHandleChatCompletionFallbackHopSkipsRateLimitedTargetButChainStillSucceeds` (integration-level: "gpt-4o-ratelimited must never be called" assertion tripped) failed for the exact right, specific reason — the rate-limited target got called anyway — then restored the real fix and confirmed `git diff` showed zero trace.

## [2026-09-08] Judge-panel reducer built (Phases 1-3, 5, 7 of the approved implementation plan) — Phases 4/6 blocked, honestly, on live provider API keys

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/models.py` (+`PanelVote`, `Score` gains `panel_votes`/`quorum_reached`), `evals/evals/judge/llm_judge.py` (+`PanelVerdict`, `reduce_panel_votes`, `judge()`'s real panel branch replacing `NotImplementedError`), `evals/evals/cli.py` (+`--llm-judge-panel` flag on `run`/`rollout`, `PanelSpec`, `_load_cached_panel_votes`, `_judge_panel_with_cache`, `_sum_panel_cost`, widened `_judge_all_axes`/`_judge_case`/`_JudgeOutcome`, `report_cmd`'s quorum-tie note), `evals/tests/test_llm_judge.py` (+16 tests), `evals/tests/test_models.py` (+4 tests), `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (+13 tests), new `.github/workflows/evals-judge-nightly.yml`, new `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md`, `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Data Model sketch + prose corrections), `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Direct follow-through on the deep-research-and-plan pipeline this session ran for the judge-panel-reducer feature: the original 2026-09-07 verdict ("do not build the panel reducer next") was blocked by a thin, unreal regression corpus; that blocker was resolved by this session's own corpus-growth work (113 real cases), and a 2026-09-08 targeted follow-up research pass found the corpus, though now real and sized, doesn't exercise the judge path at all (100% deterministic exact-match scoring). A `/plan`-mode session produced a 7-phase shovel-ready implementation plan, validated by a Plan-type subagent that caught a real design error in the research's own illustrative code sketch before it could ship (see Decisions below). This pass implements everything buildable without live provider API keys (Phases 1-3, 5, 7); Phases 4 (the judge-accuracy corpus slice) and 6 (the real disagreement-rate measurement) are explicitly, honestly left undone — both require live-verified `(output, reference)` judge verdicts this sandboxed session cannot fabricate without violating this project's own "verify, don't guess" discipline.

**Decisions made:** `PanelVote` was moved from the research's own illustrative placement (`evals.judge.llm_judge`) to `evals.models` — verified directly against `.importlinter`'s `[importlinter:contract:layers]` contract before implementing: that contract places `evals.judge.llm_judge` ABOVE `evals.models`, so `Score` (a lower-layer type) embedding a type defined in a higher layer would be a real, CI-caught layering violation. This is the first time `llm_judge.py` has ever imported from `evals.models` — a deliberate, new, one-directional dependency edge, not an accident; `import-linter`'s own contract check (re-run after the change) confirms all 3 contracts still KEPT, with the dependency count rising from 19 to 20. The reducer's tie-break policy (fail-closed: `passed=False, quorum_reached=False` on a 2-judge disagreement) was NOT redesigned — implemented exactly as the research's own "Concrete reducer design for Kelvran" section specified, since that section had already reasoned through and rejected the fail-open alternative. Two genuinely new design decisions this pass made, both left open by the research: (1) **cache-key design** — per-panelist granular caching (each panelist's key computed exactly as a standalone judge would), never a composite panel-level key, so a vote cached from a standalone `--llm-judge` run is transparently reusable inside a later `--llm-judge-panel` run for that same model id and vice versa; the panel's own reduced verdict is never itself cached. (2) **CI-scheduling design** — a new, separate nightly + `workflow_dispatch` GitHub Actions workflow, never wired into the existing per-push `ci.yml`, justified by a directly-verified fact: neither `_AnthropicCallModel.__call__` nor `_OpenAICallModel.__call__` (`evals/evals/judge/providers.py`) sets a `temperature` parameter, so a judge/panel verdict on an identical prompt is genuinely non-deterministic run-to-run — gating PR mergeability on that would be a real, self-inflicted CI-flakiness source, deliberately not fixed in this pass (changing `temperature` on the shared `providers.py` call sites would affect every existing `--llm-judge` caller too, out of scope here).

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → 294 passed (up from 266), 11 skipped, ruff clean, all 3 import-linter contracts KEPT. New tests cover: `reduce_panel_votes` (unanimous, 2-judge tie fail-closed [load-bearing], 4-judge even-split fail-closed, 3-judge majority, empty-list, bias mitigations); `judge()`'s panel branch including the concrete independent-refutation proof (two capturing fakes, asserted byte-identical prompts, neither's captured prompt contains the other's response text); `PanelVote`/`Score` model tests including a full JSON round-trip; 13 CLI integration tests (real panel scoring via fakes, disagreement fail-closed, `--llm-judge`+`--llm-judge-panel` mutual exclusivity, cost summation and its `None`-propagation when any panelist's cost is unknown, cache reuse including the cross-mode standalone→panel reuse property and the never-chain-a-hit-off-a-hit property — the latter proven by seeding a `from_cache=True` panel `Score` directly via the results store and confirming a subsequent invocation still makes fresh calls rather than incorrectly treating the stale hit as a source, `rollout` panel scoring, and `report --scores`'s separate-lines and quorum-tie-note behavior). **Sanity-checked-by-breaking:** temporarily flipped `reduce_panel_votes`'s tie-break branch to `passed=True, quorum_reached=True` (a fail-open regression); confirmed both `test_reduce_panel_votes_two_judge_tie_is_fail_closed_no_quorum` and `test_judge_panel_of_two_is_fail_closed_when_judges_disagree` failed with the exact expected wrong-value assertion (`passed`/`quorum_reached` `True` where `False` was expected), not a generic error; restored and confirmed `git diff` showed zero trace. Confirmed no live `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` are configured in this environment before starting (checked directly, not assumed) — this is why Phases 4/6 were correctly left undone rather than attempted with fabricated data. New workflow YAML validated via `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"` — parses cleanly; real verification (a live `workflow_dispatch` run) requires the not-yet-configured GitHub repo secrets.

**Bugs found:** None in existing code — this is new-feature work, not a fix pass. One process note worth recording: the research doc's own illustrative code sketch for `PanelVote`'s location would have been a real, CI-caught import-layering violation had it been implemented as literally written — caught by a Plan-type validation subagent cross-checking the sketch against `.importlinter` directly before any code was written, not after.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phases 1-3, 5, 7 of `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md`'s implementation plan are done, verified, and committed. **Phase 4 (the judge-accuracy corpus slice, `tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_judge_accuracy.json`, 24 cases per the plan's own spec) and Phase 6 (the real Anthropic-vs-OpenAI disagreement-rate measurement) remain explicitly undone** — both require a human or a keyed executor with real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` to author and live-verify the corpus, then `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` added as GitHub repo secrets before the new nightly workflow can succeed. I explicitly declined to read that credential out of Anvilry's/daily-dose's own `.env` files myself — Kelvran pushes to a public GitHub repo with active CI/CD, a materially larger blast radius than either of those projects, so copying a shared credential into it is the project owner's call to make directly, not mine to execute by reading and relaying secret material through my own context. The project owner will paste the credential into Kelvran themselves; this pass builds the code to consume it once they do, without ever touching the actual value.

**Decisions made:** Before implementing, flagged a real, load-bearing concern rather than silently complying: the project owner's requested models (Sonnet 5 + Haiku 4.5) are both Claude models — same vendor, same base architecture, same RLHF lineage — which weakens the panel's entire bias-reduction premise (a real finding from this session's own research: a cross-vendor Claude/GPT pair showed the *highest* pairwise agreement of all pairs tested in one study, so same-vendor correlation is not a theoretical worry). Presented via `AskUserQuestion`; the project owner explicitly chose to accept this tradeoff rather than add a 3rd, genuinely disjoint judge. Implemented as directed, with the tradeoff honestly documented in the RFC/changelog rather than smoothed over. Verified the exact, current Bedrock model ids directly against AWS's own live model-card documentation before writing any code (`anthropic.claude-sonnet-5`, no date suffix; `anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0`, with one — a real, asymmetric-naming detail that would have been wrong if inferred from a pattern) — never guessed. Cross-verified the real Bedrock Converse API request/response wire shape directly against this same repo's own `gateway/internal/adapter/bedrock/bedrock.go` (Kelvran's gateway already talks to this exact API in Go) rather than assumed from general knowledge. A live fetch of Bedrock's own pricing page did not surface a real, current per-token rate for either model (client-side rendered, not captured by a static fetch) — rather than guess a number, both models are left unpriced (`cost_usd=None`), mirroring this codebase's own established convention for exactly this situation, with the gap named explicitly as a clean future fix. `boto3`'s Bedrock client is synchronous, unlike the Anthropic/OpenAI SDKs — `_BedrockCallModel.__call__` wraps it in `asyncio.to_thread`, per this project's "no blocking calls inside async functions" convention. A real, honestly-named consequence of the swap: the cache-key design's cross-mode reuse property (standalone `--llm-judge` and `--llm-judge-panel` sharing a cached vote for the same model id) is now dormant — `--llm-judge` still uses direct-Anthropic-API's `DEFAULT_JUDGE_MODEL`, disjoint from the panel's two Bedrock model ids, so no real code path exercises cross-mode reuse today; the mechanism itself remains correct and would reactivate if a future single-Bedrock-judge flag were added. Fixed the CI workflow's `env` block to match the new credential requirements rather than leaving it referencing the removed `OPENAI_API_KEY` (caught and fixed immediately, not deferred as a TODO).

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → 302 passed (up from 294), 11 skipped, ruff clean, import-linter contracts still kept. Every CLI integration test that monkeypatched the old `make_anthropic_call_model`/`make_openai_call_model` factories for panel construction was updated to monkeypatch `make_bedrock_call_model` (a single factory called twice with different model ids) instead — confirmed no test silently kept testing the old, now-dead code path. **Sanity-checked-by-breaking:** temporarily emptied `_BedrockCallModel`'s text-block extraction list; confirmed 5 of 6 dependent tests failed with the exact expected "no text content block" `ValueError` (the 6th test, which specifically asserts that exact error, correctly still passed); restored with zero `git diff` trace. ## [2026-09-08] Real Bedrock model-id bug found and fixed — first live test against real AWS access

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` (`BEDROCK_SONNET_5_MODEL_ID`/`BEDROCK_HAIKU_4_5_MODEL_ID` values + docstring), `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (literal `scorer_id` assertions updated to match), `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Once the project owner pasted their real AWS Bedrock credential into `evals/.env`, I ran the panel against it for the first time this feature has ever had live AWS access. First attempt failed with `ClientError: UnrecognizedClientException: The security token included in the request is invalid` — diagnosed via a small standalone script calling the provider directly and printing only the exception type/message (AWS's own error text never echoes back credential material, so this was safe to read without ever touching or displaying the actual secret). After the project owner re-pasted the credential, that error cleared, but a second, different error surfaced: `ValidationException: Invocation of model ID ... with on-demand throughput isn't supported. Retry your request with the ID or ARN of an inference profile that contains this model.` This is a real, pre-existing bug in this session's own earlier work, not a credential problem — the bare `bedrock-runtime` model ids used for both `BEDROCK_SONNET_5_MODEL_ID`/`BEDROCK_HAIKU_4_5_MODEL_ID` had been verified only against AWS documentation, never against a real Converse call, and the docstring's claim that the bare form was "the correct default for a single-region judge workload" turned out to be an unverified assumption, not a checked fact.

**Decisions made:** Verified the fix live before committing to it, not just from documentation: searched AWS's docs for the correct inference-profile id format, then tested both the `us.`-geo-prefixed and `global.`-prefixed forms directly against the real credential — both worked. Chose `global.` over `us.` specifically so the constant never needs to track whatever region the deployer's own `AWS_REGION` happens to be set to (a geo profile's source region must match the profile's own supported region set; `global.` has no such constraint, and AWS's own docs confirm global cross-Region inference supports on-demand model inference). Confirmed the region value itself (`us-east-1`, in this case) is safe to read and print — it's plain, non-secret configuration data, the same convention this repo's own `gateway/config.example.yaml` already documents for Bedrock's `region` field — never read or printed the actual access key/secret values at any point in this diagnosis.

**Verification performed:** Ran a real, throwaway one-case suite (`/tmp/bedrock-smoke-suite.json`, deleted after) through the actual `evals run --llm-judge-panel` CLI command against the live credential — not just a raw provider call. Both judges (Sonnet 5 and Haiku 4.5, both via the corrected `global.` inference profile ids) independently reasoned about the case in genuinely different rationale text and both voted PASS; quorum reached; the panel's persisted `Score` round-tripped correctly through `--scores`. This is the first time this entire feature — built across two earlier sessions this same day — has run against real Bedrock access rather than fakes. `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → 306 passed, ruff clean, import-linter contracts still kept (2 test-file line-length fixups needed after the id strings got longer with the `global.` prefix, both wrapped, re-verified clean).

**Bugs found:** One real, load-bearing bug in this session's own earlier work (see Intent/summary) — the bare Bedrock model ids were architecturally plausible and matched AWS's documentation, but had never actually been invoked live before this pass, so the on-demand-invocation restriction went undetected until now. A useful lesson for this project's own "verify, don't guess" discipline: documentation-only verification is not equivalent to a real API call, even when the documentation itself doesn't state the failure mode explicitly.

**Next steps / resume point:** The judge-panel reducer is now genuinely live and working end-to-end against real AWS Bedrock access — not just fakes — for the first time. Phase 4 (author + live-verify the 24-case judge-accuracy corpus slice, `tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_judge_accuracy.json`) is now actually unblocked and can proceed for real. Phase 5's GitHub Actions repo secrets (`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`/`AWS_REGION`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) still need adding by the project owner before `.github/workflows/evals-judge-nightly.yml` can succeed. Once real price-table data is available, `_BEDROCK_MODEL_PRICE_PER_MTOK_USD` is still empty (named in the prior entry, unchanged).

## [2026-09-08] Phase 4 done: judge-accuracy corpus authored and live-verified against the real Bedrock panel

**Files touched:** `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_judge_accuracy.json` (new, 24 cases), `evals/tests/test_regression_corpus_fixtures.py` (new, 4 tests), `.github/workflows/evals-judge-nightly.yml` (one stale comment fixed — the corpus file it referenced as "does not exist yet" now does), `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** With real AWS Bedrock access now working (prior entry), authored the 24-case judge-accuracy corpus slice the plan's Phase 4 specified — 8 designed obviously-correct, 8 obviously-incorrect, 8 boundary/ambiguous, covering paraphrase/reorder/unit-conversion equivalence, fact-errors/contradictions/hallucinations, and partial-info/numeric-rounding/unrequested-caveat boundary cases respectively. Ran all 24 through the real, live `evals run --llm-judge-panel` CLI command (not a raw provider call, not fakes) twice, on the same day, with zero code changes between runs, specifically to distinguish a genuinely stable verdict from a lucky single sample — this codebase's providers deliberately set no `temperature`, so a single run cannot tell the two apart.

**Decisions made:** 22 of 24 cases were stable across both runs. 2 were not, and both were handled the same honest way: reclassified to `flaky: true` with BOTH runs' real verdicts and full rationale text recorded in `corpus_note`, rather than re-run a third/fourth/fifth time hoping for a "clean" result matching the original design intent — that would have been p-hacking the corpus, the exact thing this project's own verify-don't-guess discipline exists to prevent. `judgeacc-correct-units-2` (designed obviously-correct: "2.2 pounds" vs. "approximately 1 kilogram") flipped from unanimous PASS to a genuine split — Haiku 4.5's own rationale on the second run is worth noting directly: it worked through the exact same 1kg=2.2lb conversion and called it "mathematically equivalent," then still voted FAIL for changing both the unit and the precision level at once — a real, substantive judgment call, not a model glitch. `judgeacc-boundary-numeric-3` (designed boundary: "$89.99" vs. "roughly $90") flipped from a genuine quorum-tie fail-closed FAIL to unanimous PASS — updated its `corpus_note` to report both runs honestly rather than freezing the framing at "the one disagreement case" from the first run alone. Separately, 7 of the other 8 designed-boundary cases turned out unanimous under live verification and were reclassified to `flaky: false`, per the plan's own explicit Phase 4 instruction ("reclassify any that turn out unanimous") — all 16 designed-obvious cases (minus the one exception above) were also unanimous both times, confirming the corpus's "obvious" bucket is genuinely obvious for this panel. New `evals/tests/test_regression_corpus_fixtures.py` adds the first-ever corpus-wide structural validation across all 6 `regression_corpus_*.json` files (137 cases total) — no prior test checked for an `EvalCase.id` collision within or across files, which would silently break anything that joins on id.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → 310 passed (up from 306), 11 skipped, ruff clean, import-linter contracts still kept. **Sanity-checked-by-breaking:** temporarily injected a real duplicate id into the new fixture (copied case 0's id onto case 1), confirmed both new duplicate-detection tests failed with the exact expected assertion (`24 == 23`, `has a duplicate id`), restored and re-confirmed all 4 new tests pass again. Live-verified the exact CI command the nightly workflow will run (`evals run --suite tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_judge_accuracy.json --llm-judge-panel`) directly, not just inferred it would work from the fixture's shape.

**Bugs found:** None in existing code. One stale doc comment fixed in `.github/workflows/evals-judge-nightly.yml` (claimed the corpus file "does not exist yet," no longer true) — caught while reading the workflow to plan the dry-run, fixed immediately rather than left for a future reader to trip over, consistent with this repo's own recurring doc-staleness gotcha (`AGENTS.md`'s Gotchas section).

## [2026-09-08] Standalone `--llm-judge` moved to Bedrock too; 2 real bugs found and fixed verifying it live

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/cli.py` (`--llm-judge`'s `call_model`/`scorer_id`/help text now Bedrock Haiku 4.5; `_load_cached_scores` widened), `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py` (13 monkeypatches retargeted + 2 new cross-mode tests), `.github/workflows/evals-judge-nightly.yml` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` removed from `env`; `--fail-under`/`--category-fail-under` removed from the report command; comments updated), `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md`, `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The project owner still had no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and asked which Bedrock model the standalone `--llm-judge` should use instead — offered Sonnet 5, "Sonnet 4.6," or "Haiku 4.6" (both `.6` are the same typo pattern as an earlier "Haiku 4.6"/Haiku 4.5 mix-up this session already resolved once). Recommended and implemented Haiku 4.5, specifically reusing the panel's own `BEDROCK_HAIKU_4_5_MODEL_ID` constant rather than introducing a third model id — matches the pre-existing default judge's model family, and (the real reason worth recording) reactivates the cache-key design's documented-but-dormant cross-mode reuse property.

**Decisions made:** Verified the reactivation live rather than assuming it worked symmetrically. Standalone-into-panel reuse worked immediately (`_load_cached_panel_votes` already read both `llm_judge` and `llm_judge_panel` sources correctly). Panel-into-standalone reuse ("and vice versa," the RFC's own explicit claim) did NOT work — a real, pre-existing bug: `_load_cached_scores` only ever read prior `llm_judge` Scores, never a prior `llm_judge_panel` Score's own embedded `panel_votes`, so that half of the claimed property had no real code path and no real test until this exact change made the scenario live for the first time. Fixed by widening `_load_cached_scores` to synthesize a matching `llm_judge` `Score` from any matching panel vote, carrying the panel Score's own `bias_mitigations_applied` forward (an honest choice — that vote genuinely was produced under independent-refutation conditions, even though it's now being consumed by a non-panel caller). Also ran a real, live full-pipeline dry run of the entire nightly workflow (both judge legs against the finished Phase 4 corpus, then the exact report-gate command) rather than assuming removing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was the only blocker — found the gate itself was broken: `--fail-under 0.60` was calibrated on a flawed pre-corpus assumption (that "all obvious cases pass" meant all 16 obvious-bucket cases score PASS, when 8 of those 16 are deliberately wrong outputs a correct judge should FAIL). Both real judge legs scored ~42-46% pass rate while correctly classifying every single obvious case — the gate would have failed every run regardless of judge quality. Decided to remove the gate entirely rather than lower the threshold number: lowering it would only mask the real problem (raw pass_rate is the wrong metric for a corpus that's deliberately half designed-to-fail) rather than fix it; a real fix needs an accuracy-vs-designed-label metric `evals report` doesn't have today, named as separately-scoped future work.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → 312 passed (up from 310), 11 skipped, ruff clean, import-linter contracts still kept. All 13 pre-existing tests that monkeypatched the now-removed `make_anthropic_call_model` for the standalone judge path were retargeted to `make_bedrock_call_model`. 2 new tests prove both cross-mode reuse directions live: `test_run_with_llm_judge_panel_reuses_a_prior_standalone_haiku_score` (passed immediately) and `test_run_with_llm_judge_reuses_a_prior_panel_haiku_vote` (failed with 4 calls instead of 2 before the `_load_cached_scores` fix — confirmed via sanity-check-by-breaking: disabled the fix, watched the test fail for the exact expected reason, restored). Live-verified `--llm-judge` standalone against the real credential with a real one-case smoke test (real rationale text returned, `scorer_id` correctly `global.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0`). Live-verified the corrected nightly-workflow command sequence (both judge legs + the now-gate-free report call) against the real 24-case corpus end-to-end locally.

**Bugs found:** Two, both real and both found only by verifying live rather than trusting the design on paper. (1) `_load_cached_scores`'s missing panel-vote read path (see Decisions made). (2) The nightly workflow's own `--fail-under 0.60` gate, miscalibrated before the corpus it gates ever existed to check the assumption against.

**Next steps / resume point:** `evals`'s entire judge system (standalone and panel) now runs on AWS Bedrock only — zero Anthropic/OpenAI credentials needed anywhere. GitHub Actions secrets for `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`/`AWS_REGION` are already set (prior turn); `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is no longer needed as a secret at all — the nightly workflow should now succeed on its next real run. Still to do: trigger `workflow_dispatch` once manually to prove it in real CI (not just local dry run), then Phase 6 — pull that run's artifact and record the real disagreement-rate numbers. A real accuracy-vs-designed-label gate for the judge-accuracy corpus remains a separately-scoped, not-yet-built future item.

## [2026-09-08] Third real bug this round: `AWS_REGION` alone isn't honored by this project's botocore

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` (`make_bedrock_call_model` now resolves region explicitly), `evals/tests/test_providers.py` (+3 tests), `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md`, `evals/changelog/unreleased.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Triggered `.github/workflows/evals-judge-nightly.yml` via `workflow_dispatch` to prove the prior round's fixes worked in real CI, not just locally. It failed instantly, twice in a row (including after re-pushing the `AWS_REGION` secret to rule out a paste error), with `botocore.exceptions.NoRegionError: You must specify a region` — despite `AWS_REGION` being a correctly-set, byte-length-verified GitHub Actions secret.

**Decisions made:** Rather than guess at a fix, reproduced the exact failure locally on purpose: setting `AWS_CONFIG_FILE=/dev/null AWS_SHARED_CREDENTIALS_FILE=/dev/null` for one throwaway Python process (never touching the real `~/.aws/config` file) reproduced the identical `NoRegionError` with only `AWS_REGION` set. This revealed a real, uncomfortable fact: every prior "it works" claim this session about Bedrock credentials was run against a local machine that has its own `~/.aws/config` with `region = us-east-1` under `[default]` — completely unrelated to `evals`'s own env-var-based configuration, but silently providing the region botocore actually needed all along. Confirmed the real fix directly: with `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` set instead of `AWS_REGION` (same config-file-free test), the call succeeded — this project's pinned botocore (1.43.89) genuinely does not honor `AWS_REGION` alone for a bare `boto3.client(region_name=None)` call, contradicting AWS's own newer-SDK convention and this session's own prior, never-actually-verified assumption. Fixed by having `make_bedrock_call_model` resolve region itself (`region_name` param, else `AWS_REGION`, else `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION`) and pass a real, non-`None` value to `boto3.client(...)`, rather than delegate to botocore's own env-var scan.

**Verification performed:** `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --with-editable . --from import-linter lint-imports` → 315 passed (up from 312), 11 skipped, ruff clean, import-linter contracts still kept. New tests mock `boto3.client` directly to capture the exact `region_name` it receives — proving `AWS_REGION` resolution, `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` fallback, and explicit-param precedence over both, as three separate cases. **Sanity-checked-by-breaking:** reverted the fix (region_name back to a bare `None` passthrough), confirmed 2 of the 3 new tests failed for the exact expected reason (`None` captured instead of the real region string; the third, testing explicit-param precedence, correctly still passed since that path never depended on the fix), restored with zero `git diff` trace. Re-ran the exact config-file-free reproduction locally after the fix landed — confirmed success.

**Bugs found:** One, real and load-bearing, found only by testing against a genuinely clean environment (real CI) rather than a local machine with its own pre-existing AWS config as an unnoticed safety net. A useful lesson for this project's own "verify, don't guess" discipline, layered on top of the one from two entries ago: verifying locally is not equivalent to verifying in the actual target environment, even when the local test appears to pass for the right-looking reason.

**Next steps / resume point:** Push this fix, then trigger `workflow_dispatch` again — this should be the fix that finally lets the nightly workflow succeed for real in CI. Once it does, Phase 6 (pull the artifact, record the real disagreement-rate numbers) can finally proceed.

## [2026-09-09] 6 parallel deep-research passes on remaining real gaps; Phase 1 of the consolidated plan (isVolatileQuery fix + 2 deferral decisions recorded)

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`isVolatileQuery` role-scoped), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/lexical_cache_test.go` (+1 test), `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json` (`corpus_note` update), `docs/rfcs/2026-09-08-evals-judge-panel-reducer.md` (cross-reference addendum), `DECISIONS.md`, `docs/upgrade-research/*-2026-09-09.md` (6 new files, one per research pass).

**Intent/summary:** With the judge-panel-reducer fully closed, the user asked for the next ideal steps for Kelvran, then explicitly asked for a `/deep-research` pass on each remaining candidate — in parallel, before any implementation — rather than acting on intuition. 6 topics were researched in parallel (each grounded directly in Kelvran's own real code before researching external precedent): the TPM PerModel-for-fallback-hops gap, a per-deployment concurrency/rate ceiling, a distributed `ConcurrencyLimiter` backend, real-traffic failures feeding router health state, `temperature=0` pinning for judge determinism, and `isVolatileQuery`'s system-prompt-scope cache bug. All 6 findings were consolidated into one phased implementation plan (Plan Mode), with a Plan-type subagent used to work out the concrete Go implementation detail for the most complex item (the per-deployment concurrency/rate ceiling) before the plan was finalized and approved. This entry covers Phase 1 of that plan — the two items small and validated enough to ship immediately.

**Decisions made:** `isVolatileQuery` (dataplane.go) scanned every message including `role: "system"` against a volatility-keyword regex to decide whether to bypass Cache L3-lite — a static system prompt containing any of those words permanently disabled L3 for the whole deployment regardless of the actual user query. Fixed by scoping the scan to `role == "user"` only, per `docs/upgrade-research/gateway-cache-volatility-scope-2026-09-09.md`'s finding that two production semantic-cache vendors (Portkey, GPTCache) independently converge on exactly this scoping for per-request cache-eligibility decisions. A pure narrowing — can only make L3 available in more cases, never fewer — so it doesn't weaken any of L3's actual hard gates (`Fingerprint`, `freshnessRiskModel`), consistent with `AGENTS.md`'s rule against weakening those. Separately, two research passes concluded "do not build the thing that was asked about," each with a real, specific reason, and both were recorded as informed decisions rather than silently dropped: the TPM PerModel-for-fallback-hops gap (no production gateway surveyed has solved reservation-transfer bookkeeping across a fallback hop; real exposure already bounded by existing controls) and blanket `temperature=0` pinning on the shared judge provider call sites (a real, measured quality cost on boundary-case grading that the corpus's own hard subset would hit hardest).

**Verification performed:** New test `TestHandleChatCompletionVolatileKeywordInSystemPromptDoesNotBypassL3` (lexical_cache_test.go) proves a static system prompt containing a volatility keyword no longer bypasses L3 when the user message is a lexical near-duplicate with no volatility content of its own; the existing `TestHandleChatCompletionVolatileQueryNeverHitsL3` (user-message-triggers-bypass) still passes unchanged, proving no regression. **Sanity-checked-by-breaking:** temporarily reverted the role-scoping fix, confirmed the new test failed with the exact expected wrong-count assertion (2 upstream calls instead of 1), restored with zero `git diff` trace. `cd gateway && go build ./... && go test ./...` → clean except the same two pre-existing Docker-only failures (`redislimiter`'s container-dependent `TestMain`, one `cmd/gateway` test needing Redis); `golangci-lint run ./internal/gateway/dataplane/...` → 0 issues. `cd evals && uv run pytest tests/test_regression_corpus_fixtures.py` → all 4 corpus-wide structural checks still pass after the `corpus_note` update (verdict/revision correctly left unchanged — a documentation addition, not a verdict change).

**Bugs found:** One real, previously-undiagnosed bug fixed (`isVolatileQuery`'s role-scoping), found via external research rather than internal code review — the research explicitly named it as a bug class with real production precedent for the fix (Portkey, GPTCache), not merely a hypothetical concern.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 1 of the consolidated plan (`/Users/sairamugge/.claude/plans/snuggly-exploring-finch.md`) is done. Phase 2 (per-deployment concurrency + rate ceiling — the single most externally-validated new feature across all 6 research passes) is next: build a deployment-scoped `ConcurrencyLimiter`/`KeyLimiter` sibling pair, keyed by `DeploymentConfig.Name`, acquired/released at every hop of `attemptFallbackChain`, with a new `DeploymentCapacityError` mapping to a client-facing 503 distinct from the existing 429 bucket. Phase 3 (inert router-health filter primitives) and Phase 4 (optional smaller follow-ups) follow. Not yet committed/pushed as of this entry.

## [2026-09-09] Phase 2 of the consolidated plan: per-deployment concurrency + rate ceiling shipped

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (`HasLimit` + `configs` populated in in-memory mode too), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`checkDeploymentConcurrency`/`releaseDeploymentConcurrency`/`checkDeploymentRateLimit`/`checkDeploymentCapacity`/`callDeploymentWithCapacityCheck`, new `Config`/`Pipeline` fields, `runMissPath` call sites), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (`streamDeploymentWithCapacityCheck`, matching call sites), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback.go` (`DeploymentCapacityError`, `classifyFallbackError`'s new branch, `attemptFallbackChain`'s new `deploymentCapacityOK` parameter), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback_test.go` (`alwaysAllowDeploymentCapacity` + new table entries), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/deployment_capacity_test.go` (new file, 11 tests), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`DeploymentConfig`'s new `rate_limit:` fields, `parseDeploymentRateLimit`), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go` (+5 tests), `gateway/config.example.yaml` (new deployment `rate_limit:` example), `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (`buildPipeline`'s two new `dataplane.Config` fields, `writeErrorResponse`'s 503 case), `gateway/cmd/gateway/error_response_test.go` (new file, 3 tests), `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Phase 2 of the plan approved after the 6-pass research round (`/Users/sairamugge/.claude/plans/snuggly-exploring-finch.md`) — the single most externally-validated new feature across all 6 passes (LiteLLM, Envoy, Kong, Stripe, Google's SRE book all independently converge on the same design). Most of the production code (the `checkDeploymentConcurrency`/`checkDeploymentRateLimit`/`callDeploymentWithCapacityCheck` wiring, the `DeploymentCapacityError` type, the `attemptFallbackChain` signature change, and the `config.go`/`main.go` wiring) had already been implemented and was mid-verification when this entry's own session picked up; this entry covers finishing it: closing the last `main.go` wiring gap (the `dataplane.Config{}` literal wasn't yet passing the built `DeploymentConcurrency`/`DeploymentLimiter` values into `NewPipeline`), adding the `config.example.yaml` documentation and `config_test.go` coverage for the new `rate_limit:` schema, and writing the full test suite the plan's own Tests section called for.

**Decisions made:** Reused `ratelimit.ConcurrencyLimiter`/`ratelimit.KeyLimiter` exactly as-is, keyed by deployment name instead of virtual-key ID — no new limiter types needed, since neither existing type has anything key-specific baked into its implementation. Acquired/released the deployment-scoped concurrency slot PER HOP (unlike the per-key slot, held for the whole request) — a request that moves from one deployment to another via fallback must not keep the original deployment's slot pinned. `DeploymentCapacityError` maps to 503, deliberately kept out of the existing 429 (`ErrRateLimited`/`ErrConcurrencyLimitExceeded`) bucket, since a deployment-capacity rejection is a backend condition the calling client's own rate limit/concurrency cap has nothing to do with. `probeOneDeployment`/`ProbeDeployments` deliberately still call `callDeployment` directly, bypassing the new capacity wrapper entirely — a synthetic health probe getting capacity-rejected would incorrectly mark a healthy-but-busy deployment unhealthy. Deployment-scoped TPM fields are parsed/validated in `config.go` now (so the YAML schema doesn't need a second breaking change later) but deliberately not wired to any enforcement — real TPM accounting needs per-hop reserve/reconcile bookkeeping, the same unsolved-anywhere complexity Phase 1's TPM-fallback research already found.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean. `go test ./...` → every package `ok` except the same two pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker failures (confirmed unrelated — both are Redis-testcontainer-dependent tests that fail identically on unmodified `main`). New `deployment_capacity_test.go` (11 tests): the load-bearing regression proof that an unconfigured deployment is never denied even with a real, non-nil, already-populated `*KeyLimiter` wired in; the mirror proof that a genuinely configured ceiling IS enforced; concurrency-ceiling enforcement; slot-leak proofs on both the success and error path of `callDeploymentWithCapacityCheck`; a real `attemptFallbackChain` skip-without-attempting proof backed by actual limiter state (not a stub closure); a full `HandleChatCompletion`-level integration test with two real virtual keys racing for one shared deployment's only concurrency slot via a blocking channel (the core end-to-end proof the plan called for); a capacity-constrained-hop-tries-next-hop full-chain test; and a streaming symmetry test. New `error_response_test.go` (3 tests) directly proves `writeErrorResponse` maps a bare AND a wrapped `*DeploymentCapacityError` to 503, and never to 429. New `config_test.go` tests (5) prove the `rate_limit:` schema parses correctly, defaults to all-zero (unconfigured) when omitted, and rejects a half-set burst/refill or TPM pair at load time. **Sanity-checked-by-breaking the single most safety-critical fix**: temporarily removed the `HasLimit` check from `checkDeploymentRateLimit`, re-ran the regression test, confirmed it failed with the exact predicted false-deny (`checkDeploymentRateLimit(never-configured) = false, want true`), then restored and re-verified green.

**Bugs found:** None new in this session's own continuation (the severe zero-capacity-bucket bug was found and fixed in the prior session segment, before this entry) — this entry's own work is completing the wiring, writing the deferred test suite, and proving the earlier fix with a dedicated, sanity-checked-by-breaking regression test.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 2 is code-complete, fully tested, and verified — not yet committed/pushed as of this entry (that, plus watching CI green, is the immediate next step). After that: Phase 3 (build, but leave unwired, the router-health real-traffic filter/volume-floor primitives) and Phase 4 (optional smaller follow-ups — PerModel-for-TPM direct-path extension; a scoped nightly-only `temperature=0` experiment), per the approved plan's own ordering.

## [2026-09-09] Phase 3 of the consolidated plan: router-health real-traffic filter/volume-floor primitives, built but deliberately unwired

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback.go` (`isCandidateHealthFailure`), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/fallback_test.go` (`TestIsCandidateHealthFailure`, 15 cases), `gateway/internal/router/samplewindow.go` (new file, `SampleWindow`), `gateway/internal/router/samplewindow_test.go` (new file, 6 tests), `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Phase 3 of the plan approved after the 6-pass research round. The research (`docs/upgrade-research/gateway-router-health-real-traffic-2026-09-09.md`) explicitly recommended NOT wiring real-request outcomes into `Router.ReportProbeResult` yet — every production system studied gates a passive health signal behind a real traffic-volume floor Kelvran doesn't have — but named a concrete, defensible "build the how now, defer the when" first slice: a status-class/origin filter and a minimum-sample-size gate, both as real, tested, but genuinely inert primitives.

**Decisions made:** Built `isCandidateHealthFailure` co-located with the existing `classifyFallbackError` in `fallback.go`, deliberately reusing `*UpstreamHTTPError`/`*DeploymentCapacityError` rather than inventing a second classifier — a candidate backend-health signal is a real 5xx or a local/connection-level failure (never got a response at all), never an ordinary 4xx, and never Kelvran's own `DeploymentCapacityError` (a self-throttling rejection, not evidence the deployment itself is unhealthy — excluding this was a deliberate design call beyond what the research doc explicitly named, since it's a real gap a naive implementation reusing only `UpstreamHTTPError`'s status code would have missed entirely: `DeploymentCapacityError` never wraps an `UpstreamHTTPError` at all, so a status-code-only check would have silently treated it as a local-origin failure and wrongly counted it). Built `router.SampleWindow` as a deliberately clock-free, window-boundary-free rolling counter (plus an explicit `Reset` a future caller can use to compose a real time- or request-boxed window) — matching `router`'s own established "I/O-free, no wall-clock dependency" design rather than introducing the package's first clock dependency for this. Neither primitive is wired to any real call site, `ReportProbeResult`, or the request-handling path in `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go` — both exist purely as tested-and-ready building blocks, exactly as the research's own "smallest, most defensible first slice" recommendation specified.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean; `gofmt -l .` clean; `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go test ./...` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing rootless-Docker failure (`redislimiter`'s `TestMain`; the `cmd/gateway` Redis-integration test wasn't touched by this pass at all, so it wasn't re-run). New tests: `TestIsCandidateHealthFailure` (15 subtests covering nil, every 5xx/4xx boundary, content-policy/context-window 4xx, a bare and wrapped `DeploymentCapacityError`, a local/connection error, and wrapped 5xx/4xx through `errors.As`); `samplewindow_test.go` (6 tests: below-floor, at-and-above-floor, never-recorded, zero/negative-floor-means-unrestricted, per-deployment independence, `Reset`).

**Bugs found:** None — this is new, additive, unwired code with no existing behavior to regress.

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 3 is done. Per the approved plan (`/Users/sairamugge/.claude/plans/snuggly-exploring-finch.md`), **Phase 4** is next — optional, lower-value than Phases 1-3: extending `PerModel` to TPM for the direct (non-fallback) path only, and/or a scoped nightly-only `temperature=0` experiment for the judge-accuracy corpus specifically (never the shared judge provider call sites). Both are explicitly optional per the plan — offered, not required. Not yet committed/pushed as of this entry.

## [2026-09-09] Phase 4 of the consolidated plan: PerModel-for-TPM shipped (4a); nightly temperature=0 experiment decided against (4b)

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (`ModelRateLimit.TPMCapacity`/`TPMRefillPerSecond`, `perModelTPMBuckets`, `buildPerModelTPMBuckets`, `ReserveTPM`/`ReconcileTPM` gain a `model` param, new `resolveTPMBucket` helper, `Register` upserts/deletes the new bucket map), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter_test.go` (+8 tests), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (2 call sites updated: `checkRateLimit`'s `ReserveTPM`, `finalize`'s `ReconcileTPM`), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/tpm_ratelimit_test.go` (+1 full-pipeline test, +1 helper), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go` (`ModelRateLimitConfig`'s new TPM fields, `parsePerModelRateLimits` validation), `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go` (+3 tests, +1 extended assertion), `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go` (bridging), `gateway/internal/admin/admin.go` (`perModelRateLimitRequest`'s new fields + validation + bridging), `gateway/internal/admin/admin_test.go` (+2 tests), `gateway/config.example.yaml` (documented example), `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Phase 4 is the plan's own explicitly-optional final phase. 4a (PerModel-for-TPM) is real, well-scoped, additive work — extends the already-shipped RPM PerModel override to the TPM dimension for the direct (non-fallback) path. 4b (a scoped nightly-only `temperature=0` experiment) required first checking whether Bedrock's Converse API exposes a `seed` parameter to pair with it, per the plan's own explicit prerequisite — that check is what this entry's own research resolved.

**Decisions made:** 4a's design mirrors `AllowForModel`'s existing RPM-dimension pattern exactly: a per-model TPM bucket, checked first, falling back to the key's own key-level default TPM bucket when no override exists for that model (or model is `""`) — implemented via a single shared `resolveTPMBucket(keyID, model)` helper `ReserveTPM`/`ReconcileTPM` both call, so a reservation and its later reconciliation can never resolve to two different buckets. Deliberately did NOT touch `checkFallbackTargetRateLimit` — TPM stays scoped to the single pre-routing reservation, matching the already-recorded TPM-fallback-hop deferral (Phase 1). Config-side, the new per-model TPM pair is OPTIONAL (unlike the already-mandatory burst/refill_per_second): an entry may set RPM-only or RPM+TPM together, never TPM-only, and TPM's own two fields must be set together or neither, mirroring the deployment-level `rate_limit.tpm_capacity` convention already shipped in Phase 2. For 4b: fetched AWS's own current Converse API reference directly (not from training memory) — `InferenceConfiguration` has exactly 4 fields (`maxTokens`, `stopSequences`, `temperature`, `topP`), no `seed`. Separately fetched Anthropic's own Messages API reference for the actual model family in use (Claude via Bedrock) — no `seed` parameter there either, and `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k` are now deprecated for models released after Claude Opus 4.6 (fixed-value-only for backwards compatibility, all else rejected with 400). Concluded 4b's entire premise doesn't hold — decided against implementing it, recorded as a decision with the real evidence, not silently dropped.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean; `gofmt -l .` clean; `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go clean -testcache && go test ./... -race` (ratelimit/gateway/dataplane/admin) → all clean; full-module `go test ./...` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing rootless-Docker failure. New tests: 8 in `internal/ratelimit` (per-model TPM bucket independence, byte-identical-when-unconfigured, non-positive-treated-as-absent, reconcile-credits-the-same-bucket-it-reserved-from, no-op-when-unconfigured, Register upsert/delete), 1 full-pipeline test in `dataplane` (`TestHandleChatCompletionPerModelTPMExhaustsIndependentlyOfDefaultBucket` — gpt-4o's own small override exhausts after 2 real calls while gpt-4o-mini, same virtual key, no override, keeps succeeding against the much larger default bucket), 3 in `controlplane` (parses correctly, defaults to zero when omitted, rejects a half-set TPM pair), 2 in `internal/admin` (live Admin-API-configured override enforced end-to-end through a real `HandleChatCompletion` call — confirmed via the real `OUTCOME_RATE_LIMITED` log line, not just a bare error check; half-set pair rejected with 400). **Sanity-checked-by-breaking**: temporarily made `resolveTPMBucket` always fall through to the default bucket regardless of `model` — every one of the 3 layers (unit tests, full-pipeline test) failed decisively for the exact predicted reason, then restored and re-verified fully green.

**Bugs found:** None — this is new, additive functionality with no existing behavior to regress (confirmed: `AllowTPM`/`RecordTokens`, the older non-reserving pair, are called nowhere in production code, so no third call site needed updating).

**Next steps / resume point:** Phase 4 is done — 4a shipped, 4b decided against with real evidence. This closes the ENTIRE consolidated plan from the original 6-parallel-deep-research round (`/Users/sairamugge/.claude/plans/snuggly-exploring-finch.md`): all 4 phases are now either shipped or deliberately, evidence-backed deferred.
