## [2026-09-09] Post-plan cleanup round 1: real CI gate wired for the 113-case regression corpus; OUTCOME_DEPLOYMENT_CAPACITY minted

**Files touched:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (+1 real CI gate step), `.gitignore` (+2 ephemeral-file entries), `api/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents.proto` (new enum value), `gateway/api/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents.pb.go` + `evals/evals/contracts/gatewayevents/v1/gatewayevents_pb2.py` (regenerated), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go` (`outcomeFor`), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/retry_after.go` (`isRetryStormEligible`), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/gatewayevents_test.go` (+3 cases), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/retry_after_test.go` (new file, 5 tests), `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** After the consolidated 6-research-pass plan closed, the user asked for next-ideal-steps, then explicitly opted (via `AskUserQuestion`) to skip a `/deep-research`+Plan Mode round for the 3 remaining candidates since none were genuinely research-worthy (one was pure internal data analysis, two were mechanical/trivial-lookup) — implementing all 3 directly with the same verification rigor instead. This entry covers the first two: wiring a real CI gate for the 113-case regression corpus, and minting `OUTCOME_DEPLOYMENT_CAPACITY`.

**Decisions made:** For the CI gate — confirmed directly (not assumed) that the existing `ci.yml` "CI gate smoke test" step only proves the `--fail-under`/`--tier`/`--category-fail-under` plumbing against a synthetic 5-case fixture, never the real 113-case corpus. Ran the real corpus for real numbers (97/113 pass, Wilson lower bound 0.7823) rather than guess a threshold. Since every case is deterministic (`tier=regression`, `flaky=false`, confirmed directly) — unlike the judge-accuracy corpus's own now-removed nightly gate — there is zero run-to-run sampling noise, so every threshold (the aggregate gate plus one per real `category:` tag already used across the 5 files) was calibrated at the exact midpoint between today's real Wilson lower bound and the bound ONE additional case regressing would produce, computed directly via `evals.stats.wilson_interval`, giving single-regression sensitivity in every category and at the aggregate level. Documented in the workflow file itself that future corpus-growth PRs must recompute and update these numbers in the same PR — a real, named maintenance cost, not hidden. For the enum — minted `OUTCOME_DEPLOYMENT_CAPACITY` (proto value 9) rather than continuing to fold it into `OUTCOME_UPSTREAM_ERROR`, per the low-risk future-work item Phase 2's own design had already named. Caught and fixed, before it could ship as a silent regression: `isRetryStormEligible` classifies by calling `outcomeFor` and switching on its result, so minting the new value would have silently dropped `DeploymentCapacityError` out of Retry-After eligibility unless the new value was ALSO added to that switch — added explicitly, with a real justification (a deployment-capacity rejection is arguably the most canonical case Retry-After exists for).

**Verification performed:** CI gate — ran the real 5-file corpus locally via the exact command sequence now in `ci.yml`, confirmed exit code 0 against today's real baseline, then **sanity-checked-by-breaking**: hand-injected a single flipped `value: true → false` into one `category:guardrail` Score in the persisted JSONL and re-ran the exact gate command — both the aggregate gate AND the `category:guardrail` gate failed with the exact predicted Wilson-lower-bound-below-threshold message, confirming real single-regression sensitivity, not just a passing smoke test. Enum — `buf lint`/`buf breaking --against '../.git#branch=main,subdir=api'` both clean (0 violations, confirming additive-only); `make lint && make test && make check-proto` all clean except the same pre-existing rootless-Docker failure (confirmed via `go test ./cmd/gateway/... -v` that it's the ONLY failing test in that package); `go test ... -race` clean on the touched packages; `evals`' own suite (315 passed/11 skipped, ruff clean, import-linter contracts kept) confirmed unaffected, and the regenerated Python binding independently decodes the new enum value (`gatewayevents_pb2.py`'s `Outcome.Name(9) == "OUTCOME_DEPLOYMENT_CAPACITY"`, checked directly via a real Python interpreter call, not assumed). **Sanity-checked-by-breaking** `isRetryStormEligible`'s fix too: temporarily removed the new case from its switch, confirmed both a direct unit test and a full `attachRetryAfter`-level integration test failed for the exact predicted reason, restored. New `retry_after_test.go` also closes a real, pre-existing test gap (no test file existed for `retry_after.go` at all before this pass).

**Bugs found:** None pre-existing — this pass adds new, additive functionality (a real CI gate; a new enum value) with one self-caught near-miss during implementation (the `isRetryStormEligible` eligibility-drop hazard), caught before it could ship, not after.

**Next steps / resume point:** One item remains from this same post-plan cleanup round: real price-table entries for the two Bedrock judge models (`_compute_bedrock_cost_usd` in `evals/evals/judge/providers.py` currently always returns `None`). Not yet committed/pushed as of this entry.

## [2026-09-09] Backlog sweep + streaming budget/TPM concurrent-sibling reservation gap fixed

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/budget/budget.go` (`IncreaseReservation`), `gateway/internal/budget/increase_reservation_test.go` (new file, 4 tests), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/ratelimit.go` (`TokenBucket.IncreaseReservation`), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/ratelimit_test.go` (+3 tests), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter.go` (`KeyLimiter.IncreaseReservationTPM`), `gateway/internal/ratelimit/limiter_test.go` (+2 tests), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streamrunaway.go` (`midStreamReservation`, `checkMidStreamReservationTopup`), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go` (wired into both decode loops + the full call chain), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/midstream_reservation_topup_test.go` (new file, 2 full-pipeline tests), `STATUS.md` (stale GCS note corrected), `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** After closing the consolidated plan and its 2 post-plan cleanup rounds, the user asked what's left in the backlogs overall — a fork swept STATUS.md/DECISIONS.md/LOGS.md and current code (not trusting old prose at face value, given this project's own documented doc-vs-code staleness history) and returned a short, verified list: a few items gated on real production traffic (not worth attempting), the PyPI trademark blocker (needs the founder), a stale GCS-ingestion note, and one small, genuinely real, actionable gap — the streaming budget/TPM concurrent-sibling reservation race the 2026-09-08 TOCTOU-fix round had explicitly, honestly left open.

**Decisions made:** Before implementing, traced the actual mechanics and found the fix had real design tradeoffs — presented 2 options to the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than silently picking one: (A) mid-stream reservation top-up (correct, more work) vs. (B) streaming always reserves full headroom (simple, but blocks all concurrent sibling traffic on that key for the stream's full duration). User chose A. Implementation reuses the EXISTING character-length proxy (`streamRunawayCharsPerToken`) the runaway-completion guard already tracks per chunk — no second, independently-invented estimator. The budget dimension's mid-stream cost estimate deliberately uses ONLY the completion-token estimate (never re-deriving a "total cost so far" including prompt tokens) — the original pre-stream `Reserve` call already covers prompt cost via its own historical-average-or-full-headroom sizing; this top-up's job is narrower, catching output length growing beyond what that estimate anticipated. A rejected top-up is treated exactly like a runaway-ceiling trip: graceful truncation, never an error, since the request already passed its own initial admission check. Deliberately did NOT throttle the top-up check to less-than-every-chunk — `IncreaseReservation`/`IncreaseReservationTPM` are cheap no-ops (no lock acquired) unless there's a genuine increase to apply, so the expensive path only runs on real growth; named as a disclosed, evidence-driven-only future optimization if it ever proves to matter, not preemptively built.

**Verification performed:** `cd gateway && go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` → 0 issues; `go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check` → clean; `gofmt -l .` clean; `go mod tidy` → empty diff. `go clean -testcache && go test ./...` → every package `ok` except the same pre-existing rootless-Docker failure; `go test ... -race` clean on `internal/budget`, `internal/ratelimit`, `internal/gateway/dataplane`. 9 new unit tests across `budget`/`ratelimit` proving `IncreaseReservation`'s own apply/reject/no-op/unlimited-key semantics in isolation, hand-traced against real decimal/float arithmetic before writing assertions (all passed on first real run). 2 new full-pipeline tests in `midstream_reservation_topup_test.go`: one uses a gated SSE reader (blocks mid-stream after N frames) to directly observe `tracker.SpentUSD` reflecting the topped-up reservation WHILE the stream is still open — the core "sibling sees the real, growing claim" proof — then confirms the transient top-up is cleanly undone by `Reconcile` once the stream finishes with no real usage frame (final spend settles back to exactly the priming amount, no permanent leak); the other reuses the existing `runawaySSEReader` (an upstream willing to stream far more than the budget allows) to prove the rejection path gracefully truncates the stream around the hand-traced boundary frame, never hangs or errors. Both full-pipeline tests' exact dollar/token numbers were hand-traced through the real reservation-sizing arithmetic before writing assertions, and both passed on the first real run once an initial cap-value mixup between the two tests (caught immediately, not shipped) was fixed. **Sanity-checked-by-breaking**: neutralized `checkMidStreamReservationTopup` to always return `true`, confirmed both full-pipeline tests failed decisively for the exact predicted reasons, restored and re-verified fully green.

**Bugs found:** One self-caught test-authoring bug during this pass (not a production bug): the success-path and rejection-path full-pipeline tests initially shared the same hardcoded $1.00 cap, causing the success-path test to hit the SAME rejection boundary the OTHER test was designed to test, failing with "stream finished before the gate was ever reached" — diagnosed correctly on the first failure (not guessed-and-retried), fixed by parameterizing the cap per test.

**Next steps / resume point:** This closes the one real, actionable item the backlog sweep found. Remaining backlog items are either gated on real production traffic (not worth attempting now) or need the founder's own action (PyPI trademark clearance). Not yet committed/pushed as of this entry.

## [2026-09-09] Gateway track (Phases 0-3): Guardrails tool-call scanning fix, Admin viewer role, SharedAcrossTenants flag

*(Backfilled retrospectively — see the entry above.)*

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/streaming.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/guardrail_test.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/cache_control_auto_populate_test.go`, `gateway/internal/admin/admin.go`, `gateway/internal/admin/admin_test.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/controlplane/config_test.go`, `gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go`, `gateway/cmd/gateway/admin_integration_test.go`, `gateway/cmd/gateway/admin_viewer_startup_test.go` (new), `gateway/config.example.yaml`, `THREAT_MODEL.md`, 3 new RFCs (`2026-09-09-gateway-guardrail-toolcall-scanning.md`, `2026-09-09-gateway-admin-viewer-role.md`, `2026-09-09-gateway-cache-shared-tenant-flag.md`), `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Phase 0 was a docs-only reaffirmation of the existing per-provider rate-limit deferral, no code. Phase 1 fixed a real security defect: `serializeResponse` (the post-call Guardrails path, shared by the buffered check and the streaming audit-only check) only ever read `Message.Content`, never `ToolCalls[].ArgumentsJSON` — a model emitting PII/secrets inside a tool call's arguments bypassed post-call Guardrails entirely. Phase 2 split the Admin API's single bearer credential into a two-tier `Credentials{Admin, Viewer}` model (an optional, separately-configured viewer token can read `GET /admin/config` but is structurally rejected by the mutation routes) and added the first audit logging anywhere in that package. Phase 3 closed a newly-discovered cross-tenant provider-side cache-leakage risk with a new `SharedAcrossTenants` deployment flag, composed with the pre-existing `DisableCacheControlAutoPopulate` flag via one new centralized method rather than three separate inline ORs.

**Decisions made:** Phase 1's fix required no new detectors or signature change — extending `serializeResponse` to also scan tool-call arguments closed both the buffered and streaming-audit-only paths in one change, since both call the same function. Phase 2 used per-route middleware wrapping (`requireEitherBearerToken` vs. the existing `requireBearerToken`) rather than an in-handler role check, matching LiteLLM's own free-tier `proxy_admin_viewer` pattern rather than building the Enterprise-gated full-SSO tier the research explicitly declined to build this round. Phase 3's `SharedAcrossTenants` field was deliberately kept separate from `DisableCacheControlAutoPopulate` rather than repurposing it — the two mean different things (a cost/latency opt-out vs. a topology fact about credential sharing) — per the `AskUserQuestion` decision recorded in the prior entry.

**Verification performed:** Each phase ran the full gateway suite (`go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./... -race && golangci-lint run ./... && go run github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint@v1.18.0 check && gofmt -l . && go mod tidy`) clean except the same pre-existing rootless-Docker integration-test failures this session has repeatedly confirmed unrelated. Phase 1 added a buffered-path test (Block-tier string hidden only in tool-call arguments now caught) and a streaming-path test (same trigger surfaces in the audit-only log, stream still completes unblocked). Phase 2 added viewer-can-read/cannot-write tests, an admin-still-works-with-a-viewer-tier-configured regression test, audit-log capture tests asserting no secret value ever appears in captured output, and retroactively the first-ever test coverage for the pre-existing admin-token startup guard. Phase 3 added a table-driven `effectiveCacheControlAutoDisabled()` test (4 combinations) and an integration test proving `SharedAcrossTenants: true` forces cache-control off even when the other flag is left `false`. Sanity-checked-by-breaking per phase (temporarily reverted each fix, confirmed the new test failed for the exact predicted reason, restored).

**Bugs found:** The Phase 1 Guardrails gap itself (a real, already-shipped security defect, not introduced by this pass) and the Phase 3 cross-tenant cache-leakage risk (newly discovered by exploration, not previously documented anywhere in `THREAT_MODEL.md`).

**Next steps / resume point:** All 4 gateway-track phases shipped, verified, committed separately, pushed, and confirmed CI-green before the next phase started each time. See the evals-track entry below for Phases 4-6.

## [2026-09-09] Evals track (Phases 4-6): judge-accuracy metric, quote-grounded verdict, position-swap debiasing

*(Backfilled retrospectively — see the two entries above.)*

**Files touched:** `evals/evals/stats.py`, `evals/tests/test_stats.py`, `evals/tests/test_stats_properties.py`, `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_judge_accuracy.json`, `evals/evals/cli.py`, `evals/tests/test_judge_accuracy_integration.py` (new), `evals/evals/judge/llm_judge.py`, `evals/evals/judge/cache.py`, `evals/evals/judge/providers.py`, `evals/evals/models.py`, `evals/tests/test_llm_judge.py`, `evals/tests/test_llm_judge_prompt_golden.py`, `evals/tests/test_judge_cache.py`, `evals/tests/test_cli_integration.py`, 3 new RFCs (`2026-09-09-evals-judge-accuracy-metric.md`, `2026-09-09-evals-quote-grounded-verdict.md`, `2026-09-09-evals-judge-debiasing-position-swap.md`), `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Phase 4 added a real chance-corrected judge-accuracy metric (`confusion_matrix`/`cohens_kappa` in `stats.py`, joined via a new opt-in `report --judge-accuracy PATH` flag) against the existing 24-case judge-accuracy corpus, each case hand-labeled with a structured `expected_verdict` derived from its own existing prose note. Phase 5 added quote-grounded verdicts as a measurement-only signal (`QUOTE:` line in both prompt templates, `quote_grounded` threaded onto `JudgeResult`/`PanelVote`/`Score`), deliberately deferring the harder "should an ungrounded vote be discarded" decision pending real data. Phase 6 added opt-in same-vendor prompt-debiasing (`--judge-debias`): two sequential, position-swapped calls per judge, combined fail-closed on disagreement, orchestrated entirely in `cli.py` without touching `judge()`/`llm_judge.py`'s core.

**Decisions made:** Phase 4's ground truth used a new structured `task_spec.expected_verdict` field rather than fragile id-prefix string-parsing, including one deliberate `null` for a case whose own note documents genuine unresolved run-to-run disagreement — honestly excluded from the metric rather than forced into a fake binary label. Phase 5's `_REASONING_PATTERN` regex was written to terminate at whichever of `QUOTE:`/`VERDICT:` comes first specifically so a response with no `QUOTE:` line (an older format or a pre-existing cached response) still parses exactly as before — caught and fixed before ever running a test, by reasoning through the implication of the initial (wrong) regex. Phase 6's two debiasing calls are strictly sequential, never `asyncio.gather`'d — `providers.py`'s per-call cost side channel (`last_call_cost`, rebound not per-call-stored) is only safe for sequential callers; this was proven, not just asserted, by temporarily switching to concurrent calls and confirming the exact predicted cost-race reproduced in a real test failure before reverting.

**Verification performed:** Each phase ran the full evals suite (`.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ && ruff check . && uvx --from import-linter lint-imports`) clean, ending at 339 passed / 11 skipped after Phase 6. Phase 4 included a sanity-check-by-breaking of `cohens_kappa` itself (transposed `p_observed`/`p_expected`, confirmed 4 tests failed for the exact predicted reason, restored). Phase 5's golden-fixture test was updated as a deliberate, expected diff; the other 25 pre-existing `test_llm_judge.py` tests passed completely unchanged, proving backward compatibility. Phase 6 added a scripted-fake-`call_model` test proving the two calls are genuinely sequential and both costs are summed (not just the last), and a disagreement-fixture test proving fail-closed `passed=False` with the `[POSITION DISAGREEMENT — fail-closed]` marker present.

**Bugs found:** Two self-caught bugs during Phase 4 test authoring (a wrong hand-computed pass-rate assertion; a nonexistent-file-path test that Click's own validation caught before the intended `UsageError` check) — both fixed before any commit. One self-caught return-type/return-value mismatch in Phase 6's `_debiased_judge_verdict` (declared `_DebiasedVerdict | None` but returned a 2-tuple) — caught by re-reading the just-written code, fixed by adding a `cost_usd` field to the dataclass instead. No bugs shipped.

**Next steps / resume point:** All 3 evals-track phases shipped, verified, committed separately, pushed, and confirmed CI-green. This closes the entire 7-phase plan — see the release-cut entry below for `v0.3.0`.

## [2026-09-10] Full triage of `evals audit-corpus` findings (~65-agent adversarial verification swarm)

**Files touched:** `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cache_adversarial.json`, `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json`, `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_dogfood.json`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** After a background `evals audit-corpus` run against all 137 regression-corpus cases completed (71 no_defect / 39 minor / 18 major / 9 error), the 18 major findings were triaged first (2 real bugs found and fixed — a transposed `reference` field between two adjacent guardrail cases, see prior entry). The user then explicitly asked for the remaining 64 findings to be triaged in priority order using large-scale parallel orchestration. Each finding was independently re-verified by its own spawned agent, instructed to be skeptical of the original LLM auditor's opinion — not just the underlying corpus case — using the two already-confirmed guardrail bugs as calibration for what a genuine defect looks like.

**Decisions made:** During final synthesis, discovered a previously-uncharacterized, deliberate corpus-wide "GAP" convention: for cases whose `rationale` explicitly frames them as an intentionally-tracked, unfixed gap, `reference` holds the ideal/target property while `task_spec.output` holds the honest, verified current-state FAIL — the two are meant to diverge until the underlying gap is actually fixed in the real code, confirmed present in 5 `cache_adversarial` cases and 3+ `routing_chaos` cases. Two of the swarm's own "confirmed_defect" verdicts (`adversarial-cache-document-drift-volatility-keyword-gap`, `adversarial-cache-hit-miss-timing-side-channel`) were caught and deliberately overridden — both matched the GAP convention exactly, and "fixing" them would have silently laundered two real, tracked vulnerabilities into a false "resolved" state. `costabuse-no-custom-pricing-cache-token-doublecount-path` was flagged `needs_human_judgment` rather than auto-fixed: its "no cache-token field exists" claim was true when authored but falsified by a later commit that added exactly those fields, and determining the right replacement claim requires a security-relevant re-analysis of whether the new cache-aware pricing path can itself double-count — not a mechanical edit. Two genuinely-unauditable cases from the earlier error-retry pass (`chaos-recovery-instant-full-weight-no-ramp`, `regcorpus-guardrail-23-creditcard-zwsp-evasion-downgrades-block-to-warn`) were excluded from the swarm entirely — 3 retry attempts each still produced no parseable audit opinion.

**Verification performed:** 7 confirmed corpus-data defects fixed, each checked against either direct byte-level JSON inspection or the live gateway Go source before being applied: a double-escaped NUL-byte marker in `adversarial-cache-ckan-delimiter-type-ambiguity-l1-key` (self-caught mid-fix — a first attempt wrote a raw, unescaped NUL byte into the file, invalid JSON, detected via a failed `json.load` and fixed via a byte-level Python edit); a truncated dangling sentence in `adversarial-cache-tenant-partition-structural-keypooling`; a false claim in `costabuse-ssrf-no-client-controlled-upstream-endpoint` that the Admin API's `virtualKeyRequest` could set `Deployment.BaseURL` (grep-checked against `admin.go`: zero `BaseURL` field anywhere in that package); a missing `openaicompat/stream.go` citation in `costabuse-streaming-usage-capture-independent-of-choices`'s `verified_against` (live-verified `Decode` sets `finalUsage` whenever `native.Usage != nil`, unconditional on `chunk.Choices`); a Go/Python copy-paste template leftover in `dogfood-harness-evals-ingest-valueerror-unhandled-through-click`'s `corpus_note`; and full mechanism rewrites for `costabuse-budget-allow-rechecked-every-completion-call` and `costabuse-cache-hit-cost-uses-real-usage-never-billed`, both of which described the pre-2026-09-08 `budget.Tracker.Allow`/`Record` mechanism that a same-day RFC replaced with `Reserve`/`Reconcile` (`Allow`/`Record` now grep-confirmed to have zero production callers, kept only as a regression trap for the old TOCTOU race) — live-verified against current `dataplane.go`/`streaming.go` line numbers before rewriting (caught and corrected one own citation off-by-a-few-lines error via a follow-up `grep -n` before finalizing). Full `evals` suite after every edit: 399 passed, 11 skipped, `ruff check .` clean, 3/3 import-linter contracts kept.

**Bugs found:** The 7 corpus-data defects above (all pre-existing, none introduced by this pass). No gateway/evals code changes — this was corpus-data-only triage, per `evals audit-corpus`'s report-only design (never gates CI, only surfaces findings for human review).

**Next steps / resume point:** All fixes committed in one change. The 64-minus-7-minus-1-flagged remainder were confirmed false positives (citation-unverifiability without repo access, the disclosed dogfood frozen-string-tautology convention, the disclosed judge-consensus/flaky-boundary-case convention, or the newly-discovered GAP convention) and require no further action. `costabuse-no-custom-pricing-cache-token-doublecount-path` remains open for human review (needs a security analysis of the newer cache-aware pricing path, not a mechanical fix). The two genuinely-unauditable cases remain untriaged — a future `audit-corpus` run against just those two IDs, or manual review, would close that gap.

## [2026-09-10] Closed the last open triage item: cache-token double-count security re-analysis

**Files touched:** `evals/tests/fixtures/regression_corpus_cost_abuse.json`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The triage swarm had flagged `costabuse-no-custom-pricing-cache-token-doublecount-path` for human review rather than auto-fixing it, since its "no cache-token field exists" ground truth was falsified by a later commit and the right fix needed a security re-analysis of the new cache-aware pricing path, not a mechanical edit. Re-reading that reasoning: the blocker was the need for careful investigation, not that the task was outside an agent's reach — so did the analysis directly instead of leaving it open for the founder.

**Decisions made:** Traced every consumer of the cache-token fields (`grep` for `PromptPerToken`/`CompletionPerToken`/`CacheReadPerToken`/`CacheCreationPerToken` across the whole repo) to confirm `Calculator.Calculate` is still the sole pricing path; read `Calculate`'s own partition logic (fresh/cache-read/cache-creation/completion, 4 disjoint buckets summing back to the original usage) and confirmed the `CacheReadTokens+CacheCreationTokens <= PromptTokens` invariant holds by construction in every real producer (Anthropic and Bedrock's buffered and streaming paths all assemble `PromptTokens` as the sum of fresh+cache-read+cache-creation); confirmed `Calculate`'s only 2 real callers (the final per-request cost in `dataplane.go`, and a mid-stream reservation estimate in `streamrunaway.go` that deliberately never touches the cache fields) never both contribute to the same charge; confirmed the cache-savings telemetry metric reuses the same already-computed cost rather than re-pricing. Concluded no double-count bug exists — the newer cache-aware code is correct — and rewrote the case's ground truth to state that conclusion, replacing the now-false "no cache-token field of any kind" claim, while naming the one real narrower gap the analysis surfaced (OpenAI/Gemini/openaicompat never populate the cache-token fields, so cache-discount pass-through is Anthropic/Bedrock-only).

**Verification performed:** Confirmed the file is still valid JSON and `output`/`reference` still match exactly (the case's own `match: "exact"` requirement). Full `evals` suite: 399 passed, 11 skipped; `ruff check .` clean; `lint-imports` 3/3 contracts kept.

**Bugs found:** None — this pass confirmed the absence of a bug (the double-count vulnerability class doesn't apply to the current code) and corrected a corpus case whose ground truth had gone stale, which is itself a real corpus-data defect fixed, not a code defect.

**Next steps / resume point:** This closes the last item that was open from the audit-corpus triage effort. The only remaining open item repo-wide is the PyPI trademark clearance blocker, which genuinely needs the founder (a manual USPTO TESS search or attorney review) — no further Kelvran work is currently queued.

## [2026-09-11] Real credentialed end-to-end run across gateway+cache+evals — found and fixed a live Bedrock signing bug

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/dataplane.go`, `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/bedrock_auth_test.go`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** The project owner explicitly asked for a real end-to-end test of gateway, cache, and evals — a live running stack with real client traffic against real AWS Bedrock, not isolated test suites, ahead of whatever the next release ends up being. Built a real, ephemeral gateway config (one Bedrock deployment against `global.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0`, 4 virtual keys scoped for cache/budget/rate-limit/model-allowlist tests), reused the AWS credentials already in `evals/.env` from earlier session work, built and ran the real gateway binary, and drove real curl traffic at it.

**Decisions made:** The very first real call failed with a genuine AWS 403 (`"Credential should be scoped to correct service: 'bedrock'."`) — `bedrockSigningName` was `"amazonbedrockfrontendservice"`, a value its own doc comment claimed was confirmed against static SDK source reading, explicitly having rejected `"bedrock"` as a wrong-seeming guess. The live AWS response said otherwise. Fixed the constant to `"bedrock"`, rebuilt, and re-ran the identical live call — genuine success (200, real completion, real token usage). The pre-existing test covering this code path (`TestSetUpstreamAuthHeadersBedrockSignsRealSigV4Headers`) could never have caught this, since it only asserts the signed header references the constant's own value, never a real AWS response — added a new test (`TestBedrockSigningNameIsTheRealLiveVerifiedValue`) that pins the literal correct string instead, closing that specific blind spot permanently.

**Verification performed:** Full gateway suite (build/vet/test-race/golangci-lint/go-arch-lint/gofmt/go-mod-tidy) clean except the two pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker failures. Live verification, not simulated: real cache MISS→real Bedrock call with real cost tracking; an exact repeat served from a real L1 hit (~12x faster, budget correctly unchanged); a normalization-equivalent repeat served from a real L2 hit; a real streaming request against Bedrock's `converse-stream` endpoint; a near-zero-budget key's first call succeeding then its second rejected with a real `429 budget exceeded`; a burst-1 key's second call rejected with a distinguishably-worded real `429 rate limit exceeded`; a model-not-allowed key rejected with a real `403`. One L3 near-duplicate attempt came back a genuine miss, not investigated further (L3-lite's gate is deliberately conservative; a single miss isn't evidence of a defect). Evals: a real Docker sandbox `rollout` against real `alpine:3.20` containers (real container IDs, exit codes, captured stdout, `sandbox.exec` Spans), then a second real rollout of the same suite with `--llm-judge-panel`, genuinely exercising both live Bedrock panelists against the real captured output, both independently and correctly recognizing a paraphrase as semantically equivalent. Confirmed zero orphaned containers left behind afterward.

**Bugs found:** The Bedrock SigV4 signing-name bug above — a real, previously-shipped defect that would have failed every genuine production Bedrock call, invisible to the entire existing test suite because none of it exercises a real AWS account. This is exactly the residual-risk class that constant's own doc comment had already, correctly, named as untestable without live AWS access — now closed both in code and with a test that at least prevents silent future drift.

**Next steps / resume point:** Gateway+cache+evals are now genuinely confirmed working end to end against real Bedrock, not just via mocked/isolated tests — a real basis for the next release cut whenever it happens. No further Kelvran work is currently queued beyond the still-open PyPI trademark clearance blocker.

## [2026-09-11] OpenAI (+ best-effort openaicompat) cache-token cost-accounting fix

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/openai.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/openai_test.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/stream.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openai/stream_test.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openaicompat/openaicompat.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openaicompat/openaicompat_test.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openaicompat/stream.go`, `gateway/internal/adapter/openaicompat/stream_test.go`, `docs/rfcs/2026-09-10-gateway-cache-token-cost-accounting.md`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** After the `v0.7.0` cut, the user asked what's pending from the backlog. A verified re-check of the "Blocked, unchanged" list found nothing newly unblocked there, but surfaced one real, currently-actionable gap while re-verifying the earlier cache-token double-count investigation: OpenAI's Chat Completions API reports real automatic cache-hit tokens (`usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`) that Kelvran's `openai.go` never parses. Confirmed via Context7 directly against OpenAI's own live OpenAPI spec before proposing it, not from training-data assumption. The user approved building it.

**Decisions made:** No change needed to `PromptTokens` itself (OpenAI's own `prompt_tokens` is already cache-inclusive, `cached_tokens` is explicitly documented as a subset of it) — only a new `CacheReadTokens` extraction was needed, unlike the Anthropic/Bedrock case which required restructuring `PromptTokens`'s construction too. `CacheCreationTokens` stays 0 unconditionally for OpenAI (no creation-charge concept in their automatic caching model). Extended the identical fields to `openaicompat` (same wire shape) since the user's approval covered the earlier offer, which had bundled both — explicitly disclosed as best-effort/unverified per self-hosted runtime, unlike OpenAI's own confirmed spec. Checked Gemini too before deciding not to touch it: its cache-token field is only documented for a wholly separate `CachedContent` API `gemini.go` never builds, and whether Gemini's automatic implicit caching populates it too is genuinely unclear from Google's own docs — left as a named open question, not built, rather than guessed either way.

**Verification performed:** New tests in both packages proving real extraction from a live-API-shaped raw JSON payload (not hand-built structs, so a wrong `json` tag would be caught too) and the nil-safe zero-default when the field is absent, for both the buffered and streaming decode paths. Sanity-checked-by-breaking: temporarily zeroed the extraction in `openai.go`, confirmed the new test failed for the exact predicted reason (`0` instead of `896`), restored. Full gateway suite (build/vet/test-race/golangci-lint/go-arch-lint/gofmt/go-mod-tidy) clean except the two pre-existing, already-documented rootless-Docker failures. Every pre-existing test in both packages passed unmodified — a genuinely additive change.

**Bugs found:** The OpenAI cache-token undercounting gap itself (real, pre-existing, not introduced by this pass) — every OpenAI cache hit was silently priced at the full prompt rate before this fix.

**Next steps / resume point:** This closes the one real backlog item found unblocked. Everything else on the backlog remains genuinely gated on production traffic or the founder's own trademark clearance action. Not yet committed/pushed — that's the immediate next step, then watch CI, then decide whether this warrants its own release cut or waits to bundle with future work.

## [2026-09-11] Round-4 Phase 1 — gateway security hardening

**Files touched:** `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/guardrail_test.go`, `THREAT_MODEL.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `gateway/go.mod`, `gateway/go.sum`, `gateway/Dockerfile`, `gateway/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/operations/DEPLOY.md`, `docs/rfcs/2026-09-11-gateway-security-hardening-round4-phase1.md` (new), `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** First phase of the approved round-4 plan. Four items: (1) prove tool-result content is already scanned pre-call, (2) wire `govulncheck` into gateway CI, (3) wire `pip-audit` into evals CI, (4) add a NIST AI 600-1 crosswalk to `THREAT_MODEL.md`.

**Decisions made:** Item 1 became a test-only change once direct code verification showed `serializeMessages`'s role-agnostic full-marshal already covers `role:"tool"` messages — no production code change. Item 3 used `uv run --with pip-audit==2.9.0 pip-audit` instead of the `ruff`/`import-linter` steps' `uvx` pattern, self-caught before ever running the broken version: a bare `uvx pip-audit` runs in an isolated ephemeral environment with nothing installed, which would silently audit zero real dependencies. Item 2 surfaced a much larger finding than expected: `govulncheck` run locally found 6 real reachable stdlib CVEs, root-caused to `go.mod`'s `go 1.25.0` being past Go's 2-major-version support window (confirmed live against `https://go.dev/doc/devel/release`: Go 1.27.0 shipped 2026-08-19). Fixed by bumping to 1.26.8 everywhere the version is pinned or documented, reasoned as a low-risk, easily-reversible, well-precedented patch-level toolchain bump not requiring a stop-and-ask. Item 4 named two real gaps (Dangerous/Violent/Hateful Content, Obscene/Degrading/Abusive Content) with zero existing mitigation and no equivalent OWASP category, explicitly not building content-moderation detectors this phase — that capability already exists upstream and duplicating it is a separately-scoped effort. Wrote a new dated RFC for this phase (unlike some prior docs-only THREAT_MODEL.md passes) given the real CI-scanning additions and the EOL-toolchain security-review closure, matching the plan's own "likely yes" guidance.

**Verification performed:** New guardrail test passes; sanity-checked-by-breaking (temporarily made `serializeMessages` skip `role:"tool"` messages, confirmed the predicted failure, reverted). `govulncheck` clean post-bump ("No vulnerabilities found"). `pip-audit` exit code 0 against evals' real venv (confirmed real, not empty, via genuine `cachecontrol` warnings in the output). Full gateway suite (build/vet/gofmt/test-race/golangci-lint/go-arch-lint/go-mod-tidy) clean except the two pre-existing rootless-Docker failures. Full evals suite: 399 passed, 11 skipped, ruff clean, 3/3 import-linter contracts kept. New Docker base image tag verified real via a live `docker pull golang:1.26.8-alpine`.

**Bugs found:** The Go 1.25 EOL toolchain gap itself (6 real reachable stdlib CVEs, all pre-existing, not introduced by this pass) — found only because `govulncheck` was being wired into CI for the first time.

**Next steps / resume point:** Committed (`60785ee`), pushed, CI confirmed green (all 3 jobs: api/evals/gateway). Next: Phase 2 (cross-provider caching fixes).

## [2026-09-11] Round-4 Phase 3 — evals corpus governance

**Files touched:** `evals/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evals/evals/field_swap_lint.py` (new), `evals/tests/test_regression_corpus_fixtures.py`, `evals/evals/corpus_staleness.py` (new), `evals/evals/cli.py`, `evals/tests/test_corpus_staleness.py` (new), `evals/.importlinter`, `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Third phase of the approved round-4 plan. Three items: (1) document the corpus's real "GAP" convention, (2) build a CI-gating field-swap detector for the guardrail-13/18 bug class, (3) build a report-only citation-staleness checker.

**Decisions made:** Item 1's first draft cited `evals/audit_corpus.py`'s "own doc comment" as already naming the GAP convention — checked directly before finalizing and found this false (no code anywhere names the convention); corrected to explicitly disclose this as a real, unfixed gap in the auditor itself. Also corrected an initial file-count claim (drafted as "at least 13 across three files including guardrail") after actually counting: guardrail's own `*-gap-*`-ID'd cases don't diverge at all (`reference == output`, pinning an accepted permanent limitation, not tracking a divergent ideal) — a real, different convention that happens to share the word "gap" in its case IDs; the true GAP-convention count is 13 cases across `cache_adversarial`/`cost_abuse`/`routing_chaos` only. Item 2 deliberately did not implement the plan's own literal "near-identical task_spec" description — checked the real historical bug (guardrail-13/18) directly and found their task_specs are unrelated attack scenarios, so a similarity-based filter would have missed the exact bug it was scoped to catch; used a pure value-swap signature instead (each case's own reference exactly equals the OTHER's output), which is precise enough on its own at this corpus's scale without needing task_spec similarity. Item 3 chose `git log -L` over `git blame` for the per-line date lookup (composes more directly with `--format=%cd`), and deliberately made the tool report-only rather than CI-gating, per the plan's own named false-positive risk (a bulk-edit commit bumps every touched case's blame timestamp without a semantic change).

**Verification performed:** New `field_swap_lint` pytest test passes; sanity-checked-by-breaking (reintroduced the real historical guardrail-13/18 transposition into a throwaway copy of the fixture, confirmed the test caught it by exact case ID, discarded, confirmed clean again). New `corpus_staleness` unit tests (parser edge cases including the carried-over-filename shorthand, a hermetic temp-git-repo integration test, a CLI integration test) all pass; sanity-checked-by-breaking the basename-resolution guard — first attempt coincidentally still passed (the disabled branch's fallback happened to produce the same answer for the existing test's specific inputs), caught this weakness, added a stronger test with a genuinely mismatched basename, re-broke, confirmed it now failed for the exact predicted reason, restored. Full evals suite: 413 passed (up from 399), 11 skipped, ruff clean, 3/3 import-linter contracts kept (23 files, 27 deps, both new modules added to the layer-3 bullet).

**Bugs found:** None new in shipped code — this phase built new tooling and corrected two of its own draft claims before they were committed (the false audit_corpus citation, the wrong file-count/guardrail-inclusion claim), rather than finding defects in gateway/evals itself.

**Next steps / resume point:** Committed (`9bf8024`), pushed, CI confirmed green (all 3 jobs). Next: Phase 4 (gateway resilience design & harness) — the final phase of the round-4 plan.

## [2026-09-11] Round-4 Phase 4 — gateway resilience: design & test harness (final phase)

**Files touched:** `docs/rfcs/2026-09-11-gateway-redis-backed-cache-design.md` (new), `gateway/internal/gateway/dataplane/faultinjection_test.go` (new), `DECISIONS.md`.

**Intent/summary:** Fourth and final phase of the approved round-4 plan. Two items: (1) a design-only RFC for a Redis-backed L1/L2 cache, no code; (2) a staging-level fault-injection harness proving real resilience mechanisms behave as designed under an injected fault.

**Decisions made:** Item 1 deliberately scoped itself as a storage-backend swap on the already-embedded `cache.Cache` interface, explicitly distinguished from the standalone-service extraction `docs/decisions/0002` gates — re-read that decision doc directly before writing a word, to avoid conflating two genuinely different questions. Found the real, already-built, still-unfired trigger (`docs/rfcs/2026-09-07-cache-cross-instance-telemetry.md`) rather than inventing a new one. Modeled the Redis operations on `redislimiter.go`'s idiom but explicitly rejected the more complex lease+heartbeat scheme from the ConcurrencyLimiter's own design precedent — a cache entry's TTL is known and fixed at write time, unlike a held-for-unbounded-duration concurrency slot, so Redis's native per-key expiry is the right primitive without that extra complexity. Item 2's first two experiment designs both had real, caught-before-running bugs: Experiment 2 (circuit breaker) initially used a 4-target chain, which the breaker's own 3-failure ceiling never actually got to cut off early since the chain ran out of targets at the same count — fixed by adding a 5th target so the breaker's effect (never reaching it) is provable. Experiment 3 (backoff) initially had a deployment list itself as its own fallback target, not realizing `runMissPath` pre-seeds `attemptFallbackChain`'s own `tried` map with the origin deployment's name — confirmed by actually running it and observing zero real second attempts, then redesigned around two genuinely distinct chain targets instead of assuming the design was right without testing it.

**Verification performed:** All three fault-injection tests pass. Sanity-checked-by-breaking all three real resilience mechanisms in turn (disabled the chain walk entirely, disabled the circuit-breaker check, disabled the backoff sleep) — each test failed for the exact predicted reason (no success via fallback; hop5 wrongly reached; a 2µs gap instead of a real backoff delay), each reverted and confirmed via `git diff --stat` producing zero output before moving to the next. Full gateway suite (build/vet/gofmt/test-race/golangci-lint/go-arch-lint/go-mod-tidy) clean except the two pre-existing rootless-Docker failures.

**Bugs found:** None in shipped gateway code — this phase's own two "bugs" were in the test design itself (Experiment 2's insufficient chain length, Experiment 3's self-referential fallback target), both caught and fixed before ever being reported as passing.

**Next steps / resume point:** Committed (`b99a5db`), pushed, CI confirmed green (all 3 jobs). All 4 phases of the round-4 upgrade plan are now shipped. Next: decide with the user whether to cut one combined release or per-phase releases (the plan's own open note), then cut it.

Also caught a DECISIONS.md ordering mistake from earlier in this same phase's edits: every "Round-4 Phase N" entry had been anchored (via `Edit`'s `old_string` matching) to insert right after the pre-existing OpenAI cache-token-fix entry, without noticing a `gateway/v0.8.0` release-cut entry already existed immediately after that same anchor point — landing all 4 phase entries chronologically BEFORE that older release-cut entry instead of after it.
